[Open Page 138 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=138)

### Monday, January 25

_Better is it to go to the house of mourning than to go to the banquet house, because that is the end of all mankind; and the one alive should take it to his heart._ —Eccl. 7:2.

Do the foregoing words clarify the statement that the day of your death is better than the day of your birth? (Eccl. 7:1) It does if you know the occasion and the background of those words. They concern a custom in ancient Israel. When a household lost a loved one in death, their dwelling became a house of mourning. It was the custom for friends and neighbors to come and offer condolences. The most consoling thought would be that the day of the death of this loved one would be better than the day of his birth—if he had made a good name with God. It is true that in the original Hebrew, verse one of this passage merely says “a name” rather than “a good name.” However, it is to be understood that the name is a good one. A similar case is Proverbs 22:1: “A name is to be chosen rather than abundant riches.” W 2/15 3

### Tuesday, January 26

_Tell Archippus: “Keep watching the ministry which you accepted in the Lord, that you fulfill it.”_ —Col. 4:17.

The _Watch Tower_ magazine in its issue of June 1882 states: “The disciples were sent to preach and teach and baptize. . . . We believe that every consecrated member of the body of Christ is a minister in some sense, and all are ‘anointed to preach the glad tidings.’” Was the _Watch Tower_ magazine being presumptuous in saying that every consecrated

or dedicated, baptized disciple of Christ is a “minister”? Not at all! The English words “minister” and “ministry,” as well as the corresponding words in Italian, Spanish, Portuguese and French, are drawn from ancient Latin words found in the Latin Vulgate Version of the Bible, whose definitions indicate that these words should have a broad meaning. Particularly in quotations from the _King James Version_ the key words “minister” and “ministry” are used in the sense given them in the Christian Greek Scriptures, even as they are in the _New World Translation_. W 3/15 9, 11, 12

### Wednesday, January 27

_Happy are those conscious of their spiritual need._—Matt. 5:3.

Doing things God’s way—in accord with his moral standards—reduces life’s problems and so clears away the roadblocks to our attaining happiness. Yet simply avoiding problems is not in itself a guarantee of our being happy. We need to develop a relationship with the Creator, to learn his will and purposes and accept them ourselves. Jesus acknowledged this fundamental need, saying the above. It is easy, though, to overlook that need. Even one who has long been involved with God might lose appreciation for how much happiness this relationship produces. He might see some who are pursuing hobbies and forms of relaxation. If he follows their course at the expense of time or interest devoted to his relationship with God, his life, in a physical sense, might seem to be more varied, more in line with current trends. But will he be truly happier? In view of Jesus’ words, the answer must be No. W 4/1 7-9

### Thursday, January 28

_There is no authority, save by God, and they that are in being have by God been arranged._ —Rom. 13:1, Rotherham.

Strangely, in this highly intelligent age, many have given way to doubt as to whether God has an organization. Some hesitate, yes, now fear to use the expression “God’s organization,” because they have noted that their translation of the Bible does not use the word “organization” in connection with God or at all. Certainly no reasonable person will argue that the highly organized governments of today, even those of Christendom, are God’s organization or even a part of it. No one could prove it even by appealing to the words of the apostle Paul above. At the same time, particularly since the year 1922, Jehovah’s Witnesses have been efficiently organized for fulfilling the prophecy of Jesus at Matthew 24:14. So where does that put them with relation to God, Jehovah of armies? The facts certainly prove they are indeed the visible earthly part of Jehovah God’s organization. W 5/1 1, 2a

### Friday, January 29

_I take delight, not in the death of the wicked one, but in that someone wicked turns back from his way and actually keeps living. Turn back, turn back from your bad ways, for why is it that you should die, O house of Israel?_—Ezek. 33:11.

Jehovah expressed a most tender love toward Israel. (Deut. 32:11, 12) But in time, foreign gods intruded into Israel’s worship. Thus, the God who had declared that he would pay back vengeance to their adversaries was compelled to exact ven-

gengeance from his own people. But how patient Jehovah was toward Israel! Why, for 900 years he endured their waywardness! During all that time, Jehovah extended his hand of mercy toward them, even as he had declared by Ezekiel. Time and again, God warned his people of the consequences of their idolatry, their immorality and their bloodguiltiness. But, at last, Jehovah was compelled to exact vengeance by having Jerusalem and its temple destroyed. What a comfort to know Jehovah is such a long-suffering God! W 1/15 16, 17

### Saturday, January 30

_I did not hold back from telling you any of the things that were profitable nor from teaching you publicly and from house to house._—Acts 20:20.

Clearly, Paul was here speaking of his efforts to preach to persons needing to repent and put faith in Jesus. (Acts 20:21) He went to the homes of such unbelievers. He had no reason to feel hesitant about such preaching to strangers, for he was doing a holy work that God approved. In modern times we have been active in house-to-house witnessing as a primary way to contact persons and preach God’s truth. Of course, each Christian who feels his obligation and privilege to declare the “good news” to others will use every appropriate occasion to witness—to relatives, schoolmates, fellow workers, neighbors and to strangers while in informal conversation. And wherever possible we systematically visit each home. This results in a thorough witness and in our reaching many persons who might not otherwise meet true Christians and hear the “good news.” W 1/1 10, 11a

### Sunday, January 31

_After these things I saw, and, look! a great crowd, which no man was able to number.—Rev. 7:9._

Yes, today an unnumbered “great crowd” of responsive hearers of the warning looms up before the “watchman” class. The eyes of those of the “great crowd” have been opened to see the sword of Jehovah’s executional officer that is about to slash down the wicked opposers of his Messianic kingdom. In 1935 this “great crowd” began to take definite form and to align itself with the “watch-

man” class. World war and its aftermath did not stop or slow down the thronging of those of the “great crowd” to the side of the “watchman” class amid worldwide persecution. By this they have come under the obligation to join the “watchman” class in blaring out the divine warning fearlessly. They have not shunned to undertake this responsibility. In this way they want not only to express their loving obedience to Jehovah God but also to express their love for their neighbor, their fellowman. Are you enjoying this privilege? _W 2/1 17a_

### Monday, February 1

_The Father is greater than I am.—John 14:28._

What a fine example Jesus set in conducting himself as a lesser one! He gladly served as a “master worker,” with his Father in the works of creation. (Prov. 8:30) While on earth, Jesus continued to conduct himself as a lesser one, saying the above. In fact, when on earth Jesus even conducted himself as a lesser one in relation to fellow humans, taking the role of servant. (Matt. 20:28) He even performed the most menial task of washing his apostles’ feet. And how highly he was exalted for his lowliness of mind! (Phil. 2:9-11) Since his resurrection and ascension to heaven, Christ has continued to conduct himself as a lesser one in relation to Jehovah his Father, being willing to wait patiently until Jehovah places his enemies as a stool for his feet. (Ps. 110:1) Then, after Jesus’ millennial reign, when God has put all his enemies under his feet, Jesus will also subject himself to his Father.—1 Cor. 15:25-28. _W 6/1 17-19_

### Tuesday, February 2

_We shall devote ourselves to prayer and to the ministry of the word.—Acts 6:4._

Jehovah God showed mercy to the believing, baptized Gentiles by admitting them into the spiritual kingdom. (Col. 1:13) The nations, whether circumcised Samaritans, circumcised Egyptians, or uncircumcised non-Jews, were favored with becoming members of the ‘seed of Abraham,’ by means of whom all the families of the earth will have to bless themselves. Although at the present time these are in the spiritual kingdom of the Son of God’s love, all those proving faithful up to their earthly death will be resurrected into the heavenly kingdom of Jehovah God. There they will reign with Christ for 1,000 years, to bless all the families of the earth. In the meantime, while yet in the flesh on earth, they have the “ministry” that comes upon them as “ambassadors substituting for Christ,” engaging in “the ministry of the word” about Jehovah’s kingdom by Christ. Those of the great crowd aid them in this work.—2 Cor. 5:20. _W 6/15 5, 6a_

### Wednesday, February 3

_Do you, however, the one teaching someone else, not teach yourself?—Rom. 2:21._

To make a good name with Jehovah God it is not enough to study and learn answers. We must apply this knowledge to ourselves first. To benefit personally we must take it personally. Like the man in ancient Israel in the house of mourning, we must ‘lay it to heart.’ (Eccl. 7:2, _AV_) Paul declared: “I pummel my body and lead it as a slave, that, after I have preached to others, I myself should not become disapproved somehow.” (1 Cor. 9:27) It is more basic even than what we are doing. It is, What are we? What is “the secret person of the heart”? (1 Pet. 3:4) What we are is not determined by what we appear to be, even as Jehovah reminded Samuel when he sent him to anoint one of Jesse’s sons to be king of Israel. (1 Sam. 16:7) Paul listed great things that he might accomplish, and then added, “But \[if I] do not have love, I am not profited at all.” To make a good name with God we must be motivated by love.—1 Cor. 13:1-3. _W 2/15 12-14a_

### Thursday, February 4

_The senseless one has said in his heart: “There is no Jehovah.”—Ps. 53:1._

Today voices are being raised in solemn warning. But is the whole world really in mortal danger? Observant persons who are by no means calamity howlers answer Yes! Their gloomy answer is based not just on what mankind of itself can do to itself. There is something vastly more serious than that for us to take into account. Is that really possible? Yes. How so? It is

because of the Person to whom this earth and all animal and human life belong. True, very few today care to take him into consideration. Worldly-wise persons of this scientifically advanced century think themselves to be too independent in thought as to believe in a Creator or to ask themselves, What does he have in mind? But the Creator is not so hardhearted as not to care about man’s plight. However, they brush him aside as if he did not exist or as being so far away as not to be involved or even interested. Thereby they show themselves to be senseless ones. _W 2/1 2, 3_

### Friday, February 5

_Unless you turn around and become as young children, you will by no means enter into the kingdom of the heavens.—Matt. 18:3._

Among the most favored men ever to walk this earth, without a doubt, were the 12 disciples of Jesus Christ whom he chose to be apostles. What privileges were theirs to accompany him as he went from city to city declaring the good news! (Luke 8:1) What a pleasure it must have been for them to hear Jesus’ discourses and to witness his performing one miracle after another! Besides, did not Jesus constantly give them private instruction? Yes, indeed. Yet, strangely, these most favored ones were repeatedly arguing among themselves as to who was the chief or foremost among them. Not one wanted to conduct himself as a lesser one. Did such rivalry make for peace, harmony and happiness? Was it pleasing to Jehovah God? Was it the course of wisdom? Hardly, for what did Jesus on one occasion say to them? The above. Here there is a lesson for all of us. _W 6/1 1, 2_

## Saturday, February 6

_The four angels were untied . . . And the number of the armies of cavalry was two myriads of myriads.—Rev. 9:15, 16._

Here the anointed remnant are symbolized by “four angels,” or messengers, released from captivity to “Babylon the Great,” “at the great river Euphrates.” (Verses 14, 15) These liberated messengers have at their disposal “armies of cavalry” numbering 200,000,000, used for, symbolically speaking, “killing” a large fraction of humanity. The “horses” in this vision picture the means used by the anointed remnant to publicize Jehovah’s judgment messages directed particularly against Christendom, the most reprehensible part of “Babylon the Great.” Explaining this fascinating vision, the book “_Then Is Finished the Mystery of God_” states: “The charging of these symbolic ‘horses’ increased greatly when the Watch Tower’s magazines began to be offered on the streets, from house to house, from store to store.” Are you sharing in this activity? _W 3/1 6, 7a_

## Sunday, February 7

_Praise Jehovah, all you nations, and let all the peoples praise him.—Rom. 15:11._

How can all nations and peoples do so unless we tell them about Jehovah and explain why he should be praised? So, international preaching about his kingdom by Christ needs to be done. This is the “ministry” that is assigned to the remnant of Kingdom heirs and the “great crowd” of their companion workers. (Rev. 7:9-17) King David did rule nations after he subdued the non-Jewish nations

that had been left in the Promised Land. However, David has now been dead since 1037 B.C.E., and people of the nations cannot rest their hope in him. Happily they can rest their hope in the presently reigning Greater David, Jesus Christ, “the root and the offspring of David.” (Rev. 22:16) Their resting their hope in him, the antitypical David, will not prove to be in vain, or to be misplaced. Since 1935 hundreds of thousands of those who make up the “great crowd” have joined the remnant in calling on all peoples to praise Jehovah. _W 6/15 15, 17, 18a_

## Monday, February 8

_Whatever you are doing, work at it whole-souled as to Jehovah, and not to men.—Col. 3:23._

In reflecting on our view and pattern as to work, we need not be harshly critical or overly demanding of ourselves. We all have our physical limits as well as limiting circumstances. Also, Jesus’ course shows that we can rightly enjoy rest, relaxation, pleasant association and some comforts. But the very imperfection that stresses our need for rest can move us to carry rest to extremes, as emphasized by the Bible’s warning about laziness. The fact is, just as being hungry makes a meal taste better, so hard work makes rest and relaxation more enjoyable. The word “work” may particularly bring to our minds secular work. Bible counsel shows that we should be conscientious, diligent. That should be true, also, with our duties in the home or even our studies at school, for all aspects of our life can reflect the improvement made in us by applying Bible principles. _W 1/1 7, 8_

## Tuesday, February 9

_Happy is the man . . . \[whose] delight is in the law of Jehovah.—Ps. 1:1, 2._

We can appreciate that those who are adrift, lacking answers, cannot be truly happy. Yet, as we assign due importance to our spiritual need and seek understanding from God’s Word, we see where we fit in. Our life has more direction, more meaning, more likelihood of happiness. In another way, too, we add to our happiness by recognizing our spiritual need and by having a relationship with God. We know that accepting God’s standards helps us to avoid problems, but doing so goes beyond that. We actually need a sound set of values. God’s Word fills that need perfectly. And God’s standards are harmonious with our inherent sense of conscience. So as we comply with them we feel better, more at peace. We can even help our children toward happiness by sharing God’s standards with them. Yes, true happiness is linked with recognizing our spiritual need and having a good relationship with Jehovah God. _W 4/1 10-12_

## Wednesday, February 10

_Be fruitful and become many and fill the earth and subdue it, and have in subjection . . . every living creature that is moving upon the earth.—Gen. 1:28._

“Will mankind ever fulfill the agelong dream of dwelling in an earthly paradise or will all men be destroyed in a cataclysm of fire brought on by their own folly or God’s judgment?” For some such questions may be merely of academic interest. But for many honest-hearted people in

all lands they are very realistic present-day problems of burning interest. We have found the answer to these questions in the Bible. For us, the hope of living forever “in an earthly paradise” is not an “agelong dream.” That hope has sure foundations, being based on sound Bible scholarship. Both the Hebrew and the Greek Scriptures show that God will not allow wicked men to destroy the earth. Neither is he going to destroy the earth himself. God revealed his purpose after creating man, and his purposes do not change.—Isa. 46:10, 11. _W 4/15 12, 13a_

## Thursday, February 11

_All these things I will give you if you fall down and do an act of worship to me.—Matt. 4:9._

Today the choice is between the two biggest organizations in existence. This is nothing new, but today the need for making the right choice is more urgent than ever before. Two thousand years ago a historical person, whose decision on the question would carry the greatest consequences for all the universe, was faced with the need to make such a choice. Two witnesses to this fact, two searchers for the historical facts, namely, Matthew and Luke, give us the testimony establishing the truth of it. Matthew tells us that Satan “showed \[Jesus] all the kingdoms of the world and their glory,” and he further said the above. Jesus did not dispute Satan’s claim, but he unhesitatingly rejected Satan’s offer. Jesus refused to desert the organization to which he already belonged and make himself a part of Satan’s organization, setting a fine example for us today.—Matt. 4:8-10. _W 5/1 1, 2_

## Friday, February 12

_I tell everyone there among you not to think more of himself than it is necessary to think; but to think so as to have a sound mind._—Rom 12:3.

Having the spirit of a sound mind will greatly help us to be willing to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. When we take a sober view of ourselves, we can find plenty of reason for conducting ourselves as lesser ones. How so? Each one is far more familiar with himself than with others. We should be able to see, far more than do others, how far short we come of being and doing what we should. (Rom. 7:15) Besides, as Christians we may not judge others too severely but must make allowances for their imperfections and give them the benefit of the doubt. But we are aware of our own weaknesses and where some wrong motive might have influenced us. So by this very fact we should be willing to conduct ourselves as lesser ones in relation to others. Yes, having the spirit of a sound mind will help us in these matters.—2 Tim. 1:7. W 6/1 6, 7a

## Saturday, February 13

_The kingdom of the world did become the kingdom of our Lord and of his Christ, and he will rule as king forever._—Rev. 11:15.

From the spring of 1935 a “great crowd” of people of all the nations began to be glad with the spirit-anointed “people” of Jehovah, who were heirs of his heavenly kingdom. Gladdened persons who make up that “great crowd” of all nationalities are keenly interested in becoming earthly subjects of God’s millennial kingdom by Christ.

They clearly discern that at the close of the Gentile Times in 1914 the above scripture applies. They appreciate that they are living in the time of crossing over from the doomed political governments of this world to the millennial kingdom of God’s Messiah or Christ. They would very much like to live through this transition period and, without dying, enter into that new order on earth, under that Messianic kingdom. This would be a great mercy on the part of Jehovah God toward them. To merit this mercy they must keep integrity. W 6/15 8a

## Sunday, February 14

_He that exercises faith in me, that one also will do the works that I do; and he will do works greater than these._—John 14:12.

How were Christians to do greater works than Jesus? He did the greatest, most lasting good by his marvelous teachings about the Kingdom. After he was resurrected Jesus commissioned his followers to “make disciples of people of all the nations.” (Matt. 28:19, 20) Yes, Christians were to carry on a vast witnessing work. It was to be on a wider scale than Jesus’ preaching, for a longer time and to far more people. Thus, they would do works greater than he did. On the day of Pentecost 33 C.E. Jesus poured out holy spirit on the 120 disciples waiting in Jerusalem. What did they do? Multiply food? Teach people about farming? Even cure some sick persons? No, they started to speak, to declare “the magnificent things of God.” (Acts 2:1-11) As a result, 3,000 were baptized. Are you having a full share in the greater works being done today? W 1/1 14, 15, 17, 18

## Monday, February 15

_Unless Jehovah himself guards the city, it is to no avail that the guard has kept awake._—Ps. 127:1.

Still, a watchman on a wall of a city has entrusted to him the safeguarding of the lives of the people living in it. He is under obligation to warn the citizens of any endangerment to their lives and liberty. Rightly, he should not want their blood to be charged to him. For his keeping awake and watching he is to be credited with safeguarding the lives of other human souls. That is the situation of the “faithful and discreet slave” class and their companions in this time when the old system of things is about to end. However, is it only those who claim to be Christian who are facing the threat of destruction at the end of this system of things? No, but all other religionists are, also those who refuse to join any part of organized religion. Jehovah knows this. He would rather not destroy the world of mankind. In that behalf he has had his warning sounded out far and wide. W 2/1 11, 12a

## Tuesday, February 16

_A true companion is loving all the time, and is a brother that is born for when there is distress._—Prov. 17:17.

What does it take to make you happy? To what extent is your happiness determined by conditions around you, by material things or by other people? Are God and the Bible involved in your finding happiness? Obviously, external things, such as our enjoying good food in pleasant surroundings, can contribute to our being happy. We can

especially appreciate this if in the past we have painfully experienced prolonged hunger. It is not contrary to God’s will for us to find a measure of happiness in good food, even as can be seen from the words of Solomon: “Every man should eat and indeed drink and see good for all his hard work. It is the gift of God.” (Eccl. 3:13) Other people, too, can contribute to our happiness, even as implied above. What a satisfying thing it is to have loved ones who care about us, whether they are members of our family or close friends!—Ps. 127:3-5. W 4/1 1-3

## Wednesday, February 17

_In showing honor to one another take the lead._—Rom. 12:10.

It is indeed practical wisdom to strive to have good relations with others. Conducting ourselves as lesser ones, such as “in showing honor to one another,” is one way to accomplish that desired goal. It is human nature to be concerned with one’s own advantages, and when we are ambitious or overly anxious to excel we make others feel uncomfortable. But when we conduct ourselves as lesser ones we do not threaten the position of others, we do not make them feel insecure or inferior. We do not put them on the defensive. As a result, they will be all the more likely to show friendliness and affection toward us. More than that, conducting ourselves as lesser ones will keep us from overextending ourselves in rivalry so as to excel. This will keep us from presuming to do things beyond our capabilities or resources; it will keep us from ‘biting off more than we can chew.’ Yes, it is wisdom to count the cost.—Luke 14:28. W 6/1 11a

## Thursday, February 18

_I saw another angel flying in midtheaven, and he had everlasting good news to declare as glad tidings to those who dwell on the earth, and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people.—Rev. 14:6._

Let the honest-hearted person compare the kind of preaching of the gospel of the Kingdom done by the religious systems of Christendom during all the centuries with that done by Jehovah's Witnesses since the end of World War I in 1918. They are not one and the same kind. That of Jehovah's Witnesses is really 'gospel,' or 'good news,' as of God's kingdom that was established by the enthronement of his Son Jesus Christ at the end of the Gentile Times in 1914. (Luke 21:24) The worldwide witness given to this effect under the barrage of international persecution and opposition could have been given only by means of God's all-conquering spirit. It was accomplished neither by the spirit of man nor by that of Satan the Devil. It was done with the help of God's holy angels, even as indicated above. _W 5/1 3, 4a_

## Friday, February 19

_God loved the world so much that he gave his only-begotten Son, in order that everyone exercising faith in him might not be destroyed but have everlasting life.—John 3:16._

Brushing aside all differences of religious background and social standing, Paul exhorts us to 'welcome one another,' doing so warmly, cordially, sincerely, in genuine appreciation of a fellow Christian. (Rom. 15:7) Paul says to do so 'just as the Christ also

welcomed us.' When he was on earth, did not Jesus say: 'The one that comes to me I will by no means drive away'? (John 6:37) Yes! As a perfect man, he could have kept his distance because of our imperfections and sinfulness. But he did not do so. Why not? Paul states the reason, by adding the words: 'With glory to God in view.' Welcoming all believers in him, Christ brought glory to God, for it magnified God's largeheartedness and his desire for all humans to be saved through the ransom sacrifice of his Son Jesus Christ. It was just as Jesus said at John 3:16. _W 6/15 10_

## Saturday, February 20

_Jehovah has anointed me to tell good news to the meek ones . . . to proclaim the year of goodwill on the part of Jehovah and the day of vengeance on the part of our God.—Isa. 61:1, 2._

Thirty-seven years after Jesus told the Jews of his day that their house was abandoned to them, Roman armies sacked Jerusalem and destroyed its temple. This was a calamity in which 1,100,000 of its rebellious people died! Then, at that time, the full force of Jesus' prophetic words became clear. Rightly, Jehovah had exacted vengeance! (Matt. 23:37, 38) Centuries earlier, Isaiah spoke the above words. Jesus read this prophecy, in part, at the start of his earthly ministry and applied it to himself as God's Anointed One. (Luke 4:18-21) However, when he quoted those words he stopped short of mentioning the day of God's vengeance. Why? Apparently the major emphasis on proclaiming the day of God's vengeance would come 'in the final part of the days.' (Isa. 2:2) Are you sharing in sounding that warning? _W 1/15 19-21_

## Sunday, February 21

_Really, how could I ever do so, unless someone guided me?—Acts 8:31._

Today there is a maze of conflicting opinions of rights and wrongs. In their search, some try to read the Bible but find it difficult. They are not the first ones. Nineteen centuries ago an Ethiopian man was riding in his chariot, reading from the book of Isaiah. In answer to Philip's question about whether he actually understood what he was reading or not, he answered as above. So Philip got into the chariot and rode along and guided him. (Acts 8:26-35; 21:8) Where are the 'Philips' of today qualified to guide others in determining from the Bible the course that gains a good name with God? Not in the established orthodox religions of Christendom, as many might expect. Philip was not a distinguished scribe or Pharisee, but was one of the spurned, maligned, persecuted Christians. The same is true today. Are you proving yourself to be a 'Philip' by being alert to opportunities to help others understand God's Word? _W 2/15 5, 6a_

## Monday, February 22

_Go away, Satan! For it is written, 'It is Jehovah your God you must worship, and it is to him alone you must render sacred service.'—Matt. 4:10._

That unwillingness to conduct oneself as a lesser one is folly can be seen from the fact that all the trouble in the world got started because a certain angel did not want to conduct himself that way. Why can that be said? Because that angel, who started our first parents on the road to sin and death, revealed his true

motives in the third wilderness temptation he presented to the Son of God. In it he offered all the kingdoms of the world if Jesus would do just one act of worship to him. What did that show? That Satan wanted to be equal to Jehovah God, that he did not want to conduct himself as a lesser one in relation to Him. This can be seen from Jesus' reply, as given above. Because of his unwillingness to conduct himself as a lesser one, Satan covered himself with shame and ignominy and eventually will be brought to nothing.—Heb. 2:14. _W 6/1 4_

## Tuesday, February 23

_The undeserved kindness given to me from God \[is] for me to be a public servant of Christ Jesus to the nations, engaging in the holy work of the good news of God.—Rom. 15:15, 16._

Paul actively worked as a 'public servant of Christ.' For what purpose? He added that he did so in order that 'the offering, namely, these nations, might prove to be acceptable.' What does that mean? At Romans 15:16 we have the only use in the Bible of a Greek verb that means 'to work or engage in a sacred thing.' Thus Paul was saying that he was actively engaged in the holy work of preaching the good news of God to people of the nations. Those who accepted the message and became Christians were like an offering made to God, an offering that Jehovah approved of and blessed with his spirit. How did Paul and others do this 'holy work of the good news of God,' preaching the Christian message? 'In the streets and from house to house.' (Acts 20:20, _Lamsa_) It is our privilege and duty to do the same. _W 1/1 6-8a_

## Wednesday, February 24

_The love is in this respect, not that we have loved God, but that he loved us and sent forth his Son as a propitiatory sacrifice for our sins.—1 John 4:10._

So God provided his Son to release us from the death that comes to us because of the sin inherited from Adam. Yes, as First Timothy 2:6 says, Christ “gave himself a corresponding ransom for all” who would exercise faith in him. Jesus could therefore say concerning his sheeplike followers: “I have come that they might have life,” yes, everlasting life. (John 10:10) Yet, repeatedly, the Bible tells us that the ‘God of love’ is also a ‘God of vengeance.’ Why? Because God’s love cannot forever tolerate evil. (Nah. 1:2; Deut. 32:35, 41) That is why the apostle Paul writes about Jesus bringing vengeance on “those who do not know God.” (2 Thess. 1:6-9) How important it is, then, that we come to know God! In this mixed-up world of so many different religions, how vital it is, as Acts 17:27 says, ‘to seek the true God and really find him!’ _W 1/15_ 9, 10

## Thursday, February 25

_Son of man, a watchman is what I have made you to the house of Israel.—Ezek. 3:17._

More than 30 years before atomic bombs were exploded in the Far East Jehovah had mercifully posted his watchman to sound the warning of what is evidently now near, inside this generation. Even before World War I broke out in 1914 God had his “watchman” class posted to sound out the warning. This was done not only to warn those who wanted to know how to survive but also to put Jehovah’s willful

enemies on notice. Thus such enemies will know from what source the destruction comes. No faultfinders will have any basis for complaining that He gave them no advance warning. Christendom should have been the agency to sound the warning, because of what she claims to be. But she has not served as such. Instead, she has joined in the two world wars, which have measurably ruined the earth. The composite “watchman” is the “faithful and discreet slave,” who was foreshadowed by the prophet Ezekiel.—Matt. 24:45-47. _W 2/1_ 1a

## Friday, February 26

_We sent Timothy, our brother and God’s minister in the good news about the Christ.—1 Thess. 3:2._

In the January 1, 1892, issue, the _Watch Tower_ magazine said: “Few know these co-porters \[“pioneers”] as the Lord’s real representatives, or recognize that dignity which the Lord sees in their humility and self-sacrifice. Missionaries? No, say the world and the nominal church, ours are the missionaries, who go to foreign lands. Yes, says the Lord, these are my missionaries, charged with a grand mission— . . . Ministers? No, say the world and the nominal church, only ours who wear ‘clerical’ garments and preach from our pulpits are God’s ministers. Yes, says the Lord, my servants (ministers) they are because they serve me, dispensing present truth to my household. I have sent forth the message which they bear.” Yes, even as was Timothy, so were these God’s ministers. And today, not only “pioneers” but all dedicated and baptized Christians who worship Jehovah in spirit and truth are his ministers. _W 3/15_ 10

## Saturday, February 27

_You will be witnesses of me . . . in all Judea and Samaria.—Acts 1:8._

Soon news got back to “the apostles in Jerusalem” that “Samaria had accepted the word of God.” Rising to the situation, the apostles sent two of their number, Peter and John, to consolidate the good work done by these scattered Christians, including Philip the evangelizer. Using the prerogative that Christ had granted him, Peter opened up the way for the Samaritans to become spirit-begotten, anointed Christians, called to share with Christ in “the kingdom of the heavens.” (Matt. 16:18, 19; Acts 8:14-17) As for Judea, doubtless many Judeans were present in Jerusalem at Pentecost and received the fine witness given by the newly anointed Christians, notably by Peter. These inhabitants of Judea received the witness concerning Jesus. Undeniably, the early Christians zealously carried out their Christ-given commission to be his witnesses. But what about each one of us individually? Are we being imitators of them? _W 3/1_ 10, 11

## Monday, March 1

_What God has yoked together let no man put apart.—Matt. 19:6._

It is indeed sad that some among those who profess to be dedicated servants of Jehovah God opt for selfish pleasure or the easy way out of an unhappy marital situation instead of sticking to Bible principles and looking to Jehovah God in prayer, consulting his Word and seeking help from the overseers in the congregation. Much as this is to be regretted and deplored, it actually should not

## Sunday, February 28

_There are new heavens and a new earth that we are awaiting according to his promise, and in these righteousness is to dwell.—2 Pet. 3:13._

How thankful we are for coming to a knowledge of God’s way of removing wickedness from the earthly scene! Do we not await such divine intervention with keen anticipation? Yes, and joyfully we hail the governmental new heavens and a cleansed new earth society that he has promised! So, then, forward, unitedly with the warning of “the day of vengeance on the part of our God”! (Isa. 61:2) Let us keep ourselves free from bloodguilt. (Ezek. 3:19-21) This is because we want to be spared during that “day of vengeance.” Many of our neighbors would like to enjoy the same salvation. May our supreme love for Jehovah and Christ and our humane love for our neighbor, our fellowman, move us irresistibly to sound the lifesaving warning. Tremendous joy will result to us! Best of all, Jehovah will be vindicated as having lovingly cared! _W 2/1_ 7b; 19a

surprise us in view of all that we see in the world today. There is no question about the fact that more couples are getting divorces. In some lands, such as the United States and Russia, one marriage in three ends in a divorce, and in some other countries the ratio is almost one out of every two. Some states in America have “no-fault” divorce, which doubtless plays its part in increasing divorces. How does Jehovah view divorcing? He tells us at Malachi 2:15, 16, and Jesus Christ expressed a similar view. _W 7/1_ 2-4a

## Tuesday, March 2

_I will cut off sorceries out of your hand, and no practicers of magic will you continue to have. And I will cut off your graven images and your pillars from the midst of you.—Mic. 5:12, 13._

During the past 100 years there has been an exposing of the false gods of Christendom. This exposure is without equal in the history of the nations since the destruction of Jerusalem by the Babylonians in the year 607 B.C.E. This has included the exposure of Christendom's own god, the so-called Trinity. The use of man-made images in religious worship can plainly be seen in the churches of Christendom. During this time of judgment, who are the ones that have carried on the work of exposure? The dedicated, baptized Christians who have been anointed with the spirit of Jehovah God through Jesus Christ. As a company, or a class, these were prefigured by Jehovah's prophet Micah. Particularly since 1935 these have been assisted by the great crowd of 'other sheep.'—John 10:16. W 7/15 13, 14

## Wednesday, March 3

_He that conducts himself as a lesser one among all of you is the one that is great.—Luke 9:48._

Who today are wisely content to conduct themselves as lesser ones? Very few! That is why there is so much contention, strife and warring in this old world. Nations keep competing with one another; each one wants to be on top, to be foremost, to be the most powerful. So nations saddle their people with enormous armament costs. And what is much of the agi-

tation by the women's liberation movement but a refusal on the part of its members to conduct themselves as lesser ones as compared to their menfolk? And is that not also what is plaguing many modern youths? They chafe at the role their elders play; they do not want to conduct themselves as lesser ones in relation to their elders, their parents. Has all such unwillingness to conduct oneself as a lesser one brought happiness? Is it wise? Far from it! Surely we all do well to take Jesus' words above to heart! W 6/1 3

## Thursday, March 4

_Welcome one another, just as the Christ also welcomed us, with glory to God in view.—Rom. 15:7._

Back in ancient Rome of the first century C.E. there might have been natural reasons for certain divisive things. The apostle Paul had not yet reached Rome, that cosmopolitan imperial city, but in hopes of getting there shortly he wrote his inspired letter to the congregation there. After calling attention to the approachableness of Jesus Christ as the perfect example, Paul went on to say the above. For one thing, 'all those who are in Rome as God's beloved ones, called to be holy ones,' included natural circumcised Jews and uncircumcised Gentiles or non-Jews, freemen and slaves. (Rom. 1:7; 3:1-6; Phil. 4:22) So among those Roman Christians there were differences of religious background and social standing, all of this producing varieties of viewpoints and conscientious feelings. This could have resulted also in preferences of one's associations. Paul's words brushed all of that aside. W 6/15 8-10

## Friday, March 5

_Unless Jehovah himself builds the house, it is to no avail that its builders have worked hard on it. Unless Jehovah himself guards the city, it is to no avail that the guard has kept awake.—Ps. 127:1._

The need for Jehovah God to be in every undertaking of ours is stressed time and again in the Scriptures, even as is noted in these words of the psalmist. The long history of the nation of Israel bears out the truth of this principle. When Israel let God be in their affairs by giving him exclusive devotion, their efforts to protect themselves were successful. But when they abandoned his pure worship to follow other gods, their guards watched over their cities in vain. The same principle applies to our making a success of marriage. God's Word tells us that he is the Giver of 'every good gift and every perfect present.' (Jas. 1:17) Among such good gifts and perfect presents must be included the gracious gift, the blessing, of marriage. What a potential it has for bringing happiness when Jehovah God is in it! W 7/1 1, 2

## Saturday, March 6

_He will do works greater than these, because I am going my way to the Father.—John 14:12._

On Pentecost such a convincing witness was given that thousands of Jews and proselytes accepted Christ. Truly, Jesus' followers had commenced doing works greater than he did. But some Christians today may feel, 'I'm no Peter or Paul. Were they not the sort of persons that would do the works greater than Jesus?' True, the apostles and other elders in the congregations

certainly did engage in preaching the Kingdom. But notice this historical fact related by Luke: 'Great persecution arose against the congregation that was in Jerusalem; all except the apostles were scattered throughout the regions of Judea and Samaria.' (Acts 8:1) And what did all these scattered Christians do? 'Those who had been scattered went through the land declaring the good news of the word.' (Acts 8:4) Yes, these all preached, declared the good news, and so all Christians today must likewise preach. W 1/1 17-19

## Sunday, March 7

_'The kingdom of the world did become the kingdom of our Lord and of his Christ'... 'But the nations became wrathful, and your own wrath came.'—Rev. 11:15, 18._

This enthroning of Christ with authority to rule is a preliminary step that paves the way for the execution of vengeance. Today, is anyone commissioned by God's spirit to proclaim the day of Jehovah's vengeance and to comfort all that mourn? (Isa. 61:1, 2) Yes, but it is not the churches of Christendom. They have no comforting message. Not only do they avoid using Jehovah's name, but they persecute true Christians who honor that name. However, we are happy to bear God's name, the greatest in the universe! We are overjoyed that, since the Kingdom's being established in that eventful year of 1914, we have been privileged to have a share in fulfilling Isaiah 61:1, 2. And we are delighted that millions of mourning ones have listened to our message, have been comforted and have flocked to the side of God's kingdom. W 1/15 2-4a

# Monday, March 8

This is what Jehovah has said, . . . the Former of the earth and the Maker of it, . . . who did not create it simply for nothing, who formed it even to be inhabited. —Isa. 45:18.

Scientists cannot prove that our earth and mankind upon it made themselves. There had to be a Creator. So how about him? As early as the 16th century before our Common Era he inspired Job to state the scientific truth that He has hung our earth upon nothing in space, and so, did He hang it there for nothing? (Job 26:7) Is it accidental that we find ourselves by the billions upon it? Was this a mistake or without purpose on his part? By now he has let this earth fairly brim with living creatures. Did he have in mind that all of today's billions of humans should kill themselves off and leave our earth floating around in space like a dead planet? We cannot assign such foolishness to Jehovah God in view of what he tells us above and that "the earth is standing even to time indefinite."—Eccl. 1:4. W 2/1 14

# Tuesday, March 9

This is at last bone of my bones and flesh of my flesh. This one will be called Woman, because from man this one was taken. —Gen. 2:23.

Marriage is truly one of the greatest blessings that Jehovah God, the Creator, has bestowed upon mankind. No wonder that when finally Eve was presented to Adam he exclaimed the above. Adam could rejoice over the blessing of a loving companion, someone of his own kind with whom he could converse,

work, plan—she was his perfect complement! Additionally, the connubial joys that go with marriage are indeed an evidence of our Creator's wisdom and love. Well has it been noted by lawyer I. Linton that the way God created man and woman is "an unequivocal evidence of creative design." Yes, "the peace and happiness growing out of the marital relation when God is in it has given some idea of the skilful power of God to bring happiness into being." Note, "when God is in it." This can indeed be true if we let Jehovah God be in our marriage. W 7/1 2, 3

# Wednesday, March 10

The mountain of the house of Jehovah . . . will certainly be lifted up . . . And many nations will certainly go and say: "Come, you people, and let us go up to the mountain of Jehovah . . . and he will instruct us about his ways, and we will walk in his paths." —Mic. 4:1, 2.

Truly, it has been solely by means of his Witnesses that Jehovah God has brought about the fulfillment of this prophecy. Thus, indeed, the worship of the one living and true God has been established, as it were, "above the top of the mountains," for it has been shown to be superior to every other form of worship. It is "firmly established" and so will never be toppled from its lofty position. It is being dignified by Jehovah's Witnesses, and so, figuratively speaking, it is being "lifted up above the hills." This exalting of the worship of Jehovah, making it visible from far away, was meant to be for the benefit of persons out of all nations as can be seen from the fulfillment of Revelation 7:9, 14. W 7/15 18, 19a

# Thursday, March 11

The Lord Jesus . . . said, "There is more happiness in giving than there is in receiving."—Acts 20:35.

Clearly the Bible record, as well as ancient and modern secular history, proves that conducting oneself as a lesser one is the course of wisdom. It makes for good relations with Jehovah God, with our fellow Christians and with members of our own families. Moreover, it is the course of happiness because it means giving others the advantages and the preferences, and "there is more happiness in giving than there is in receiving." Many, too, are the aids available so as to motivate us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones: heeding Biblical examples, recognizing the principle of headship, exercising the spirit of a sound mind, following the leadings of God's holy spirit, and showing practical wisdom and unselfish love. May we ever consider it a privilege, a blessing, to conduct ourselves as lesser ones, to the benefit of others and ourselves, and primarily to Jehovah's praise. W 6/1 18a

# Friday, March 12

I glorify my ministry.—Rom. 11:13.

It was not for self-glory that Paul zealously carried on his ministry. It was without cost to the ones to whom he preached the "good news." But by this he was not cheapening his ministry. Receivers of the "good news" still had to "count the cost" for doing something beneficial to themselves without paying for the "good news." Paul felt most highly honored at being entrusted with such glorious "good news,"

a ministry without equal when compared with all the high-paying occupations of this world. Today we imitate the example of Paul. How? By not engaging in the ministry of God's Kingdom message for personal gain in a material or worldly way. We view it as wrong to handle the Kingdom message like something of a commercial kind, as merely a means of making a comfortable living. Any secular work that we might be obliged to do takes a secondary place with us as a sideline. The Kingdom ministry is something worth making sacrifices for! W 3/15 7a

# Saturday, March 13

Better is it to go to the house of mourning than to go to the banquet house, because that is the end of all mankind; and the one alive should take it to his heart. —Eccl. 7:2.

When an ancient Israelite went to a house of mourning to comfort the bereaved ones, there was benefit for him also. Consider this in the light of the above words. Not only does a person show a kindly sympathy for the survivors, rather than callously pursuing his own pleasures, but also he ponders the fact that in this house someone has died, that it is not anything unusual, that death comes to every man, and that it will come to him also. When it does, will that day of his death be better than the day of his birth? Will he have acted wisely during his lifetime, so that by the day of his death he will have made a good name with God? The living must lay it to heart, while there is time to change, for a good name cannot be made in the few minutes of a deathbed repentance. W 2/15 5

## Sunday, March 14

_They keep on . . . saying: 'Salvation we owe to our God, . . . and to the Lamb.'—Rev. 7:10._

The order to be witnesses 'to the most distant part of the earth' and to go 'make disciples of people of all the nations' applies to all who claim to be Christians. Hence, all dedicated witnesses of Jehovah should have a deep, heartfelt determination to have a share in fulfilling that blessed commission. (Acts 1:8; Matt. 28:19) If you are now a member of the 'great crowd,' described in Revelation 7:9-17, remember that in order to 'come out of the great tribulation' you have to 'keep on saying' the above. This requires more than merely leading a good life and giving an occasional witness if and when the opportunity to do so occurs. How could the Christian witness have been given 'to the most distant part of the earth' if the early Christians and the modern-day remnant had taken that attitude? In order to make disciples, they first had to 'GO'! Yes, they had to get out and witness 'from house to house.'—Acts 5:42. W 3/1 9-11a

## Monday, March 15

_Moses, out of regard for your hardheartedness, made the concession to you of divorcing your wives, but . . . I say to you that whoever divorces his wife, except on the ground of fornication, and marries another commits adultery.—Matt. 19:8, 9._

When the religious leaders of Jesus' day asked him about the lawfulness of divorce, Jesus quoted from the Hebrew Scriptures: 'For this reason a man will leave his father and his mother and will stick to his wife, and the two will be one flesh,'

and then added, 'So that they are no longer two, but one flesh. Therefore, what God has yoked together let no man put apart.' (Matt. 19:5, 6) Upon receiving this answer, those Pharisees referred to Moses' granting a certificate of dismissal, to which Jesus replied as above. What do these scriptures tell us? That neither God nor his Son Jesus Christ takes lightly the violation of God's laws on marriage and divorce. Divorce tears apart what Jehovah God has joined together. He views marriage as a lifetime bond. W 7/1 4, 5a

## Tuesday, March 16

_When you spread a feast, invite poor people, crippled, lame, blind; and you will be happy, because they have nothing with which to repay you. For you will be repaid in the resurrection of the righteous ones.—Luke 14:13, 14._

If you are a husband, a wife or another member of a family group, why not make it a point today to discuss how you can put Jesus' suggestion into action? Then see how happy you will be. One means of achieving such happiness is by sharing with others Bible truths that can add to their happiness. Imagine how you will feel if you find a person who is truly hungering spiritually and you can help him to meet that need. True, it might take persistent effort to locate such a person and much patient care to help him to take in spiritual food from the Word of God. Yet he will find Jesus' words to be true: 'Happy are those hearing the word of God and keeping it!' (Luke 11:28) And you, too, will be happier for having given of yourself in making someone else happy. W 4/1 20, 21

## Wednesday, March 17

_To Jehovah the heavens belong, but the earth he has given to the sons of men.—Ps. 115:16._

Serious Bible study has led us to believe that the Christian Scriptural hope is twofold: The gift of immortality in heaven for a limited few, and everlasting life on earth for the greater number. The heavenly hope to 'rule as kings' with Christ is offered as an exceptional 'undeserved kindness.' (Rom. 5:17; Rev. 5:9, 10) The earthly hope is the original hope to which Adam and Eve could have attained, if they had stayed under God's sovereignty and not sought moral independence. Man is earthly by nature. His aspirations are earthly, for which reason the psalmist stated the above. And the Bible plainly states that Jehovah 'did not create the earth for nothing, but formed it to be inhabited.' (Isa. 45:18) Therefore the hope of everlasting life on earth in paradisaic conditions is both natural and Scriptural. We gladly make known this hope to others. W 4/15 5, 6a

## Thursday, March 18

_Trust in Jehovah with all your heart and do not lean upon your own understanding.—Prov. 3:5._

Since unwillingness to conduct oneself as a lesser one is folly, whereas being willing to do so is proof of wisdom, we will want to take this wise course. To do so we will need help because of our inherited imperfections. What will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones? Certainly considering what the Bible shows as to the bad fruits resulting from not doing so should

serve as warning examples for us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. Surely we do not want to lose out in the quest for life, do we? On the other hand, all the fine Scriptural examples of those who did conduct themselves as lesser ones and the rewards they received should encourage us to want to imitate them. One of the greatest aids in this regard is having a good relationship with Jehovah God, fully trusting him. Doing so, we will not be unduly concerned lest we come short, are downgraded or neglected. W 6/1 21; 1, 2a

## Friday, March 19

_Love never fails.—1 Cor. 13:8._

Jehovah God is the personification of principled love, of unselfishness, _a·ga·pe_ in Greek. That is why we read that 'God is love.' (1 John 4:8) So to let God be in our marriage there is the need to have not only the love based on natural attraction, sex interest (_e·ros_), and the affection based on kinship of mind and spirit (_phi·li·a_), but also the unselfish, principled kind of love. This love will keep a marriage together, even if the other two kinds of love diminish. The apostle Paul at 1 Corinthians 13:4-8 gives a fine description of how this love manifests itself. In view of those words of Paul, we cannot escape the conclusion that to be a good Christian means to be a good marriage mate. Conversely, to fail in marriage reflects unfavorably on one's being a Christian. Problems in marriage are to be viewed as challenges to cultivate the fruits of the spirit, chief of which is love. (Gal. 5:22, 23) And remember, 'LOVE NEVER FAILS'! W 7/1 16, 17

## Saturday, March 20

_'You are my witnesses,' is the utterance of Jehovah, 'even my servant whom I have chosen.'_—Isa. 43:10.

The Watch Tower of March 1, 1925, showed that the bringing forth of the man-child, mentioned at Revelation, chapter 12, portrayed the birth of God's Messianic kingdom from his wifelike heavenly organization. This took place in the heavens at the end of the Gentile Times in the autumn of 1914. The earthly part of Jehovah's spiritual organization was thrilled at this revelation! In 1925 also, the noteworthy fact came to the fore that the time had come for God to make a name for himself. This fact was publicized that summer at a regional convention. In line with God's purpose to take out of the nations 'a people for his name,' in July of 1931 the designation 'Jehovah's witnesses' burst forth upon the world, when, formally and by resolution, thousands of those belonging to 'this fold' of the Fine Shepherd adopted this Scriptural designation for God's dedicated, baptized people. W 5/1 17, 18b

## Sunday, March 21

_Christ actually became a minister of those who are circumcised in behalf of God's truthfulness, so as to verify the promises He made to their forefathers, and that the nations might glorify God for his mercy._—Rom. 15:8, 9.

The promised Messiah had to come in David's family. (2 Sam. 7:12-16) This accounts for it that Jesus was born in Bethlehem. Thus the Son of God from heaven could not escape being born a Jew. The unbreakable

promises of God his Father had to be confirmed or vindicated. God was not letting himself be proved a liar. Jesus was very glad to cooperate with his heavenly Father. Hence, he 'actually became a minister of those who are circumcised.' For three and a half years after Jesus' death and resurrection special favor was shown to the circumcised Jews. Yet a welcome into Jehovah's theocratic organization awaited the uncircumcised Gentiles. Also there was to be a confirmation of God's inviolable promises to men. It is our privilege to tell others about these things. W 6/15 19, 20

## Monday, March 22

_I myself have become full of power, with the spirit of Jehovah, and of justice and mightiness, in order to tell to Jacob his revolt and to Israel his sin._—Mic. 3:8.

The prophet Micah had a strong message to deliver and this took great courage on his part, yes, great spiritual strength. (Mic. 3:1-3) But Micah was equal to what this service called for, even as noted by his words above. Micah had something that the false prophets, visionaries and diviners of that day did not have, and so he had Jehovah's 'answer' to the national problems, namely, the 'revolt' of Jacob and the 'sin' of Israel. Personally, Micah had a sense of justice, so that he deeply appreciated that his own people were treating God unjustly, callously breaking God's law and violating their national covenant with him through the mediator Moses. As a fine example for the Christian witnesses of Jehovah of today, Micah was no religious weakling, but he was full of spiritual mightiness to identify sin as sin! W 7/15 9, 10

## Tuesday, March 23

_Look! a white horse. And the one seated upon it is called Faithful and True, . . . the armies that were in heaven were following him on white horses._—Rev. 19:11, 14.

What follows the devastation of Babylon the Great? The ten horns of the beast will turn against true religion, as represented on earth by Jehovah's Witnesses. But when that happens, those nations will find themselves fighting against God at Armageddon! It will be the greatest conflict of all time! (Rev. 16:14, 16) God will use Christ Jesus with the armies of heaven to 'judge and carry on war in righteousness.' Yes, Armageddon will be a righteous war in that it will bring vengeance upon peoples of earth that have chosen not to know God and who oppose his right to rule. It will destroy the wicked, but it will bring salvation to those who 'obey the good news about our Lord Jesus.' (2 Thess. 1:8) Thus, Armageddon will balance out God's vengeance with God's love. May we be found to be among those obedient ones! W 1/15 21, 22a

## Wednesday, March 24

_Wives, be in subjection to your husbands, as it is becoming in the Lord. You husbands, keep on loving your wives and do not be bitterly angry with them._—Col. 3:18, 19.

The husband should be concerned with pleasing his wife and bringing her joy, comfort and security, and the wife should be concerned with ministering to the needs of her husband and pleasing him the best she can, even as Paul notes. (1 Cor. 7:33, 34) More than that, to let God be

in your marriage means for both mates to have a warm personal relationship with Jehovah God, never neglecting prayer together. Share with God your joys, sorrows, disappointments, trials. Be concerned with giving him as a Person pleasure and gladness of heart. At Psalm 147:11 we read: 'Jehovah is finding pleasure in those fearing him.' And God tells us at Proverbs 27:11 that by being wise we can make his heart glad. So we want to be concerned, not just with his requirements for us as married folk, but also with how he feels about the way we heed them. W 7/1 5, 6

## Thursday, March 25

_The earth became filled with violence. So God saw the earth and, look! it was ruined, because all flesh had ruined its way on the earth._—Gen. 6:11, 12.

An owner of property would want to keep it in the best state of repair. Especially so if his property is valuable. That is the way the Creator feels about the earth. There is no denying the fact that the earth is being ruined today and is in danger of being ruined to a horrifying extent. It seems as if the time is already overdue for Jehovah God to get rid of all those responsible for ruining his originally perfect property. By now the time when he should do this cleansing work ought to be near. He has had a book written about this. Once before, all mankind then living was in a situation like the one all earth's population finds itself in today. At that time something occurred on a global scale. This was in the days of Noah. Jesus foretold that that deluge foreshadowed what would take place in our day. —Matt. 24:37-39. W 2/1 4, 5

### Friday, March 26

_Looking, they look in vain, and hearing, they hear in vain, neither do they get the sense of it._—Matt. 13:13.

The majority of mankind today do not understand that we are living in the 'last days,' just as the majority in Jesus' day did not grasp his warning message. What he said to them then applies to mankind today. (Matt. 13:13-15) In the next verse Jesus adds these words for his followers: 'However, happy are your eyes because they behold, and your ears because they hear.' Those today who have eyes and ears and hearts that see and hear and understand can be truly happy. 'As these things start to occur,' they are told, 'raise yourselves erect and lift your heads up, because your deliverance is getting near.' (Luke 21:28) Some of those living now may never have to face the day of their death. Just as Noah and his family were preserved through the Flood, so today those who act wisely and make a good name with God may not see death when God destroys this evil system of things. W 2/15 14, 15

### Saturday, March 27

_Go on telling them, 'The kingdom of God has come near to you.'—Luke 10:9._

Good effects do indeed come when true Christians live their lives in harmony with God's will. The fine conduct of Christians will move some to glorify God. But the Bible makes it clear that the focus of Christianity is not merely on developing a fine personality that reflects the 'fruitage of the spirit.' (Gal. 5:22-24) Important as these features of Christianity are, there is yet a

unique work to do if one is to be a true follower of Jesus. As a man, Jesus at all times honored his heavenly Father. His way of life and personality set a fine example for others, moving many to glorify God. As soon as he was baptized Jesus commenced 'preaching the good news of the kingdom.' (Matt. 4:23) He also trained others to share in this work, sending them out to teach people. That work expanded, for it was God's will that all peoples of the earth might be helped to glorify him. Are you having a fine share in this Christian work? W 1/1 4, 5a

### Sunday, March 28

_It does not belong to you to get knowledge of the times or seasons which the Father has placed in his own jurisdiction; but you will receive power when the holy spirit arrives upon you.—Acts 1:7, 8._

The import of Jesus' parting words was twofold. He first tactfully, yet firmly, told his disciples that the timing of the outworking of Jehovah's purposes was not their concern. He thus confirmed what he had already stated in his prophecy on the conclusion of the system of things. (Matt. 24:36) Then he went on to show them what would henceforth be their concern. They were to be Christian witnesses first in Jerusalem, where they had been told to remain for the time being, then in all Judea and Samaria and, ultimately, 'to the most distant part of the earth.' (Acts 1:8) To that end they would receive 'power' by means of the holy spirit. That 'power' arrived upon them at Pentecost. Immediately they set about carrying out their commission, setting a fine example for us today. W 3/1 4, 5

### Monday, March 29

_A day in your courtyards is better than a thousand elsewhere._—Ps. 84:10.

If we appreciate the importance of God's kingdom, then we will be ready to put its interests first in our lives. Doing so will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. Why can this be said? Because if we appreciate that the Kingdom work all of us are engaged in is the important thing and not we ourselves, then we will not feel unduly disturbed if we happen to have been overlooked. We want to take God's work, not ourselves, seriously. After all, there are only so many parts on the various congregation meetings and on circuit assembly and district convention programs. So some of us are bound to be left out. If that should be our lot, let us rejoice with the brothers that do have the privileges rather than envy them. Let us have the mental disposition of the psalmist as expressed above. Yes, it is far better to be a lesser one in Jehovah's organization than to be a greater one in Satan's organization. W 6/1 9a

### Tuesday, March 30

_In anger and in rage I will execute vengeance upon the nations that have not obeyed.—Mic. 5:15._

Jehovah was not the one responsible for the bad conditions in ancient Israel. Neither is he responsible for the bad conditions morally and religiously inside Israel's present-day counterpart, Christendom. The responsible ones back in Micah's day, and likewise today, were and are 'the god of this system of things,' Satan the Devil, and

all the demon gods associated with him. (2 Cor. 4:4) All these false gods have had their day, to do all the damage they could. Now, though, the time has come for Jehovah the Almighty God to bring them all into judgment and to put a stop to all their forms of worship. Jehovah's irreversible purpose to do this in the case of typical Israel he set forth by his prophet Micah, as recorded at Micah 5:12-15. Jehovah's people today have a like obligation to make known Jehovah's anger against all the false gods of Christendom. W 7/15 11, 12

### Wednesday, March 31

_Abhor what is wicked.—Rom. 12:9._

Today, the world has gone sex mad. Promiscuity is the order of the day. There is no doubt about there being an increasing of lawlessness. A Christian is thrown in the way of temptation at his or her place of employment, being surrounded by persons who are not governed by Bible principles and who may appear physically attractive. So one must continually be on guard, exercising self-control and keeping contacts with those of the opposite sex, other than one's marriage mate, on a businesslike basis. Also to be guarded against is the media—newspapers, magazines, television and motion pictures. Do not, by means of TV, in effect invite into your home fornicators, adulterers and suchlike persons. Nor should we overlook the fact that overindulgence in rich food and liquor may cause a Christian to become more easily aroused. Practice self-control in all aspects of life! 'Hate what is bad.'—Ps. 97:10. W 7/1 18a

<sup>**Page 149**</sup>

## Thursday, April 1

_Seek ye Jehovah, . . . it may be ye will be hid in the day of Jehovah's anger.—Zeph. 2:3, American Standard Version._

'Hidden of Jehovah'—that is the thought behind the name of God's prophet Zephaniah, which name means 'Jehovah has concealed.' Is it our desire to be hidden of Jehovah? It should be, for the world of today is plunging ever deeper into distress and hopelessness. Not for long will the righteous God, Jehovah, permit such conditions to remain. His day of Armageddon is at hand. All of us should be deeply interested in Zephaniah's prophecy, for it has much to say about that day of decision. It shows how we may find the place of refuge, so as to be 'hidden of Jehovah.' Zephaniah's prophecy looks down to our day with a dynamic warning that must be heeded by all who hope to find security during the impending day of Jehovah's burning anger. It is part of Jehovah's powerful 'prophetic word' that the apostle Peter likens to 'a lamp shining in a dark place.'—2 Pet. 1:19. W 8/15 1, 2

## Friday, April 2

_Who really is the faithful and discreet slave whom his master appointed over his domestics, to give them their food at the proper time?—Matt. 24:45._

The fact that the 'harvest' of the 'sons of the kingdom' is well advanced proves that the 'conclusion \[synte'leia] of the system of things' is nearing its end (_telos_). (Matt. 13:36-43) Our attitude toward the 'brothers' of Christ and the treatment we accord them will be the determining factor as to whether we go

into 'everlasting cutting-off' or receive 'everlasting life.' (Matt. 25:34-46) Let us prove ourselves loyal companions of the anointed class, the 'faithful and discreet slave,' whom Christ appointed to provide spiritual food. Let us keep active in the ingathering work, for remember, 'this good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness to all the nations; and then the end will come,' and 'he that has endured to the end is the one that will be saved.'—Matt. 24:13, 14. W 8/1 20a

## Saturday, April 3

_May the God who supplies endurance and comfort grant you to have among yourselves the same mental attitude that Christ Jesus had, that with one accord you may with one mouth glorify the God and Father.—Rom. 15:5, 6._

The thing for us to do is to have the same frame of mind that Jesus Christ had during all his sufferings in an enemy world. In line with this Paul framed the above prayer. By building up such a mental attitude in imitation of our Exemplar, Jesus Christ, we will keep in unity as a congregation of his disciples. The same mental attitude in a group leads to similar expressions. Thus it seems as if 'one mouth' were speaking for the entire congregation with greater force and impressiveness. This is most fitting. Never can too much stress be laid upon glorifying the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ unitedly. There should be a blending of our voices with regard to the One who is to be glorified. Otherwise the hearer would become confused as to the message to be conveyed. W 6/15 7

## Sunday, April 4

_With the rod they will strike upon the cheek the judge of Israel.—Mic. 5:1._

In line with God's words at Genesis 3:15, the prophecy of Micah foretold the foregoing. But now that the judgment day of all the false gods of the nations has begun, the great archangel Michael, whose name means 'Who is like God?', has battled with Satan the Devil and his demon angels and has ousted them from the heavens. (Rev. 12:7-12) This judgment day of all the false gods will continue on until 'the war of the great day of God the Almighty' at Har-Magedon is over and Satan and all the other demon gods are abysses for the 1,000 years of Christ's reign. (Rev. 16:14-16; 20:1-3) Thus the glorified Governor out of Bethlehem, Jesus Christ, will govern all mankind without interference by all the false gods and will instruct all the families of the earth in the life-giving worship of the one living and true God. (Mic. 5:2) It is our precious privilege to make this good news known to all who will hear. Are you doing your part? W 7/15 20, 21

## Monday, April 5

_Though I am free from all persons, I have made myself the slave to all, that I may gain the most persons. And so to the Jews I became as a Jew, that I might gain Jews . . . I have become all things to people of all sorts, that I might by all means save some.—1 Cor. 9:19, 20, 22._

Among Christ's followers, the apostle Paul in particular was a fine imitator of Jesus Christ in this matter of being willing to conduct oneself as a lesser one.

For the sake of the 'good news,' he became the slave of all. That certainly was conducting himself as a lesser one. He conducted himself as a lesser one in relation to the governing body in Jerusalem, with regard to his ministerial activity, where he should preach, and in doctrinal matters. (Acts 15:2; Gal. 2:7-10) On one occasion he felt obliged to ask the Christian congregation at Corinth: 'Did I commit a sin by humbling myself that you might be exalted?' (2 Cor. 11:7) No wonder Jehovah God so richly blessed Paul's ministry! What a fine example he set for us! W 6/1 20

## Tuesday, April 6

_The end of all flesh has come before me, because the earth is full of violence.—Gen. 6:13._

What occurred in the days of Noah to make this earth a peaceful, safe location in which to live? Did those violent ruiners of the earth engage in a global war? Was the warning that Noah was then commanded to give a warning about a man-made calamity that would be impossible for humanity to outlive? No! Instead, he sounded to mankind a _divine_ warning, one that God had instructed him to give. It put all men on notice as to what God the Creator was about to do for the sake of a calm, safe earth on which decent people could enjoy living. God told Noah to build an ark for himself and his family. On the foretold day of the year 2370 B.C.E., the global flood began. It was an 'act of God.' Drowned mankind paid the penalty. That ancient 'act of God' worked good for all mankind. We can be thankful that Armageddon, foreshadowed by that deluge, will also work good for all mankind. W 2/1 6, 7
