## Wednesday, April 7

_You loved righteousness, and you hated lawlessness.—Heb. 1:9._

What did Jesus have to say about God's love and vengeance? For one thing, he spoke warmly of his Father's love. (John 3:16) But did he hesitate to speak of God's vengeance? Why no! For he was like his Father in 'loving righteousness and hating lawlessness.' In particular Jesus hated the lawlessness of the religious leaders. He never minced words when he spoke to, or about, them. Three times in his Sermon on the Mount he called them 'hypocrites.' He told them: 'You are from your father the Devil.' (John 8:44) Then, just three days before he was murdered, Jesus denounced those Jewish religious leaders publicly. (Matt. 23:13-33) Did this mean that Jesus was unloving? Not at all. For while Jesus knew that those Jews were about to add to their bloodguilt by killing him, he said he had wanted to gather Jerusalem's children as a hen gathers her chicks, but they did not want it, and so their house was abandoned. —Matt. 23:37, 38. _W 1/15_ 18, 19

## Thursday, April 8 Memorial Date After Sundown

_He is a propitiatory sacrifice for our sins, yet not for ours only but also for the whole world's. —1 John 2:2._

When John wrote those words, most likely he had already received the Revelation in which, after having seen the 144,000 'sealed' spiritual Israelites, he saw 'a great crowd, which no man was able to number.' These survive the 'great tribulation' and are guided by the 'Lamb,' Christ Jesus, 'to fountains of

waters of life.' (Rev. 7:4-17) And, of course, it was in that same Revelation that John also had a vision of the millennial reign of Christ, which again mentions two groups: those 'having part in the first resurrection,' who 'will rule as kings,' and 'mankind,' who will be blessed by God and who 'will be his peoples.' (Rev. 20:1-21:8) Today, the millennial hope triumphs in the hearts of those of the 'little flock.' (Luke 12:32) That hope has been embraced also by those of the 'great crowd' who have joined the remnant in proclaiming 'this good news of the kingdom.'—Matt. 24:14. _W 4/15_ 9, 10a

## Friday, April 9

_I say to you that everyone divorcing his wife, except on account of fornication, makes her a subject for adultery, and whoever marries a divorced woman commits adultery.—Matt. 5:32._

Divorcing and adultery nearly always go hand in hand. Especially where a legal divorce is obtained without adultery on the part of either mate, the temptation is strong for the innocent mate to become 'a subject for adultery.' And there is no doubt that adultery by either mate would not leave one with a clean conscience before Jehovah God. How can it when it is spawned in deceit, even as we read: 'As for the eye of the adulterer, it has watched for evening darkness, saying, 'No eye will behold me!' And over his face he puts a covering.' (Job 24:15) Helping us to view adulterous divorcing in the right light are the words of David at Psalm 36:1-4, which well describe the deceitfulness and self-deception of the adulterer, his lack of fear of God and his scheming hurtfulness. _W 7/1_ 13, 14a

## Saturday, April 10

_Come into his gates with thanksgiving, into his courtyards with praise. Give thanks to him.—Ps. 100:4._

If we have reason to be grateful for what fellow humans do, we have vastly stronger reasons to be grateful to our Creator. The 100th Psalm is a melody of thanksgiving, calling attention to why it is appropriate to thank God. There we read: 'It is he that has made us, and not we ourselves.' (Ps. 100:3) That is so reasonable. How could we have any possibility of finding happiness if God had not created human life and allowed us to live? Yes, the very fact that we have life is a reason for gratitude. Psalm 100:5 proclaims: 'For Jehovah is good.' That is true in many ways. Not the least of these is that God has provided good things to keep us alive, and to enjoy at the same time. We live on God's earth and sustain our lives from its bounties. The ultimate source of all good things is Jehovah God. In connection with all God's bounteous provisions, how do we feel toward God? _W 4/1_ 8, 9a

## Sunday, April 11

_The undeserved kindness \[was] given to me from God for me to be a public servant of Christ Jesus to the nations, engaging in the holy work of the good news. —Rom. 15:15, 16._

We are intensely interested in this work and so is Jehovah God. His acceptance and blessing continue on this preaching work, including the important house-to-house activity. Experiences show that good effects can be produced even when it seems

that most of those encountered reject the 'good news' being brought to them. Actually, God's blessing on this work is felt in many ways. As a circuit overseer once wrote: 'The evangelizing is growing stronger. We have been spending much more time in the preaching activity. With this increased effort, more persons interested in the 'good news' are being located. And since the brothers and sisters are busier in proclaiming the 'good news,' a spirit of peace and joy is really becoming more evident in the congregations.' _W 1/1_ 18-21a

## Monday, April 12

_I will give attention to the men . . . who are saying in their heart, 'Jehovah will not do good, and he will not do bad.'—Zeph. 1:12._

In Zephaniah's day, Jehovah's examination of his professed worshippers was thorough. Such self-centered ones were content with the status quo. They were like faithless Christendom today, and also some who have fallen away from worshiping Jehovah, saying, 'Where is the proof that these really are the 'last days'?' Such thinking proved disastrous in Zephaniah's day, and it can be even more disastrous in our day, as we face up to the end of the entire world system of things. As shown by Zephaniah 1:13, worldly possessions avail nothing in a day of divine judgment! Rather than seek such material things today, how much more practical it is to cultivate a Scriptural outlook on life, and for young people to equip themselves for full-time service to Jehovah! Happy always are those who choose 'treasures in heaven' instead of 'storing up treasures upon the earth!' _W 8/15_ 8, 9

## Tuesday, April 13

_At that time the righteous ones will shine as brightly as the sun in the kingdom of their Father._—Matt. 13:43.

From Revelation 21:23 it is clear that the heavenly kingdom does not depend for light on the resurrected “sons of the kingdom.” It is bathing in glorious divine light. At Colossians 1:13 Paul explains what Jehovah has done for these sons while they are still on earth in this regard. Hence, the “righteous ones” shining “as brightly as the sun in the kingdom of their Father” must refer to the enlightened condition and the glorious service of such anointed Christians on earth, where they “are shining as illuminators in the world.” (Phil. 2:15; Matt. 5:14) The “storehouse” into which they have been gathered since 1919 can be said to be “the kingdom of their Father,” inasmuch as the Christian congregation is a theocratic organization. It is a clean organization, for out of it the angels collect “all things that cause stumbling and persons who are doing lawlessness.”—Matt. 13:30, 41. W 8/1 15, 16a

## Wednesday, April 14

_Let wives also be \[in subjection] to their husbands in everything. Husbands, continue loving your wives, just as the Christ also loved the congregation._—Eph. 5:24, 25.

Practical wisdom that makes us conduct ourselves as lesser ones also applies within the family circle. For example, the wise wife will be content to play the complementary, submissive role in relation to her husband, knowing that this makes for peace in the family and is conducive to happiness. Being willing

to play such a role wins her husband’s affection and love, and makes him want to do things for her. And what could make a wife happier than to have her husband so well disposed toward her that he is always giving her tokens of appreciation and affection, by words and deeds? In this regard, it is also practical wisdom for a husband to acknowledge wherein his wife excels and be content to play the lesser role, granting her such recognition under his headship. This will have a fine effect on her. W 6/1 12a

## Thursday, April 15

_A name is to be chosen rather than abundant riches; favor is better than even silver and gold._—Prov. 22:1.

Some translations supply the adjective “good” to show the kind of name meant. Necessarily the name is a good one, as in Ecclesiastes 7:1; otherwise, neither of the statements would make sense. As we live, we make names for ourselves—good names or bad names. If we act wisely in God’s eyes, we make a good name for ourselves with God. But it takes time. On the day of our birth we have not lived long enough to make any kind of name at all. Furthermore, we are born under Adamic sin and condemned to death. (Rom. 5:12) Hence, if years later, on the day of our death, we have made a good name with God, we have something that we did not possess on the day of our birth. We have a name that God will remember when he resurrects the dead to life under Christ’s kingdom. “The remembrance of the righteous one is due for a blessing, but the very name of the wicked ones will rot.”—Prov. 10:7. W 2/15 3, 4

## Friday, April 16

_The tongue of the wise ones is a healing._—Prov. 12:18.

One way that married couples can show kindness to each other is by letting each other know what is in the heart and mind. Yes, this means not just exchanging ideas but also sharing the way each one feels about things. Give thought to mentioning upbuilding things in your conversation, in keeping with the above proverb. A married couple not only is of one flesh, but also should be of one heart and mind, even as Jesus prayed that his followers might be one. (John 17:21) Communication is imperative for such oneness. In particular is it a kindness to talk things over when there have been misunderstandings or hurt feelings. Jesus stated a principle regarding human relations that couples often overlook or neglect to their harm. Do you feel that you have been treated unkindly or wronged in some way? Then summon up the courage to bring the matter up at an opportune time, doing so in a kind and tactful way.—Matt. 18:15. W 7/1 10, 11

## Saturday, April 17

_You will be witnesses of me . . . in all Judea and Samaria._—Acts 1:8.

Those early disciples managed to “fill Jerusalem with their teaching.” But they could not stop there. They were to be Christ’s witnesses also in “Judea and Samaria.” Actually, it was their very zeal in carrying out the first part of their commission that led them into fulfilling its second part. Opposition to their witnessing in Jerusalem reached a climax with the

murder of Stephen, and this set in motion a wave of persecution against the Christian witnesses in Jerusalem. The purpose of this violent opposition was to silence these witnesses of Christ. Instead, it gave a new boost to the witnessing work and extended it just where Christ wanted it to go. “All except the apostles were scattered throughout the regions of Judea and Samaria.” (Acts 8:1) And what did they do in these areas? Declared the good news of the word. They defeated persecution even as Jehovah’s people are defeating it today. W 3/1 8, 9

## Sunday, April 18

_Seek Jehovah . . . Seek righteousness._—Zeph. 2:3.

We must seek to know Jehovah, along with his marvelous qualities and purposes, even as his Son has explained him. Our humbly doing this brings great reward, as Jesus himself said. (John 17:3) Our seeking Jehovah will lead to our loving him with our “whole heart, soul, mind and strength,” and to our following Jesus’ example in performing the “sacred service” that Jehovah requires of us. We must also seek righteousness. It does no one any good to “have a zeal for God” if this is not in accord with accurate knowledge of his Word. We need to know the righteousness of God to subject ourselves to that righteousness. We need to conform our lives to God’s standards—not man’s—and to put on the Christian personality that is “created according to God’s will in true righteousness and loyalty.” (Eph. 4:22-24) This true righteousness and loyalty require that we do not let the ways of the world seep into our lives. W 8/15 19, 20

## Monday, April 19

_When the blade sprouted and produced fruit, then the weeds appeared also.—Matt. 13:26._

Jesus' illustration does much to explain the history of Christianity throughout the centuries. Historical facts show that after the death of the apostles Satan introduced among the congregations of true Christians many 'weeds,' 'oppressive wolves' and 'antichrists,' just as Jesus, Paul, Peter, John and Jude had foretold. (Acts 20:29; 2 Pet. 2:1-3; 1 John 2:18; Jude 4) It has been just as Jesus stated. These 'weeds' became particularly apparent during the second and third centuries, at which time such unscriptural doctrines as the inherent immortality of the soul, hellfire and the Trinity began to be taught by the so-called church fathers. Many of them were more philosophers than true Christian overseers faithful to the teachings of the Bible. The resulting counterfeit Christianity has produced a bumper crop of 'weeds' not only throughout the centuries but also right up until the present time. _W 8/1 16, 17_

## Tuesday, April 20

_The great day of Jehovah is near. It is near, and there is a hurrying of it very much.—Zeph. 1:14._

Let no one think that God's judging of this wicked world is far away in the future. Today, destruction of all human life on earth is threatening, but that is something that Jehovah will not permit! It is the Sovereign Lord Jehovah, and not politically minded men, who will bring an end to this madness of the nuclear age. He will 'hurry up' his day of Armageddon.

Any 'mighty man' who tries to thwart that execution of judgment is doomed to cry bitterly. (Zeph. 1:15, 16) The fortifications of this militarized world will be useless against the weaponry of Jehovah's heavenly arsenal. Moreover, it will be a selective destruction. (Ps. 145:20) It will be a terrible day of judgment, indeed, for all who have not kept respectful silence before Jehovah. In this final day of decision, 'all the earth' is included in God's final execution of judgment. But there is a way of escape!—Zeph. 2:3. _W 8/15 11-13_

## Wednesday, April 21

_Those who did good things \[will come out] to a resurrection of life, those who practiced vile things to a resurrection of judgment.—John 5:29._

From a careful reading of scriptures that speak of the millennial reign of Christ, we know that the millennium will call for much self-sacrifice on the part of those who share the earthly hope. There will be much work to do in cultivating and beautifying the earth, but they will not selfishly cultivate paradise conditions just for themselves and their families. Christ's 1,000-year reign is in fact a 'day' of judgment for those who survive the fast-approaching 'war of the great day of God the Almighty.' (Acts 17:30, 31; Rev. 16:14, 16) It is also the judgment day for the millions of the dead who will be resurrected and judged according to the works they will practice on earth. They will need to be taught the ways of righteousness by those already living under the Messiah's millennial rule. It will mean hard work, also, on a spiritual level. _W 4/15 19a_

## Thursday, April 22

_We have a wrestling, not against blood and flesh, but . . . against the wicked spirit forces in the heavenly places.—Eph. 6:12._

Thus Paul exposes Satan's invisible organization. But Paul is not saying that there is no visible earthly part to Satan's organization. He is merely reminding us that we as Christians are not fighting a violent war against the visible human elements of Satan's organization in order to overthrow it. Jesus Christ, when in the flesh on the earth, did not try to overthrow Satan's organization, and neither should his followers try to do so, for in such an effort they would waste their time and would fail. They need to carry on a spiritual war in order not to be made a part of Satan's organization. (Eph. 6:11-18) There just is no denying that Satan has a mighty organization with invisible and visible parts. Satan the Devil is a mimic for the purpose of deception, and the fact that he has an organization argues, in effect, that his chief opponent, Jehovah God, also has an organization. _W 5/1 6, 7_

## Friday, April 23

_'For this reason a man will leave his father and his mother and will stick to his wife, and the two will be one flesh.' So that they are no longer two, but one flesh. Therefore, what God has yoked together let no man put apart.—Matt. 19:5, 6._

That is why the seventh of the Ten Commandments stated: 'You must not commit adultery.' (Ex. 20:14) More than that, the law of Moses required that the Israelites stone to death any man who committed adultery

tery with another man's wife, as well as the adulteress. (Lev. 20:10) Most fittingly, at Hebrews 13:4 the apostle Paul warns that Jehovah God will judge adulterers and fornicators. Strictly speaking, adultery is far more serious than fornication, which English lexicographers define as sex relations between persons not married. But adultery has the added factor of violating, or breaking, or adulterating, the marital bond. Thus the German word for adultery is _Ehebruch_, which, literally translated, means a breaking of the marital tie. _W 7/1 6, 7a_

## Saturday, April 24

_These are the things that the Amen says, the faithful and true witness.—Rev. 3:14._

In 1931 Jehovah's people confessed themselves to be 'Jehovah's witnesses.' Though challenged by Christendom, the name has stuck! Those bearers of this new name recognized that their Leader, Jesus Christ, was and is the greatest witness of Jehovah. In the last book of the Bible, the Revelation that Jehovah God used the glorified Jesus Christ to impart to the aged apostle John, the only-begotten Son of God says the above about himself. Like his Father, Jehovah, the Lord Jesus bore witness against the deplorable conditions in the Israel of his day, similar to those that Micah described. Outstandingly, Jesus is the one whom Jehovah now uses in this judgment day for all the false gods. And even as Jesus was 'the faithful and true witness' while upon earth, so all his followers both the remnant and the 'great crowd' of 'other sheep' must be faithful and true witnesses. _W 7/15 14, 15_

### Sunday, April 25

_Welcome one another, just as the Christ also welcomed us, with glory to God in view.—Rom. 15:7._

When we welcome all truth seekers without distinction, it is quite a stimulus to us to remember that we are doing so “with glory to God in view.” It moves the welcomed ones to appreciate the warmhearted generosity of God and to glorify him themselves. When we go outside the walls of our Kingdom Halls and go from door to door to proclaim the good news of God’s kingdom to all whom we meet, we demonstrate that we “welcome one another, just as the Christ also welcomed us, with glory to God in view.” This course results in glory to the God of whom we are witnesses regardless of whether those upon whom we call appreciate the Kingdom message or not. Those who do not welcome our God-given message of salvation will at some future time realize that Jehovah God had thought of them, leaving them no reason to find fault with God. God thus stays free from accusation with respect to their blood. W 6/15 12

### Monday, April 26

_There also arose a heated dispute among them over which one of them seemed to be greatest. But he said to them: “The kings of the nations lord it over them . . . You, though, are not to be that way. But let him that is the greatest among you become as the youngest.—Luke 22:24-26._

The problem of conducting oneself as a lesser one was a recurrent one for the apostles, lasting even to the evening of the last valid passover. However, let it be noted that we

read nothing of this kind concerning them once they had received God’s holy spirit at Pentecost. But other first-century Christians did have that problem. It appears that some of the Christians at Corinth were not content to conduct themselves as lesser ones in relation to the apostle Paul. And the apostle John found it necessary to censure Diotrephes severely because of his wanting to have the first place and not treating with respect what John had to tell the Christians back there.—2 Cor. 10:1-11; 12:5-9; 3 John 9, 10. W 6/1 8

### Tuesday, April 27

_Show yourselves thankful.—Col. 3:15._

Though this advice involves especially our gratitude to God, it should suggest to us the value of cultivating a spirit of thankfulness in everyday matters of life. Paul himself did not hesitate to express commendation of those who had done good, or to thank them directly. At Romans 16:1-4, he gratefully commended Phoebe as ‘one who had defended many,’ possibly by using her influence in the community to defend wrongly accused Christians, or by displaying hospitality to traveling Christians. Then Paul specifically thanked Prisca and Aquila for ‘risking their necks’ in behalf of himself and others. You can imagine what pleasure Phoebe, Prisca and Aquila must have felt over such openly expressed gratitude. But it was also good for Paul to express himself in that way. He could thus have the happiness of giving—giving recognition, honor and encouragement. We, too, can receive such happiness when we express gratitude.—Acts 20:35. W 4/1 4, 5a

### Wednesday, April 28

_Love is long-suffering and kind. Love is not jealous, it does not brag, does not get puffed up, does not behave indecently, does not look for its own interests.—1 Cor. 13:4, 5._

Truly such love is the key to a happy marriage. And what opportunities for happiness there are in the marital state! Christ Jesus said “there is more happiness in giving than there is in receiving,” and this principle also applies to married persons. (Acts 20:35) How many opportunities for giving husbands and wives have, giving of themselves, their time, their attention, their thoughts, their feelings, giving material things! And in their relationship also the Scriptural principle applies: “He that sows sparingly will also reap sparingly; and he that sows bountifully will also reap bountifully.” (2 Cor. 9:6) Heed all such counsel and you will let God be in your marriage. More than that, thereby you will vindicate the love and wisdom of the great Institutor of the marital arrangement. W 7/1 20

### Thursday, April 29

_The harvest is a conclusion of a system of things.—Matt. 13:39._

The word here used, _synte’leia_, is the same word used at Matthew 24:3, where the disciples asked Jesus: “Tell us, When will these things be, and what will be the sign of . . . the conclusion \[_synte’leia_] of the system of things?” So the “harvest season” is connected with Christ’s invisible presence as Harvester. In answer to his disciples’ question, Jesus enumerated international warfare, food shortages, pestilences, great earthquakes, law-

lawlessness and a general climate of fear. (Compare Matthew 24, Mark 13 and Luke 21.) Then, showing that the “harvest season” would come to an end, he added: “And this good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness to all the nations; and then the end \[_telos_] will come.” (Matt. 24:14) The word _telos_ is said to refer to “the final act in the cosmic drama.” The facts in fulfillment of prophecy show that the present system of things is well into its “conclusion.” W 8/1 3, 4a

### Friday, April 30

_I glorify my ministry.—Rom. 11:13._

Because many of us find it necessary to do secular work most of our time, it does not mean or argue that we are not real ministers of Jehovah God. Although being worker-ministers, we put the interests of God’s kingdom ahead of everything else. Because of preaching God’s kingdom even from house to house, we are indeed Kingdom ministers. By the praiseworthy quality of the work that we render to our secular employers we, as worker-ministers, indirectly ‘glorify our ministry.’ This brings credit to Jehovah God to whom we render sacred service. It goes without saying that, if circumstances change to allow us as worker-ministers to engage in the Kingdom ministry full time, we will appreciatively take up the ministry of God’s Word to the full extent of our ability. At all events, whether we are able to devote our full time or only part of it directly in the interests of God’s now established kingdom, let us incessantly ‘glorify our ministry.’ W 3/15 18, 19a

## Saturday, May 1

_When you are entering into the house, greet the household._—Matt. 10:12.

The “good news” could save lives, and so the early Christians would want to reach as many persons as possible. At times Paul addressed Jews in synagogues. But how could he and other Christians reach the majority of non-Jews? Christians could speak to persons in public places. Yet it is unlikely that doing just that would reach virtually all persons. What about going to the homes of persons, as Jesus’ disciples did when he sent them out to preach in various cities? The early Christians used this method, too, as they enthusiastically ‘engaged in the holy work’ of spreading Christianity to all peoples. We can see this from comments Paul made to the elders in the Ephesus congregation. Speaking about his past preaching to them, Paul said: “I did not neglect to preach to you about those things which were good for your souls, and I taught in the streets and from house to house.”—Acts 20:20, _Lamsa_. W 1/1 8-10a

## Sunday, May 2

_Through one man sin entered into the world and death through sin._—Rom. 5:12.

When God created the first man, Adam, he placed him in the beautiful paradise of Eden. What pleasure Adam must have found in the abundance of God’s loving provisions around him! Later, God formed Eve and brought her to Adam. Together they could look forward to carrying out God’s will in beautifying and populating the earth, and having loving dominion over the animal creation. (Gen. 1:26-28)

However, sad to say, that future was not to be. A rebellious spirit creature selfishly took himself out from under God’s love, making himself into Satan the Devil. This Devil persuaded Eve, and through her, Adam, to ‘do their own thing.’ But in doing this, they showed themselves completely unworthy of their Creator’s love. So God rightly passed the sentence of death on those willful sinners. But lovingly God provided a basis for hope. It is our privilege to share this hope with others.—Gen. 3:15; Rom. 8:20, 21. W 1/15 4, 5

## Monday, May 3

_Make me know your own ways, O Jehovah; teach me your own paths. Make me walk in your truth and teach me, for you are my God of salvation._—Ps. 25:4, 5.

Applying divine counsel is vital, for God’s Word indicates that, in part, he will judge us on the basis of how we conduct ourselves toward others. (Matt. 18:35; 25:40, 45; Rev. 2:23) Hence, rather than being guided either by what some human says that we should do, or by our own emotions and what we “feel” is proper, we should have David’s attitude. All humans “have sinned and fall short of the glory of God.” (Rom. 3:23; 5:12) Our situation is not hopeless, though, for “Christ Jesus came into the world to save sinners.” Everyone who recognizes that and who exercises faith in Christ can gain forgiveness even though he has lived sinfully in the past. But what if he afterwards stumbles into grave sin? Often the devoted elders can provide help. With what objective? That of restoring the erring Christian spiritually.—Gal. 6:1. W 9/15 3-5

## Tuesday, May 4

_“Keep yourselves in expectation of me,” is the utterance of Jehovah, “till the day of my rising up to the booty, for my judicial decision is to gather nations, . . . for by the fire of my zeal all the earth will be devoured.”—Zeph. 3:8._

So the execution of Jehovah’s judgment does not stop with the desolating of Christendom, whose religion must perish along with the entire world empire of false religion, described in the Bible as “Babylon the Great.” (Rev. 18:2-4) The day of his “burning anger” and of “the fire of \[his] zeal” will remove all wickedness from the earth. (See also Isaiah 34:2-8; Jeremiah 25:32, 33.) How grateful we should be that this greatest tribulation of all time will be the last, according to Jesus’ own testimony at Matthew 24:21! It will have accomplished Jehovah’s purpose in “causing an outright extermination,” like that of ancient Nineveh. “Distress will not rise up a second time.” (Nah. 1:9; Dan. 12:1; Rev. 19:11-21) But some will survive.—Zeph. 3:9. W 8/15 9a

## Wednesday, May 5

_A wise person will listen._—Prov. 1:5.

Married folk will show themselves wise if they take pains to listen carefully to each other. Doing so you may detect that you have offended your loved one. If so, do not ignore it, but humbly, kindly and tactfully bring the matter up in the spirit of Matthew 5:23, 24. Thus peace, harmony and happiness may be restored. Yes, at all times couples should respectfully listen when the other is speaking.

Well has it been said that listening is an art. We want to listen not only to understand the meaning of the words but also to note the feeling with which they are said. To be good listeners we must also take note of what is not said. Yes, husbands and wives should be good listeners. It is unkind to pay little attention when one’s mate is talking. True, at times there may be a little difficulty because the one speaking might be just thinking out loud. If so, then preface your remarks by some direct address, such as “Dearie,” “John,” “Mary.” W 7/1 11, 12

## Thursday, May 6

_The head of every man is the Christ; in turn the head of a woman is the man; in turn the head of the Christ is God._—1 Cor. 11:3.

Another help in our efforts to conduct ourselves as lesser ones is for us to appreciate the principle of headship. Whenever there is work to be done that requires more than one person there needs to be one to take the lead and make final decisions or else there is confusion, a working at cross-purposes. In other words, there is need of organization. An organization of many persons can be likened to the human body. What many and varied members our bodies have, and yet they all are directed by the one head! Some members may well be more prominent, more important than others, but no one member can say to the other, “I have no need of you.” All are necessary. So, should we not be content with having a share in fulfilling the purpose of the organization? Yes, appreciating the principle of headship will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. W 6/1 4, 5a

### Friday, May 7

_If anyone is not obedient to our word through this letter, keep this one marked, stop associating with him, that he may become ashamed.—2 Thess. 3:14._

What if there is someone who is significantly deviating from God's principles, perhaps being grossly lazy or critical, a 'profitless talker' who is a constant 'meddler' with what does not concern him? (2 Thess. 3:11) Or, the problem may be one of scheming to take material advantage of others, indulging in entertainment that clearly is improper, or getting involved in questionable conduct that does not at this point merit judicial action. The elders have tried to help him, but he persists. They can discuss the matter and may assign one of their number to give a firm, direct Scriptural talk on the matter to the congregation. Without mentioning the disorderly one by name, the elders may thus be able to shut the mouth of such an unruly one. Others in the congregation can apply the counsel above. W 9/1 18

### Saturday, May 8

_When the Son of man arrives in his glory, and all the angels with him, . . . he will separate people one from another, just as a shepherd separates the sheep from the goats.—Matt. 25:31, 32._

As the harvesting of the anointed Christians draws to a close, the ingathering of the 'sheep' continues. In God's due time the remaining ones of the 'wheat' class will finish their earthly course and join the 'Son of man' as part of his heavenly kingdom. They will 'receive the kingdom' with the rest of

the 144,000 'holy ones.' (Dan. 7:18, 22, 27) As for the 'great crowd' of 'other sheep' that are now being gathered, they will survive the 'great tribulation' that will mark the end of the present satanic system of things and will become a part of the 'peoples, national groups and languages' that will serve the 'Son of man' on earth, under his 'indefinitely lasting rulership,' or heavenly kingdom. What a privilege is theirs to make these things known at the present time!—Rev. 7:4, 9, 10, 14; Dan. 7:13, 14. W 8/1 19a

### Sunday, May 9

_I take pride in my ministry.—Rom. 11:13, Weymouth._

Till Babylon the Great has been destroyed, what are we going to do with our 'ministry'? Keeping in mind what is coming according to Bible prophecy, we will wisely determine to do what that fine example, the apostle Paul, did. In the above text, as an international legate from Jehovah God, the apostle Paul described what he did about it. From such translations as this one, we note that Paul's _di-a-ko-ni'a_ is not lowered to the level of a mere 'service.' Paul uses in connection with his 'ministry' the Greek word _doz-a'zo_, the root word of which is the Greek term _do'ra_, meaning 'glory.' Certainly there was nothing about Paul's _di-a-ko-ni'a_ of which to be ashamed. Of course, one's Christian 'ministry' is nothing over which to get a swelled head. Rather, we should feel most humble, because God has so favored his dedicated, baptized worshippers by taking them into such a ministry under the now reigning King, Jesus Christ. W 3/15 16-18

### Monday, May 10

_You have your company of young men just like dewdrops.—Ps. 110:3._

Many such young men and women are performing 'sacred service' at the Brooklyn headquarters of Jehovah's Witnesses and in the 96 branches located throughout the earth. There, they willingly perform various tasks directly connected with the preparation and the shipping of the symbolic 'horses'—publications containing judgment messages related to the 'day of vengeance on the part of our God' and setting forth comforting truths about 'the year of goodwill on the part of Jehovah.' (Isa. 61:1, 2) All these willing ones not only share in preparing the 'horses' for use in the field, but also take part in the actual field service, making extensive use of such 'horses,' particularly the magazines. Other young Christians serve as pioneers, by devoting at least 1,000 hours a year to the public witnessing work. The zeal of all these young Christians is highly regarded by Christ and his anointed 'brothers.' W 3/1 17, 18a

### Tuesday, May 11

_They went out from us, but they were not of our sort; for if they had been of our sort, they would have remained with us. But they went out that it might be shown up that not all are of our sort.—1 John 2:19._

A person might renounce his place in the congregation by his actions, such as by becoming part of an organization whose objective is contrary to the Bible, and, hence, is under judgment by Jehovah God. (Compare Rev-

Rev- elation 19:17-21; Isaiah 2:4.) So if one who was a Christian chose to join those who are disapproved of God, it would be fitting for the congregation to acknowledge by a brief announcement that he had disassociated himself and is no longer one of Jehovah's Witnesses. Persons who make themselves 'not of our sort' by deliberately rejecting the faith and beliefs of Jehovah's Witnesses should appropriately be viewed and treated as are those who have been disfellowshipped for wrongdoing. We would not want to have spiritual fellowship with any such. W 9/15 15-17a

### Wednesday, May 12

_Critical times hard to deal with will be here. For men will be . . . lovers of pleasures rather than lovers of God.—2 Tim. 3:1, 2, 4._

Actually, the adulterer might be said to become a hedonist. A hedonist is one who lives primarily for selfish gratification. One who smokes tobacco, although knowing full well how harmful it is, can be said to be a hedonist. (Luke 8:14) Thus those who put the pleasures that a divorce and remarriage to another can bring ahead of their obligation to be pleasing to God can be said to be hedonists. They truly are 'lovers of pleasures rather than lovers of God.' In addition to sinning against God and against one's marriage mate, the divorcing adulterer also sins against the congregation with which he is associated, for his course besmirches its good name. He also sins against the individuals in the congregation by his bad example, causing others at times to stumble. With good reason Paul warns against stumbling others.—Phil. 1:9, 10. W 7/1 10, 11a

## Thursday, May 13

_I love the Father.—John 14:31._

Will affection and unselfish love aid us in conducting ourselves as lesser ones? Yes, and these above all! What a fine example we have of this in Jesus Christ! Because of his love for his Father, he never for one moment gave any thought to being equal to his Father, but always appreciated that Jehovah God was his head. (1 Cor. 11:3) Jesus' example of humility and love should certainly help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. And so should the fine example we have in Jonathan, the son of King Saul. We read that, right after David had slain Goliath, 'Jonathan's very soul became bound up with the soul of David, and Jonathan began to love him as his own soul.' (1 Sam. 18:1) As time went on, Jonathan began to realize that David was Jehovah's choice to succeed Saul as king in Israel. But because of his love for David, far from envying David, he was content to be 'second to him in the kingdom.' —1 Sam. 23:17. W 6/1 13, 14a

## Friday, May 14

_Then I shall give to peoples the change to a pure language. —Zeph. 3:9._

What, then, is this change to a 'pure language'? It is a turning to the message of truth, the refreshing 'good news,' the 'pattern of healthful words,' that speaks in praise to Jehovah and his righteous purposes by Christ Jesus. (2 Tim. 1:13) This 'pure language' unifies them. And it testifies that, in the epoch-marking year 1914, 'the kingdom of the world did become the kingdom of our Lord and of his

Christ.' (Rev. 11:15) Taking this message upon their lips, true Christians worldwide 'call upon the name of Jehovah' in dedication, harmoniously rendering him 'sacred service.' As the only truly united people on earth, they go to the homes of the people, heralding forth the 'good news' of God's established kingdom. Are we among those who have separated themselves from the world, to serve God zealously in dedication to Him? If so, we can hope to 'get away safe.' —Joel 2:32. W 8/15 11a

## Saturday, May 15

_You will bear witness for me . . . away to the ends of the earth. —Acts 1:8, The New English Bible._

Jesus' parting commission truly went far. And there is evidence that Christ's name was witnessed to among the Jews of the Diaspora at an early date, even before 36 C.E.—the year Peter again used his Christ-given prerogative, this time to open up the Kingdom to the uncircumcised Gentiles. For one thing, the 3,000 Jews and proselytes who became baptized Christians on the day of Pentecost 33 C.E. were not inhabitants only of Jerusalem and Judea. Many of them had come from such far-flung places as Parthia, Media, Elam and Mesopotamia, North Africa and Italy. Upon returning to their homelands on the three continents of Asia, Africa and Europe, these newly converted Christians undoubtedly witnessed to Christ's name, at least to other Jews and proselytes. Thus seeds for future expansion were sown right there at Pentecost. It is your privilege to follow their example today. Are you? W 3/1 12

## Sunday, May 16

_The spirit of the Sovereign Lord Jehovah is upon me, for the reason that Jehovah has anointed me . . . to proclaim the year of goodwill on the part of Jehovah. —Isa. 61:1, 2._

Our enemies would like to silence our voice of warning. They view the warning as subversive. But all in vain! The warning has been dinned into their ears, especially since 1919, the year when the world began trying to recover from the wounds inflicted upon it by World War I. The period from then on has been part of 'the year of goodwill on the part of Jehovah.' This means that his wrath has not yet been poured out upon the God-defying system of things. What purpose has this served? It has allowed for Jesus' prophecy at Matthew 24:14 to be carried out till now. This symbolic 'year' is a limited time. All present-day indications are that it is about to end. Its ending will mean the time for a change from Jehovah's 'goodwill' to his wrath. This makes it urgent for us to sound the warning. W 2/1 14-16a

## Monday, May 17

_Stop judging that you may not be judged; for with what judgment you are judging, you will be judged . . . Why, then, do you look at the straw in your brother's eye, but do not consider the rafter in your own eye? —Matt. 7:1-3._

We need to exercise great care in applying the divine counsel given at 2 Thessalonians 3:14, 15. In our imperfection, we might tend to make personal judgments based on individual likes or dislikes, such as about styles of dress or grooming. But if a sis-

sister's clothing, for example, is not immodest, indecent, or shocking to the brothers or the community in general, we should recognize that she simply has a different taste or preference. (Gen. 37:3, 4; John 19:23; 1 Tim. 2:9, 10) We have not been made judges of our brothers and sisters on inconsequential matters of opinion, taste or variations of conscience. Or, even if someone is incorrect on what is really a minor point, we must recall Jesus' advice above. —Rom. 14:4, 10-12. W 9/1 17

## Tuesday, May 18

_More than all else that is to be guarded, safeguard your heart, for out of it are the sources of life. —Prov. 4:23._

The Bible makes clear how Jehovah God and Jesus Christ feel about divorcing. (Mal. 2:15, 16; Matt. 19:3-9) They hate it! But how do you feel about it? What is your view of the sacred institution of marriage? Do you have God's view of it, or have you allowed yourself to be influenced, as the world in general has, by that malicious marriage wrecker, Satan the Devil? Do you tend to condone the behavior of those who have violated God's laws regarding marriage and divorce? Are you among those who, by flirting with persons of the opposite sex, are toying with sexual immorality? Concerning these Jesus said: 'Everyone that keeps on looking at a woman so as to have a passion for her has already committed adultery with her in his heart.' (Matt. 5:28) Are you guarding your heart in these matters, or are you prone to indulge in pleasurable illicit fantasies? W 7/1 12a

### Wednesday, May 19

_It is better to listen to a wise man's rebuke than to the praise of fools.—Eccl. 7:5, The New English Bible._

To hear and to heed the wise rebukes found in the pages of the Bible will enable us to make a good name with Jehovah God. To fritter away our time listening to foolish flattery is vanity. 'For as the crackling of thorns under a pot, so is the laughter of the fool: this also is vanity.' (Eccl. 7:6, AV) To use thorns as fuel under a cooking pot accomplishes nothing. The flame shoots up with noisy crackling, but soon dies down as the thorns turn to ashes. There is not enough substance to the thorns to make a fire last until the meat is cooked. The noisy display is as useless as the stupid one's laughter. We are not living in normal times. We are in the 'last days.' The death of a system of things draws near. Now is the time, while we have life, before the day of our death, to make a good name with God by heeding the words found at Ephesians 5:15-17. W 2/15 7, 16, 17

### Thursday, May 20

_Happy are those hearing the word of God!—Luke 11:28._

Once a woman who heard Jesus teach called out: 'Happy is the womb that carried you and the breasts that you sucked!' (Luke 11:27) But Jesus knew that there was a greater happiness than that of even maternity of the Messiah, and so he replied as above. Observe the emphasis Jesus put on being involved with—hearing or reading—'the word of God.' We are repeatedly assured in the Bible of the profit of being occupied with

God's Word, an example being Psalm 1:1, 2. So we can ask, 'Am I finding happiness from that source?' The Scriptures are like a storehouse of happiness, for they enrich the mind and the heart. They enliven the spirit. They give hope. They point the way to a productive, pleasant life. So, in the interest of happiness we can ask ourselves: 'Can I adjust my reading habits in order to read and enjoy God's Word more?' Indeed, read it for sheer pleasure. Read some of it each day. Yes, more reading of God's Word is a step toward happiness. W 4/1 13-16

### Friday, May 21

_Remove the wicked man from among yourselves.—I Cor. 5:13._

Sometimes a person who has walked for some years in the way of true Christianity deviates from it, gives himself over to ungodly conduct and then is not repentant despite the sincere efforts of the elders to help him. The Bible shows that this happened in the first century, and it happens today. What then must be done? In his perfect wisdom and justice, God directs that a firm step must be taken to protect the moral and spiritual cleanness of the congregation, even as noted above. Such a step might also shock the wrongdoer to his senses. That means expelling him from the Christian congregation. It is sad that a person's conduct and attitude would require such action, but once he has been expelled, how are the loyal members of the congregation to view and treat him? Jehovah God's Word counsels us that we are to treat him as 'a man of the nations and as a tax collector.'—Matt. 18:17. W 9/15 6, 7, 12

### Saturday, May 22

_Gather yourselves together, . . . before there comes upon you people the burning anger of Jehovah.—Zeph. 2:1, 2._

Any gathering that Christendom does is only to destruction at Armageddon. However, today another gathering has been proceeding. First, there has been a gathering, principally _out of_ Christendom, of true anointed Christians. This has been followed by a gathering '_out of_ all nations and tribes and peoples and tongues,' of an innumerable 'great crowd,' for survival through the impending tribulation. (Rev. 7:9, 14) Who make up this 'great crowd'? They are the meek individuals who are willing to humble themselves in heeding the counsel of Jehovah God's prophet. Relentlessly, the countdown to Armageddon is ticking away. Timely, then, is the warning of God's prophet to these meek persons. Christendom must perish, for she is bloodguilty, and her clergy have abandoned the law of Jehovah. So we must sound the warning, 'Get out of her.'—Rev. 18:4. W 8/15 14-17

### Sunday, May 23

_They will be priests of God and of the Christ, and will rule as kings with him for the thousand years.—Rev. 20:6._

Most of the well-established churches of Christendom never mention the millennial hope to churchgoers. They speak disdainfully of that hope as 'millennialism,' and of those who share it as 'millenarians.' But we are not ashamed of this belief, for irrefutable historical facts show that the millennial hope was shared by the early

Christians. Thus we are told that 'in Western Christendom millennialism was very active in Judeo-Christianity during the first three centuries. . . . Millennialism was very deep-rooted during the first centuries of Christianity.' There is evidence that the hopes connected with the 1,000-year reign of Christ were shared by Christians even before John received the Revelation at the end of the first century C.E. By reading the prophets, they had received foregleams of the wonderful millennial hope. It is our privilege to tell others about that hope. W 4/15 1-3

### Monday, May 24

_If you have bitter jealousy and contentiousness in your hearts, do not be bragging and lying against the truth. This is not the wisdom that comes down from above, but is the earthly, animal, demonic.—Jas. 3:14, 15._

Because many were unwilling to conduct themselves as lesser ones due to human imperfection, the disciple James found it necessary to write these words. He further stated: 'For where jealousy and contentiousness are, there disorder and every vile thing are.' (Jas. 3:16) And James is so right in speaking of jealousy and contentiousness as being earthly, animal, demonic! It is earthly, materialistic, the mark of selfish, imperfect humans and stands in contrast with that which is from above, heavenly. This same spirit is also animal, for it is the mark of animals. Ethologists, who study animals in their natural environment, have discovered among various animals, such as cows and chickens, what is known as the 'pecking order,' wherein certain ones always come first. W 6/1 13, 14

[Open Page 158 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=158)

## Tuesday, May 25

_May the God who gives hope fill you with all joy and peace by your believing, that you may abound in hope with power of holy spirit._—Rom. 15:13.

Hope for all the people of the nations that have died since the invasion of death-dealing sin among mankind rests upon the now ruling heavenly King, Jesus Christ. Already since he was enthroned at the end of the Gentile Times in 1914, he governs believing, hopeful persons of all the nations. He will yet rule over many, many more after resurrecting all the ransomed ones of humankind. Is there reason, then, for persons of all nations to 'be glad' with Jehovah's people? (Rom. 15:10) Yes, all the reason in the world! How fitting here is the lovely benediction with which Paul closes his quotations from prophecy, even as noted above. Beyond all gainsaying that inspired prayer is being fulfilled upon all the Christian witnesses of Jehovah in all quarters of the globe. The coming true of Bible prophecies indicates that our all-glorious hope nears realization. 'Be glad'! W 6/15 18, 19a

## Wednesday, May 26

_The One correcting the nations, can he not reprove, even the One teaching men knowledge? Happy is the able-bodied man whom you correct, O Jah, and whom you teach out of your own law._—Ps. 94:10, 12.

When was the last time we, in word or in deed, fell short of God's righteous ways and standards? Likely we do not have to think back very far, perhaps just a few hours or days, for all of us sin, failing to reflect properly God's glory. We can be happy

that in his love Jehovah desires and is able to correct us, even as the psalmist notes. If we 'let ourselves be corrected' by Jehovah, we will be happy, for we will be in harmony with him. Christians can trust the Bible's assurance, 'Whom Jehovah loves he disciplines.' (Heb. 12:6) Obtaining a clear understanding of the Scriptural principles involved in matters relating to reproof, repentance and disfellowshipping will enable us 'to be readjusted, to be comforted, to think in agreement, to live peaceably.'—2 Cor. 13:11. W 9/1 1-3

## Thursday, May 27

_I shall put enmity between you and the woman and between your seed and her seed. He will bruise you in the head and you will bruise him in the heel._—Gen. 3:15.

Do we need to guess at the identity of this symbolic serpent? No! At Revelation 12:9 it speaks of that mystery serpent as 'the great dragon,' 'the original serpent, the one called Devil and Satan.' He was originally the symbolic serpent. Since Eve, to whom Satan the Devil lied by means of the serpent, was not the 'woman' referred to in Genesis 3:15, who is she? Again the book of Revelation helps us to a correct understanding. Chapter 12 describes a confrontation of Satan the Devil and a 'woman' who, from head to foot, is clothed with light by the sun, moon and stars. She gives birth to a child, which must picture God's kingdom in view of all that is said about it. As the 'child' is not an individual person, neither is the 'woman.' Since the 'woman' was made pregnant by God as her husband, she must picture God's spiritual organization. W 5/1 15, 16, 19a

## Friday, May 28

_There are varieties of ministries, and yet there is the same Lord._—1 Cor. 12:5.

One's being Scripturally a minister as a part of the dedicated, baptized congregation of Jehovah's Witnesses does not mean living a life in luxury and at ease. The living arrangements for the professional ministers of the religious systems of Christendom may lead many to think so. But according to the inspired Bible this should not be so. Yet, those who are 'ministers' according to the Bible meaning of the word may be workers at secular employment for part of the time. Why, even Jesus Christ was a carpenter at Nazareth until 30 years of age! Then he devoted his full time to the ministry for which he had been anointed with God's spirit. Whether he spoke the Latin language and used its term 'minister,' we do not know. But when the Hebrew-Greek Scriptures were translated into the Latin of the Roman Empire, then the word _minister_ appeared in that translation, even as it appears in English translations today. W 3/15 5a

## Saturday, May 29

_My kingdom is no part of this world._—John 18:36.

Emphasis on the issue of Kingdom government finally was highlighted by Pilate's action in having placed on Jesus' torture stake a title written in Hebrew, Latin and Greek. All present that day could read it and would not be left in doubt as to why the impalement took place. This title said: 'Jesus the Nazarene the King of the Jews.' (John 19:19, 20) The events of that day should

make very evident to all Christians what their lives should be centered on today. We should examine just how real that kingdom is to each one of us. Do we see ourselves as prospective subjects of that government? What efforts are we making as advocates of Kingdom rule? How zealous is our activity in support of that government? The Scriptures give us good reason for intense interest in the issue of the Kingdom, and there is need for a sense of urgency in showing that interest. 'May the Lord be with the spirit we show' in upholding the Kingdom!—2 Tim. 4:22. W 5/15 17, 18

## Sunday, May 30

_Let him that has ears listen._—Matt. 13:43.

How does the parable of the wheat and the weeds affect those who have an earthly hope? True, this parable illustrates the gathering in of the total number required to form Christ's heavenly government. The same chapter (14) of Revelation that speaks of the 'harvest of the earth' tells that the 144,000 Kingdom heirs are the 'firstfruits to God and to the Lamb.' (Rev. 14:1-4) The harvesting of these would indicate that there was other fruitage to be gathered later as symbolized by the ingathering of other fruits of the field at the close of the agricultural year. So the ingathering goes on, and it does affect all those of the 'other sheep.' Because the faithful anointed Christians have let their light shine, many others with listening ears have allowed themselves to be separated from the 'weeds,' or counterfeit Christians within the churches of Christendom. This separating work was also foretold by Jesus.—Matt. 25:31-46. W 8/1 17, 18a

### Monday, May 31

_I am writing you . . . that you may know how you ought to conduct yourself in God's household._
—1 Tim. 3:14, 15.

Among the benefits that Christians have in studying the Bible is that they learn the godly way of dealing with other humans. Even persons unacquainted with Christianity or the Bible admit the wisdom and practicality of counsel such as: "Just as you want men to do to you, do the same way to them." (Luke 6:31) The Bible provides much more than broad general-

### Tuesday, June 1

_Who really is the faithful steward, the discreet one?_—Luke 12:42.

In the parable the "steward" was appointed over his master's "body of attendants" in order "to keep giving them their measure of food supplies at the proper time." Through the composite "steward" class this purpose has been fulfilled down till now. In the first century C.E. that was the case, during the days of the 12 apostles of Jesus Christ down to the death of the last surviving apostle, John. Now, during this "conclusion of the system of things" that began in 1914, it would especially be the "proper time" for the due "measure of food supplies" to be distributed to the "body of attendants," and it has proved to be so. The remnant of that "steward" class has proved faithful in that respect. (Matt. 24:3-14) This coincides with the fact that Christ entered into the Kingdom power with his Father in 1914 and thus finally got what he went away for, and so now could return for an accounting with his "steward." _W 10/1_ 12, 13

ities about our conduct toward others. It counsels on how we should conduct ourselves toward individuals who are in certain specific relationships to us, or in certain situations. For example, it gives advice as to a Christian wife's conduct toward her unbelieving husband, and counsel on our conduct toward lowly ones, the crippled and the opposite sex. The Scriptures also offer much sound counsel on how we should conduct ourselves in God's household, which is the congregation.—1 Pet. 3:1-6; Ps. 41:1; Lev. 19:14; 1 Tim. 5:1, 2. _W 9/15_ 1, 2

### Wednesday, June 2

_In like manner, you younger men, be in subjection to the older men. But all of you gird yourselves with lowliness of mind toward one another, because God . . . gives undeserved kindness to the humble ones._—1 Pet. 5:5.

A basic need for a Christian family that desires to be pleasing to Jehovah is to be obedient to properly constituted authority. What is your view of authority? The media provides abundant evidence that worldwide there is a defiant attitude toward authority. This attitude can easily influence a Christian's thinking. On the level of the family it can cause great disturbance if a person's view of obedience to authority is shaped by events happening around him day by day. If you are a husband and father, how do you view your authority in the family? Is it your feeling that the head of the family should have absolute say in matters related to the family, and that it is your right to rule the household? Such would not be in keeping with Peter's counsel above. _W 10/15_ 1, 2

### Thursday, June 3

_It is beauty on \[our] part to pass over transgression._—Prov. 19:11.

We should guard against being overcritical of another's minor faults, for God urges us to be long-suffering and to put up with one another. (Col. 3:12, 13) We should have 'lowliness of mind, considering that the others are superior to us, keeping an eye, not in personal interest upon just our own matters, but also in personal interest upon those of the others.' (Phil. 2:3, 4) God assures us as above. Still there are failings or errors not of a very grave nature concerning which mature Christians might be in a position to offer Bible-based correction and help. There may be some occasion when we see that a Christian associate is taking a false step or is heading into danger because of a bad pattern. For instance, in our close association with a brother we may note that he is inclined to heavy drinking. So a Christian with spiritual qualifications might kindly and tactfully provide correction. _W 9/1_ 8-10

### Friday, June 4

_Joyfully cry out, O daughter of Zion! . . . The king of Israel, Jehovah, is in the midst of you. You will fear calamity no more._
—Zeph. 3:14, 15.

Since 1919, when they were released from spiritual captivity to Babylon the Great, the witnesses of Jehovah have served him shoulder to shoulder in giving joyful public testimony. Jehovah has judged and approved these children of the heavenly Jerusalem. Prisons and slave labor camps have proved powerless to draw them again into spiri-

tual bondage. Their rejoicing is now shared by a "great crowd" of companion Witnesses, identified since 1935 as part of the Lord's "other sheep." Directly to his anointed ones, representing the heavenly Zion, and through them to their fellow workers of the "great crowd," Jehovah himself now makes the appeal, saying: "Do not be afraid, O Zion. May your hands not drop down." (Zeph. 3:16) As terrible as crime and violence are today, our very intimacy with Jehovah and our trust in him will carry us through. _W 8/15_ 16, 17a

### Saturday, June 5

_Therefore I have cause for exulting in Christ Jesus when it comes to things pertaining to God._—Rom. 15:17.

True, we will have problems due to our imperfections and this present wicked system. But as we become more occupied in the God-ordained work of the "good news" our lives will become fuller, richer, happier. (Acts 20:35) This is not mere theory. It works. It did with Paul. It does with millions of our brothers today. Others, too, will be able to notice the sort of Christians we are. They will observe our sharing in the work of proclaiming the "good news." They will see the peace and joy this and other Christian activities bring into our lives. They will note the many ways in which we reflect the fruitage of the spirit. Yes, we will manifest to many others that we are successfully serving a holy God. After Paul's comment about "engaging in the holy work of the good news," he added the above words. Let all of us as Christians so work that we, too, can thus exult in Christ Jesus. _W 1/1_ 22-24a

# Sunday, June 6

Be glad, you nations, with his people, for he will avenge the blood of his servants, and he will pay back vengeance to his adversaries and will indeed make atonement for the ground of his people.—Deut. 32:43.

Do we appreciate why the 'God of love' must be also a God of vengeance? It is because he loves righteousness and hates wickedness, and so should we. (Ps. 145:17-21) How necessary it is that Jehovah's great name and purposes be vindicated! How necessary that our earth be cleansed of all the bloodguilt and corruption that have sullied this beautiful creation of God over the past millenniums! Only then will the earth become the global paradise that God has purposed for mankind. So, first, the matchless 'God of love' must clear the way by inflicting vengeance upon his enemies. Are we not glad that his day of vengeance is now at hand? Let us, then, be among those who accept God's invitation at Deuteronomy 32:43. More than that, let us extend that loving invitation to all lovers of truth and righteousness yet in the world. W 1/15 23a

# Monday, June 7

A day in your courtyards is better than a thousand elsewhere.—Ps. 84:10.

Christians related to a disfellowshipped person living outside the home should strive to keep even business dealings to a minimum. There must have been congregations in the first century where many were related. But when someone was disfellowshipped, were all the relatives to carry on as normal as long as they did not discuss Scrip-

tural matters with the disfellowshipped person? No. Otherwise the congregation would not be applying the command at 1 Corinthians 5:13. Great care needs to be taken that a person's situation as a disfellowshipped sinner is neither overlooked nor minimized. Our chief loyalty must be to Jehovah and his theocratic arrangement. We can be sure that when we uphold his standards and prefer association with his organized people, rather than with wrongdoers, we will have his protection and blessing. The psalmist well expresses what our sentiments should be. W 9/15 19-21b

# Tuesday, June 8

Continue putting up with one another and forgiving one another freely if anyone has a cause for complaint against another. Even as Jehovah freely forgave you, so do you also.—Col. 3:13.

For married couples to let God be in their marriage they need to show kindness to each other. One of the very best ways in which marriage partners can show mutual kindness is by heeding the above words of the apostle Paul. (Also see Ephesians 4:32.) And in showing the mercy of forgiveness do not do so begrudgingly, but "he that shows mercy, let him do it with cheerfulness." (Rom. 12:8) Doing so is letting God be in your marriage, because we read that he shows mercy to those who repent, in fact, he 'forgives in a large way.' (Isa. 55:7) If we are forgiving when our mate transgresses, it is easier to expect forgiveness when we ourselves transgress, calling to mind the Golden Rule. (Luke 6:31) Not without good reason has it been said that 'a happy marriage is the union of two good forgivers.' W 7/1 13

# Wednesday, June 9

The angel of Jehovah proceeded to go forth and strike down a hundred and eighty-five thousand in the camp of the Assyrians.—Isa. 37:36.

Back there the battle action on Jehovah's part in behalf of his kingdom at Jerusalem prefigured his still greater act during "the war of the great day of God the Almighty" at Har-Magedon. There his angel of destruction will be an archangel, the installed King, Jesus Christ, in command of all the heavenly angels. (Rev. 16:14-16) The remnant of the "steward" class faces this war of all wars, never to be repeated. The "steward" class, in charge of the "food supplies" from God's Word, has been used, particularly since 1919, to sound out the warning of this coming "war" at what is called Har-Magedon. As in the case of Eliakim during the days of King Hezekiah, they face the approaching attack of the Greater "Assyrian," Satan the Devil, and his hordes. They persist in warning all mankind. In sounding this warning, they are aided by the "great crowd."—Rev. 7:9-17. W 10/1 21, 22a

# Thursday, June 10

Husbands ought to be loving their wives as their own bodies. He who loves his wife loves himself.—Eph. 5:28.

Adam was the first human creation. Because Jehovah is the Almighty God and the Creator, he has the right to say how authority is to be exercised on earth. God determined that authority would flow through the man to the woman and then to their offspring. (1 Cor. 11:3) From the Biblical account it is evident that this authority was

not to be exercised in a dictatorial way. Note the expression of Adam upon seeing for the first time the complement God had created for him: "This is at last bone of my bones and flesh of my flesh." (Gen. 2:23) This beautiful expression does not give any indication that Adam was to be a dictator over an inferior. He said that the woman brought to him was 'bone of his bones and flesh of his flesh.' Many centuries later, the apostle Paul wrote Christians, giving the above directions on how a man is to love his wife and care for her. W 10/15 8

# Friday, June 11

Produce fruit that befits repentance.—Matt. 3:8.

A wrongdoer's sin may have sprung from ignoring Scriptural counsel. He might have regularly shared in recreation with worldly workmates, and this unwholesome association led to immorality. So has the erring person ceased that association? Also, has he put forth effort to meet regularly with God's people, and has he displayed a heartfelt desire to praise God as a pure worshiper? No one of these 'fruits' is the sole criterion for determining that the sinner is repentant. The elders would be concerned that he have wholesome association, attend Christian meetings and be zealous in the field service, for they accept God's view that there should be "works that befit repentance." The elders' responsibility in determining true repentance is a weighty one. They ought to show mercy and aid erring ones who are truly repentant. Yet they also need to guard against misplaced sympathy that could lead to dangerous "leaven" remaining in the congregation. W 9/1 22, 25a

### Saturday, June 12

_Come, you people, and let us go up to the mountain of Jehovah and to the house of the God of Jacob; and he will instruct us about his ways, and we will walk in his paths.—Mic. 4:2._

John describes, at Revelation 7:9-17, a numberless “crowd” drawn from all peoples, and these he saw rendering to God on his throne sacred service in the courtyards of his temple. Obviously, the spiritual temple of Jehovah God is meant. Great was the joy of Jehovah’s Witnesses in 1935 and of persons of goodwill when those of that numberless international “crowd” were identified as being the “sheep” described in Jesus’ parable of the sheep and the goats. (Matt. 25:31-46) Yes, they were identified as being the same as the “other sheep” mentioned in Jesus’ parable of the Fine Shepherd. (John 10:16) The dedicated, baptized “other sheep” are taking part with the spiritual brothers of the King Jesus Christ in preaching “this good news of the kingdom.” (Matt. 24:14) In this way Micah 4:2 began its fulfillment. _W 7/15_ 1-3b

### Sunday, June 13

_Present your bodies a sacrifice living, holy, acceptable to God, a sacred service.—Rom. 12:1._

In the Christian congregation the family is recognized as the basic unit. If there is love, peace and unity in the family circle and an awareness of the importance of performing “sacred service” to Jehovah, the family can be a fine asset to the Christian congregation. The challenge is to build and maintain such a family unit. But there are many

things working against the success of Christian families in our day. For example, a husband and wife often feel it necessary for both to be employed in order to earn enough to support the family. In this situation, families may tend to drift apart. At times, due to the unequal pull of this system of things, the family may be torn by the desire to live beyond its means, rather than to heed the Bible’s counsel to be content with “sustenance and covering.” (1 Tim. 6:8) Such a course is bound to interfere with the family’s “sacred service.” _W 10/15_ 1, 2a

### Monday, June 14

_The inclination of the heart of man is bad from his youth up.—Gen. 8:21._

Why has the unwillingness to conduct oneself as a lesser one been such a prevalent problem throughout man’s history? It is because of inherited selfishness, even as noted above. As a result, “the heart is more treacherous than anything else and is desperate. Who can know it?” (Jer. 17:9) Generally, we have little difficulty in conducting ourselves as lesser ones in relation to those who obviously occupy a superior position. But the problem arises when it comes to conducting ourselves as lesser ones in relation to our peers, among those who are more or less our equals. For example, no doubt not one of the 12 apostles had any difficulty in conducting himself as a lesser one in relation to his Master, Jesus Christ. But when it came to comparing himself with any of the other 11 apostles, that is where the difficulty lay. No one wanted to conduct himself as a lesser one among all the rest! _W 6/1_ 9, 10

### Tuesday, June 15

_Now all the tax collectors and the sinners kept drawing near to him to hear him.—Luke 15:1._

As “the Lamb of God that takes away the sin of the world,” Jesus was a light to all people, though he concentrated on the Jews during his earthly ministry. He was like a physician in aiding all those Jews who needed him most, including sinners such as harlots, drunkards and tax collectors, who often used dishonest means. Matthew Levi, a despised tax collector, was one who responded to the new message of salvation that Jesus brought. Matthew invited Jesus to his home for a feast, thus allowing Matthew and other interested tax collectors to hear more of the wonderful new truths. These were men who had ‘sinned in their ignorance,’ but who were ready to take steps to have their sins “blotted out.” (Acts 3:19; Heb. 9:7) However, from Paul’s words at 1 Corinthians 5:11 we can see that Jesus’ effort to give a witness to tax collectors was not a pattern of how _unrepentant_ sinners were to be treated. _W 9/15_ 17, 18

### Wednesday, June 16

_You are my witnesses, . . . even my servant whom I have chosen.—Isa. 43:10._

Reason, backed up by other Scriptural examples, makes it clear that the “steward” mentioned at Luke 12:42 pictures a class, a collective body, corresponding to a _juristic person_, a legal person like a corporation that is recognized by the law of the land. For instance, Jehovah God called the entire nation of ancient Israel “my servant.” In like manner, the “steward” pic-

pictures the “little flock” of spiritual Israelites, the full body of dedicated, baptized disciples of the “master,” Jesus Christ, who have been begotten by God’s spirit to the heavenly inheritance with their “master,” the glorified Jesus. (Luke 12:32) This composite “steward” was alive and available for being appointed by the departing Master, and a remnant of that “steward” class is on earth today and proving faithful to the Master Jesus Christ. Since each member is faithful and discreet, the whole class is also. The facts show the “steward” is providing the necessary food. _W 10/1_ 11

### Thursday, June 17

_Two are better than one, because they have a good reward for their hard work. . . . And a threefold cord cannot quickly be torn in two.—Eccl. 4:9, 12._

The blessing of marriage is an evidence of God’s impartiality and of his justice. How so? In that its joys, when God is in it, do not depend upon material wealth, a higher education, great physical strength or beauty of form and feature. Neither are marital blessings limited to any race or nationality, nor do they depend upon any climate. A marriage can be likened to a cord tying a couple together and, when God is in it, it is truly a “threefold cord not easily torn in two.” What does it mean to let Jehovah God be in your marriage? Ostensibly, marriage partners are concerned with contributing to each other’s joys and pleasures, to satisfying each other’s needs. To let God be in your marriage means to be concerned with measuring up to his requirements for married couples. _W 7/1_ 4, 5

### Friday, June 18

_Now we are giving you orders . . . to withdraw from every brother walking disorderly and not according to the tradition you received from us.—2 Thess. 3:6._

There was a case in ancient Thessalonica of some Christians who were out of harmony with God's Word. The congregation as a whole was counseled that some, who evidently were healthy and able, would not work. These few wanted to live off others, contrary to God's counsel about laziness. So Paul wrote them as above. Yes, there may be someone who has 'not let himself be corrected by mere words,' including Scriptural counsel from the elders to the congregation, and he persists in going contrary to God's principles. (Prov. 29:19) Christians, then, may feel obliged to 'withdraw' from him. Continuing in the counsel to the Thessalonians about the lazy, disorderly one, Paul counseled that they were to 'mark' him and stop associating with him. Hence Christians can 'mark' a person who persists in disregarding God's principles. _W 9/1 14-16_

### Saturday, June 19

_Who is adequately qualified for these things? We are; for we are not peddlers of the word of God as many men are, but as out of sincerity . . . we are speaking.—2 Cor. 2:16, 17._

The questions are: Are you seizing the opportunity you now have, both as a family and individually, to render 'sacred service' to the Creator? Are you encouraging others to do so? Is your family unit one of those in the congregation that gives evidence of being based on a solid foundation, desiring to produce the fruits of the Kingdom? When

speaking about being a sweet-smelling incense that spreads among those being saved, Paul further said the above. By promoting love, peace and unity in the family circle, by giving primary importance to seeking first the Kingdom, by sitting down and counting the cost, Christian families may find they will indeed be able to increase their share in advancing the most important message ever proclaimed on this earth, the good news of God's established kingdom. _W 10/15 14, 15a_

### Sunday, June 20

_He . . . kept Noah, a preacher of righteousness, safe with seven others.—2 Pet. 2:5._

Noah not only preached but also built a huge ark in confirmation of his verbal message. So Noah was then Jehovah's outstanding witness and watchman. His family of seven members joined Noah in duties of a watchman. Hebrews 11:1-7 tells us that Noah had witness borne to him that God was pleased with him. He was an approved witness of Jehovah God. He was 'a preacher of righteousness.' Today we are faced with an 'act of God' just as global as the flood of Noah's day. Whom do the records show God as using to serve notice of this upon the whole world? Devoted Christians everywhere known as 'Jehovah's Witnesses.' Consequently, Jehovah's slate is clean till now. He has not failed to sound the divine warning by millions of his witnesses! So the responsibility for the loss of human life worldwide during the coming end of the system of things will not rest upon Jehovah. Are we individually doing our part? _W 2/1 2, 3a_

### Monday, June 21

_He emptied himself and took a slave's form and came to be in the likeness of men.—Phil. 2:7._

One who outstandingly conducted himself as a lesser one was Moses. How greatly he was used by Jehovah: in calling forth the 10 plagues, in having the Red Sea divide and in providing his people with water supernaturally! Yet in spite of all these privileges and the prominence accorded him thereby, we read that 'the man Moses was by far the meekest of all the men who were upon the surface of the ground.' Because of his willingness to conduct himself as a lesser one, Jehovah God made him great in the eyes of the world, as well as in the eyes of his own people. (Num. 12:3) And what a fine example Jesus Christ, the Son of God, set in conducting himself as a lesser one! In contrast to the angel who became Satan the Devil, the Word, or Logos, 'although he was existing in God's form, gave no consideration to a seizure, namely, that he should be equal to God.' —Phil. 2:6. _W 6/1 16, 17_

### Tuesday, June 22

_Let him be to you just as a man of the nations.—Matt. 18:17._

The apostles who heard these words of Jesus were Jews and knew that their countrymen did not socialize with Gentiles. The Law distinguished between Jews and Gentiles, serving to keep the Israelites separate from the surrounding nations. (Deut. 7:1-4) Peter's remark to Cornelius (Acts 10:28) shows how deeply Jews felt that there should be no fraternization with 'a man of the nations.' Also, when it be-

became known that Peter had gone to Cornelius, some strongly objected that Peter 'had gone into the house of men that were not circumcised and had eaten with them.' (Acts 11:1-3) Yes, Jews regarded it as a shocking thing to be with and eat with a Gentile. The Scriptures thus help us to understand Jesus' advice to treat an unrepentant wrongdoer who refused to listen to the congregation 'as a man of the nations.' Certainly it would not mean viewing the wrongdoer as an average person of the community. _W 9/15 19-21_

### Wednesday, June 23

_I shall give to peoples the change to a pure language, in order for them all to call upon the name of Jehovah, in order to serve him shoulder to shoulder.—Zeph. 3:9._

The 'change to a pure language' has been hard for some to make. It is not easy to come out of the world, with its easygoing, permissive ways, and to make the sacrifices necessary for 'calling upon the name of Jehovah.' It has required our getting rid of wrong thoughts, bad habits and self-seeking, loose ways of life, in order to conform to the clean, worthwhile standards of God's own people. It has required our giving proper recognition to the one organization that Jehovah is using in the earth today, and this by our associating with the 'faithful and discreet slave.' It has required meekness, in our carefully examining God's Word with the aid of that 'slave's' publications, in order to fortify our faith. In serving Jehovah 'shoulder to shoulder,' we need, as Zephaniah so often emphasizes, to cultivate the quality of meekness. _W 8/15 12, 13a_
