## Thursday, June 24

_May the God who gives hope fill you with all joy and peace by your believing, that you may abound in hope.—Rom. 15:13._

We are already living in a spiritual paradise. Moreover, those of the “great crowd” look forward with confidence to their becoming more spiritual throughout the 1,000-year reign of Christ, when symbolic “scrolls” revealing God’s requirements will be “opened.” (Rev. 20:12) Their hope is very much alive in their minds and hearts, so much so that they are always happy to give others the “reason for the hope that is in them.” (1 Pet. 3:15) Since 1914, world events in fulfillment of Bible prophecy show that we are living in the “time of the end,” and that the unprecedented “time of distress” is near. (Dan. 12:1-4; Matt. 24:3-21) The remnant of the “chosen ones” and the “great crowd” have been promised survival through that “great tribulation.” (Matt. 24:22; Rev. 7:9, 10, 14) Thereafter, their respective millennial hopes will be fulfilled. W 4/15 18, 21, 22a

## Friday, June 25

_Reprove, reprimand, exhort, with all long-suffering and art of teaching.—2 Tim. 4:2._

Even where there are witnesses who establish that wrongdoing has occurred, the judicial committee encourages the guilty person to discuss frankly not only the wrong but what led up to it and how he feels about it. Why is this necessary? Though the elders hearing the case establish the guilt or convince someone of wrongdoing, their primary interest is in helping their Christian brother who has

gone astray. They want to move him to repent so that “refreshing may come from the person of Jehovah.” (Acts 3:19) If he does not admit the wrong, recognize its grievous nature or see the need to repent, they may have to present convincing evidence concerning his sin. But in giving such godly reproof they should not be vindictive or harsh. By firmly, kindly and patiently giving reproof, they may be able to reach the sinner’s heart, helping him to hate the wrong and turn back to God. W 9/1 8, 9a

## Saturday, June 26

_Every day in the temple and from house to house they continued without letup teaching and declaring the good news about the Christ.—Acts 5:42._

Such witnessing is an indispensable part of our “sacred service.” Where do you stand? Have you been a Kingdom publisher who found inward joy in making known the “good news” and also Jehovah’s judgments by means of the symbolic “horses,” particularly the Society’s magazines? If you have slowed down or even stopped altogether, is it because you have lost sight of the spiritual significance of such activities, namely, helping the anointed remnant to fulfill their commission to “proclaim the year of goodwill on the part of Jehovah and the day of vengeance on the part of our God”? (Isa. 61:1, 2, 5) Then it is high time for you to rediscover that joy by again busying yourself in Jehovah’s service. If you have found witnessing from house to house difficult, why not ask one of the elders to put you in touch with an experienced house-to-house publisher? W 3/1 11, 12a

## Sunday, June 27

_For those who are being lost, it is a deadly stench that kills; but for those who are being saved, it is a fragrance that brings life.—2 Cor. 2:16, Today’s English Version._

The nature of the Kingdom message is such that it is not accepted by most of those to whom it is offered. That is because Satan has blinded them. (2 Cor. 4:4) The apostle Paul, at 2 Corinthians 2:14-16, tells about our fulfilling this responsibility and its effects. The problem is that the vast majority of mankind view this all-important message as something deadly, unacceptable to them. To share in spreading this message to such persons can be difficult and disheartening. This makes the role of Christian parents very important in the training of their children to be sharers in this “sacred service.” Such service spreads the “knowledge about Christ,” which is a cause for great joy to a few, but which meets with total rejection by many. Parents should begin training their children early in life to share in this “sacred service.”—2 Cor. 2:14. W 10/15 6, 7a

## Monday, June 28

_He that is walking with wise persons will become wise, but he that is having dealings with the stupid ones will fare badly.—Prov. 13:20._

At Romans 12:9-19 Paul shows the sort of persons we ought to be. It would be frightening if God’s mercy were not available where we in our weakness fall short. Here is a big source of help: associate with persons who have the same goals. “Do not be misled. Bad associations spoil useful habits.” (1 Cor. 15:33)

Shun the world empire of false religion that adulterates God’s Word. It is vital to gain strength by associating with those seeking to make a good name with God, “not forsaking the gathering of ourselves together, as some have the custom.” (Heb. 10:25) As the end of Satan’s wicked system draws near, let us concentrate on “what sort of persons we ought to be,” on making our minds over, on stripping off the old personality and putting on the new, and on making a good name with God by being his faithful witnesses. W 2/15 15-17a

## Tuesday, June 29

_To be sure, it is a means of great gain, this godly devotion along with \[contentment].—1 Tim. 6:6._

Happiness is not principally a life of possessing, but of being and enjoying. It means being satisfied with what we have, appreciating the above words. Happiness is not something that we should primarily pursue. It does not come by our making it an end in itself, but it is a by-product as we live before Jehovah God, displaying his qualities and acting as he wants us to act. It is a fruit of love and of service. When we put happiness out of mind and peacefully carry on according to God’s will, we will realize it. Consequently, it is true that to a large extent it is up to you. Recognize your spiritual need. Follow God’s moral standards. Work at having a close relationship with Jehovah God. Make more time for reading God’s Word. Experience the reward of giving, helping others to know Jehovah and his purposes. Doing so, you will gain the blessing of much happiness.—Ps. 146:5. W 4/1 22-24

<sup>**Page 164**</sup>

[Open Page 164 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=164)

### Wednesday, June 30

_I am writing you to quit mixing in company with anyone called a brother that is a fornicator or a greedy person or an idolater or a reviler or a drunkard or an extortioner, not even eating with such a man.—1 Cor. 5:11._

At 1 Corinthians 5:9, 10 Paul realistically acknowledges that most persons whom we contact in daily affairs have never known or followed God's way. But in the verse above Paul contrasts this situation with how Christians should conduct them-

themselves toward one who had been a Christian 'brother' but who was expelled from the congregation because of wrongdoing. 'Disfellowshipping' is what we appropriately call the expelling and subsequent shunning of such an unrepentant wrongdoer. Their refusal to fellowship with an expelled person on any spiritual or social level reflects loyalty to God's standards and obedience to his command. This is consistent with Jesus' advice that such a person be considered the same as 'a man of the nations.'—Matt. 18:17. W 9/15 9, 10, 12a

### Thursday, July 1

_I, Jehovah, am your God, the One teaching you to benefit yourself.—Isa. 48:17._

Thus our heavenly Father reminded his chosen people. During the teens, there is a 'blooming' of sexual desire. This period, called 'the bloom of youth,' usually brings a strong attraction for the opposite sex. So it is not unusual if a young Christian person is troubled by sexual desires. However, this normal desire is whipped up by today's entertainment and advertising media, which glorify sex. The pressure from other youngsters can also be intense. Additionally, young people feel a need for love and understanding, and this may not be satisfied at home. They cherish a boyfriend or a girl friend who treats them 'special' and who has a sympathetic ear. Such emotional closeness can lead to sexual intimacies. Some youngsters are bewildered by the swarm of all these new emotions. They may wonder, 'Could something so pleasurable be wrong?' But God's will regarding morals is clear—get control of your bodies.—1 Thess. 4:4. W 11/1 3-6

### Friday, July 2

_Who really is the faithful and discreet slave whom his master appointed over his domestics?—Matt. 24:45._

How do we know that this has been the proper time for the remnant of the 'steward' class to be appointed over the enlarged 'belongings' of the Master? In giving his prophecy concerning the 'conclusion of the system of things,' Jesus referred to a 'faithful and discreet slave' who would be at work on the earth when the world system of things neared its end. Jesus' remarks concerning this 'slave' are very much like those concerning the 'faithful steward.' Plainly they apply to the same class. Members of this class served diligently up to the end of the Jewish system of things in 70 C.E. The remaining members of the 'slave' class, the 'steward' class, have been proving themselves faithful and discreet during the modern-day 'conclusion of the system of things.' These are easily identifiable today, for they line up with what Jesus said to the original part of the 'slave' class.—Acts 1:8. W 10/1 16, 18-20

### Saturday, July 3

_Do not be afraid, O Zion. May your hands not drop down. Jehovah your God is in the midst of you. As a mighty One, he will save. He will exult over you with rejoicing. He will become silent in his love. He will be joyful over you with happy cries.—Zeph. 3:16, 17._

As fearsome as the challenges of the nuclear age may become, as cruel as may be the persecutions yet to come upon Jehovah's Witnesses, having put our hands to the plow of 'sacred service,' may we never look back at the things behind and cease plowing forward, 'shoulder to shoulder'! Jehovah is 'silent' in finding ease and refreshment by expressing love to his recovered, restored people, and great is his joy and exultation over their integrity and zeal in his service. In these 'last days' we should feel very close to our living God, Jehovah, as we unitedly apply shoulders to his service. Since 1919, it has been a grand day of restoration for all of God's people. Does our activity reflect that appreciation? W 8/15 17, 18a

### Sunday, July 4

_Those practicing such things are deserving of death.—Rom. 1:32._

The wars fought since 1914 have helped to bring in their wake horrible consequences. Among these are unbridled indulgence in sex, resulting in epidemics of venereal disease and unwanted pregnancies. These unwanted pregnancies have brought with them a tidal wave of abortions. Also, in recent times, we have seen drug addiction, divorce, delinquency and lawlessness of all types on a frightful scale. Tru-

ly, 'wicked men are advancing from bad to worse.' (2 Tim. 3:13) Viewing the immoral world around us, we are compelled to exclaim: How like Noah's day! How like the depraved Canaanites and the backsliding Israelites! Truly this present world is sinking ever deeper into the mire of immorality. But the loving God will not much longer tolerate the horrendous wickedness of this system of things. Instead, he will bring to ruin those who make his earth filthy. It is our privilege to sound out Paul's warning. W 1/15 10-12a

### Monday, July 5

_Sadness in a godly way makes for repentance to salvation that is not to be regretted; but the sadness of the world produces death.—2 Cor. 7:10._

In handling a judicial matter the elders may have to differentiate between the 'sadness of the world' and the 'sadness in a godly way.' A guilty person might feel a sadness of personal failure, shame at being exposed or feel gloomy over the possibility of facing discipline. But such 'sadness of the world' does not mean that he is sad over having sinned against God or having brought reproach on God and His people, which are indications of 'godly sadness.' Though Esau shed tears over the loss of his birthright, Jehovah knew that Esau was not truly repentant at heart. So, if a person guilty of grave sin gives way to tears, the elders must try to determine whether this is out of 'godly sadness.' It may be. In Ezra's day the people wept profusely over their sin, and Peter cried bitterly over having denied Jesus. W 9/1 16a

<sup>**Page 165**</sup>

[Open Page 165 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=165)

### Tuesday, July 6

_Just as you want men to do to you, do the same way to them._
—Luke 6:31.

Jehovah God is just. (Deut. 32:4) For us to let him be in our marriage we must also be just, honest with each other. Basically this means to go by the Golden Rule. Honesty involves many things. Obviously, it involves many matters, which may present a challenge to both husbands and wives. However, it involves far more important things, in particular sex interest. It is easy for a husband to let his desire go wandering, especially in view of all the temptations facing him daily, from both unrighteous persons and the unclean media. Just as Jehovah God requires exclusive devotion from us, so husbands and wives have the right to exclusive devotion as to the sex interest of their mates, and they are obligated to demonstrate such themselves. Proverbs 5:15-20 has very frank counsel for husbands in this regard. Also, wives need to be careful not to use the marital due as a pawn to get what they want.
—1 Cor. 7:3-5. W 7:1 14, 15

### Wednesday, July 7

_Fathers, do not be irritating your children, but go on bringing them up in the discipline and mental-regulating of Jehovah._—Eph. 6:4.

Jehovah purposed that man should be the head of the woman. The woman was to be treated with dignity and love, so that she, in turn, could have “deep respect for her husband” and could willingly “be in subjection” to him. (Eph. 5:33; 1 Pet. 3:1) The two, working together, were to produce offspring, passing along to them by word and example

what was pleasing to Jehovah so that the family arrangement would have his blessing. As God later indicated, in exercising authority over their children the father and the mother are to refrain from irritating their children. Rather, the children are to be raised in the discipline and mental-regulating of Jehovah. They are to be given Christian discipline and instruction. Parents are also called upon to use the rod of correction when necessary, at all times exercising their authority with love and fairness. W 10/15 9

### Thursday, July 8

_O Jehovah, who will . . . reside in your holy mountain? He who is walking faultlessly._—Ps. 15:1, 2.

It is not wrong to be loyal to the righteous and just God of the Bible. He tells us that he will accept ‘in his holy mountain’ only those who walk faultlessly, practice righteousness and speak truth. If, though, a Christian were to throw in his lot with a wrongdoer who has been rejected by God and disfellowshipped, or has disassociated himself, that would be as much as saying ‘I do not want a place in God’s holy mountain either.’ If the elders saw him heading in that direction by regularly keeping company with a disfellowshipped person, they would lovingly and patiently try to help him to regain God’s view. They would admonish him and, if necessary, ‘reprove him with severity.’ They want to help him remain ‘in God’s holy mountain.’ But if he will not cease to fellowship with the expelled person, he thus has made himself ‘a sharer in wicked works’ and must be expelled from the congregation.
—2 John 11. W 9/15 27a

### Friday, July 9

_Now these things went on befalling them as examples, and they were written for a warning to us upon whom the ends of the systems of things have arrived._
—1 Cor. 10:11.

Most astonishing is the fulfillment of Jehovah’s utterance concerning Assyria and Nineveh as recorded at Zephaniah 2:13-15. However, most importantly, that prophetic record carries a message for today. It encourages us to avoid the pride, the indulgent pleasure-seeking, the materialistic thinking and violent dispositions that brought God’s judgment upon those nations. Also, we should remember that those prophecies are very much alive today, and that their climactic fulfillment hastens on. It is not for a mere historical record that Jehovah, the living God, has preserved them down to this day. Those ancient nations have their modern-day counterpart, particularly in the Assyria-like politico-military powers that boast in their armed might. Jehovah will assuredly take vengeance against all such opposers of his kingdom. W 8/15 3, 5a

### Saturday, July 10

_Go therefore and make disciples of people of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the holy spirit, teaching them to observe all the things I have commanded you._—Matt. 28:19, 20.

When we, as imitators of Christ, welcome all seekers of truth into the congregation despite race, color, former religious membership or secular education, this works for God’s glory. (Rom. 15:7) It gives all those thus welcomed a right viewpoint to-

ward Jehovah God. Showing his willingness to receive all true believers into the congregation of which he was the spiritual Head, the resurrected Jesus told his disciples the above. But was not God showing partiality in giving the first opportunity to the Jews? No, for God had to start somewhere, and he made a start with those to whom he had made special promises through their forefathers, namely, the circumcised Jews. However, since the final benefits are not confined to them, there is no just cause for complaint. W 6/15 11, 13

### Sunday, July 11

_By wisdom a household will be built up, and by discernment it will prove firmly established._
—Prov. 24:3.

Many parents ask, ‘What can you do when the young folks want to be together?’ Firmness, along with the providing of activities to keep the child’s mind occupied, is needed today. In this regard parents must use genuine discernment and godly wisdom. It is extremely difficult for parents to restrain a child’s feelings once it becomes emotionally involved. Before allowing their child to date (where this is socially acceptable), Christian parents would have to consider the child’s age, degree of emotional maturity and spiritual progress, who it is the child wishes to date and what their activities will be. Some parents have sat down with the young couple and explained why they cannot approve of their dating. By discussing the matter with the parents of the other child, additional support may be gained. Though old enough to date, they need to be chaperoned. W 11/1 15-18a

## Monday, July 12

_Keep this one marked, stop associating with him, that he may become ashamed.—2 Thess. 3:14._

Should such a situation exist where some are not obedient to the Word, individual Christians might feel obliged to 'mark' the person. Paul explains what this, in part, involves. That would mean your curtailing social involvement with the 'marked' person. You should not announce or publicize your private decision, nor try to influence others. But you personally would avoid the company of the 'marked' person, in keeping with the healthful counsel given by the congregation elders. You would not, though, reject him altogether, for he is still your brother, a fellow Christian for whom Christ died. Rather than allowing any seeds of hate to develop, you should reprove him. How? Well, in addition to being a good example yourself, your kindly but firmly obeying the direction 'Stop associating with him' is one form of correction. Fine counsel along this line is given at Leviticus 19:17 and Titus 2:7, 8. W 9/1 19

## Tuesday, July 13

_God opposes the haughty ones, but he gives undeserved kindness to the humble ones.—1 Pet. 5:5._

It will greatly help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones if we 'trust in Jehovah with all our heart and do not lean on our own understanding. If we in all our ways take notice of him, he will direct our path for our greatest good.' (Prov. 3:5, 6) Then, too, we can have the confidence that he will make all his works cooperate for our own good. (Rom.

8:28) It also takes humility to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. And why should we not be willing to be humble? We are told: 'Better is it to be lowly in spirit with the meek ones than to divide spoil with the self-exalted ones.' The proud course is bound to be self-defeating: 'Pride is before a crash, and a haughty spirit before stumbling.' (Prov. 16:19, 18) More than that, pride causes God to oppose us, even as we read at James 4:6. The apostle Peter makes the same point, even as noted above.—Compare Romans 12:16. W 6/1 2, 3a

## Wednesday, July 14

_Who really is the faithful steward, the discreet one, whom his master will appoint over his body of attendants to keep giving them their measure of food supplies at the proper time?—Luke 12:42._

No one will question that the 'master' of the faithful steward pictures the teller of the parable himself, the Lord Jesus Christ. He has a 'body of attendants,' of which the steward himself is one. This matches the fact that Jesus Christ has a household in a spiritual sense. (Heb. 3:4-6) Jesus Christ, as the Greater Moses, has been over the house of spiritual Israel from the day of Pentecost of 33 C.E. down till now. Revelation 7:1-8 reveals that the 'house' is made up of 144,000 spiritual Israelites. This is a 'house' made up of spiritual sons of God, like their Head Jesus Christ himself. These collectively compose the 'little flock,' to whom, as Jesus said, Jehovah God has approved of giving the celestial kingdom. (Luke 12:32) So the 144,000 spiritual sons of God make up the 'body of attendants' over which Jesus appoints the 'steward.' W 10/1 7-9

## Thursday, July 15

_They went out from us, but they were not of our sort.—1 John 2:19._

A Christian might grow spiritually weak, perhaps because of not studying God's Word regularly, having personal problems or experiencing persecution. Such a one might cease to attend Christian meetings. What is to be done? Recall that the apostles abandoned Jesus on the night of his arrest. Yet Christ had urged Peter, 'When once you have returned, strengthen your brothers.' (Luke 22:32) Hence, out of love Christian elders and others might visit and help the one who has grown weak and inactive. It is another matter, though, when a person repudiates his being a Christian and disassociates himself. One who has been a true Christian might renounce the way of truth, stating that he no longer considers himself to be one of Jehovah's Witnesses. When this event occurs, the person is renouncing his standing as a Christian, deliberately disassociating himself from the congregation. W 9/15 13, 14a

## Friday, July 16

_There exists the one speaking thoughtlessly as with the stabs of a sword, but the tongue of the wise ones is a healing.—Prov. 12:18._

You will make it easier for your child to open up to you if you remind him that you went through the same period, and that you too are imperfect. You can also accomplish this by creating opportunities so that the child can be alone with you at times. Yet a thoughtless word or statement can be devastating. Some speak thoughtlessly,

'as with the stabs of a sword.' Their words hurt and can cause separation. So strive to be cool of spirit as you really listen. Perhaps you can recall when someone talked down to you or made fun of your feelings. Perhaps he said, 'You know better than that!' Did you yearn to confide in that person again? When a child reaches his teens, his sexual desires become very strong. The youngster needs to talk with someone who can explain what is happening to his body and can answer very delicate and personal questions. W 11/1 3-5a

## Saturday, July 17

_Keep on, then, seeking first the kingdom and his righteousness.—Matt. 6:33._

Today the spirit of independence and the desire of each one to do what he wants, rather than working together, are factors that at times separate families instead of bringing them together in unity. Whatever the influence may be, there is a course that, if followed, can be of tremendous assistance to the Christian family. The course is set out in a statement made in the Sermon on the Mount by the Lord Jesus, as noted above. There are positive procedures a family can follow in seeking first the Kingdom, and promoting those things will result in a happy, loving Christian family, one that can effectively render 'sacred service' to God. If something is first in a person's life, then it is primary, of No. 1 importance. Because of our imperfect inclination of mind and heart from birth onward due to inherited sin, we must all be schooled in and taught about what is expected of us in order to please God.—Gen. 8:21; Rom. 5:12. W 10/15 2-4a

# Sunday, July 18

I have other sheep, which are not of this fold; those also I must bring, and they will listen to my voice, and they will become one flock, one shepherd.—John 10:16.

In the spring of 1935 the Fine Shepherd definitely began to bring these "other sheep" with an earthly destiny into recognized association with the spirit-begotten remnant of "this fold." Thus the Fine Shepherd merges the "sheep" of "this fold" with the "other sheep" to form just "one flock" under him as their "one shepherd." So the "flock" becomes one organization. The separation that must eventually come between the two classes will be because the "sheep" of "this fold" will share in "the first resurrection," to be absorbed into the invisible heavenly organization. (Rev. 20:4, 6) Those of the "little flock" being then all gone, readjustments will have to be made among the remaining "other sheep," that these may serve harmoniously as subjects of the Greater David, the Messianic King Jesus Christ. W 5/1 20, 21b

# Monday, July 19

We lie down in our shame, and our humiliation keeps covering us; for it is toward Jehovah our God that we have sinned, . . . and we have not obeyed the voice of Jehovah our God.—Jer. 3:25.

One of the greatest challenges for elders handling cases of wrongdoing is deciding whether the sinner shows true repentance. They must not judge merely on surface appearances. Hence, not being able to read hearts, they need to exercise great discernment, balance and

wisdom in evaluating the wrong, its relation to God's law and what the sinner says and does. The elders ought not to be hasty in concluding that repentance exists. Why? Because if they have misjudged the matter and turned back to the congregation a wrongdoer who was not truly repentant, they would be endangering the moral and spiritual purity of all in the congregation. A person who is truly repentant does not try to minimize or justify his bad course. He recognizes the wrongness of what he did and feels deep regret. W 9/1 13-15a

# Tuesday, July 20

He has sent me . . . to proclaim . . . the day of vengeance on the part of our God.—Isa. 61:1, 2.

All the indications are that the symbolic "year" of Jehovah's "goodwill" is about to end. This makes it urgent to sound the warning that Jehovah's "day of vengeance" is about to break. Our obediently sounding the divine warning will not save this old system of things and its supporters from being destroyed in the "great tribulation." (Matt. 24:21) We are assured that if we turn back wicked persons from their wicked way to righteousness these will be spared from annihilation with this old world. Thus we will be spared from bloodguilt in the case of those who have been warned away from their deadly wickedness. We also have the assurance that by persistently keeping up our warning we will dissuade many righteous persons from going bad and joining the wicked world and thus dooming themselves to perishing with it in the "great tribulation." Thus our service is not to be in vain! W 2/1 15, 16a

# Wednesday, July 21

Let the peace of the Christ control in your hearts . . . And show yourselves thankful.—Col. 3:15.

To show ourselves thankful, to be truly grateful to God, involves more than words of thanks. We need to manifest in our lives that we are grateful to him. Major steps in doing so are the studying of his Word to find out what his will for us today is, and then doing it. Paul's words here should bring to mind also that Jehovah God has called persons together to form a united body of Christians. These are the ones who have recognized that they are sinners, but who appreciate that God's forgiveness is possible on the basis of Jesus' ransom sacrifice. Think what that should mean for you. You can have a clean standing in the eyes of God. (Rev. 7:10, 14) That is one more reason we have to be grateful to God. We can well consider, though, whether our spirit of gratitude is something that relates only to our day-to-day life now. No, our gratefulness involves our future, our hope.—Titus 2:13. W 4/1 11-13a

# Thursday, July 22

Foolishness is tied up with the heart of a boy; the rod of discipline is what will remove it far from him.—Prov. 22:15.

Many parents in instructing their children regarding sex have made good use of the Youth book. Such discussion is not always easy, but genuine love for the child will motivate the parents. So do not let your child suffer untold heartache because of a lack of good instruction in this most delicate area. Yet, despite all the good information that can

be put into a child's heart, foolishness is also deeply ingrained there because of inherited sinfulness. What can drive foolishness out of the young heart? Discipline. Discipline is training that molds or corrects. It is firmness with understanding; so it does not smother or irritate a child with unreasonable restrictions. Discipline is vital when your child forms an interest in the opposite sex. To allow a couple to keep company when either one is too young to marry is to invite disaster.—Eph. 6:4. W 11/1 12-14a

# Friday, July 23

Certainly if, after having escaped from the defilements of the world . . . they get involved again . . . the final conditions have become worse for them than the first.—2 Pet. 2:20.

At one point a man in the Corinthian congregation was practicing immorality. Paul wrote that this man 'should be taken away from their midst.' (1 Cor. 5:1, 2, 6, 11) But, was he, when once expelled, to be treated as if he were just an average person of the world? Not according to Paul's words. The expelled person is not a mere man of the world who has not known God nor pursued a godly way of life. Rather, he has known the way of truth and righteousness, but he has left that way and unrepentantly pursued sin to the point of having to be expelled. So he is to be treated differently. Peter at 2 Peter 2:20-22 comments on how such former Christians differ from an average "man on the street." Yes, the Bible commands us not to keep company with a person who has been disfellowshipped. W 9/15 8, 10-12a

<sup>**Page 168**</sup>

[Open Page 168 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=168)

## Saturday, July 24

_You will receive power when the holy spirit arrives upon you, and you will be witnesses of me . . . to the most distant part of the earth.—Acts 1:8._

In being witnesses of the Lord Jesus Christ those Jewish disciples had to be at the same time witnesses of his God and Father, Jehovah. They never ceased to be Jehovah's witnesses! Those Jewish disciples had to be witnesses of Jehovah, for by birth they belonged to the nation to whose forefathers God had said: 'You are my witnesses, . . . even my servant whom I have chosen . . . So you are my witnesses, . . . and I am God.' (Isa. 43:10-12) Thus those Jewish witnesses of Jehovah were now to become witnesses also of his Son Jesus Christ. On the day of Pentecost, 10 days after his ascension back to heaven, they were baptized with the holy spirit. In this way they were appointed by their glorified Master Jesus Christ to be over his 'body of attendants,' to give them spiritual food, thus becoming the original members of the composite 'steward.' _W 10/1 20, 21_

## Sunday, July 25

_We are not peddlers of the word of God as many men are, but as out of sincerity, yes, as sent from God, under God's view, in company with Christ, we are speaking.—2 Cor. 2:17._

Who today are speaking in sincerity as did Paul? Those who believe the Bible is the inspired Word of God; who take it as a lamp to their feet and a light to their path; who let God and his Word be true though they make every man a liar; who expend themselves in preaching

ing the good news of the Kingdom publicly and from house to house, namely, Jehovah's Witnesses. They use the Bible as their authority, and they encourage their hearers to check up on them, not taking their word for it, but taking only God's Word for it. (Acts 17:11) So, for you to make a good name with Jehovah God, you should aim to be a faithful guide, 'always ready to make a defense before everyone that demands of you a reason for the hope in you, but doing so together with a mild temper and deep respect.'—1 Pet. 3:15. _W 2/15 9, 11a_

## Monday, July 26

_He has sworn to what is bad for himself, and yet he does not alter.—Ps. 15:4._

It may well be that some marriage mates fail in the test of keeping integrity as to their marriage covenant because they did not use good judgment in choosing each other. They may find that they are not very compatible, or they may be disappointed in the more intimate aspects of marriage. In that case, it is a matter of making the best of the situation, thus vindicating Jehovah's arrangement. The Bible speaks approvingly of the one 'who has sworn to what is bad for himself and yet does not change.' It is a matter of taking the bitter with the sweet. Thus an elder who was widely known and very well liked by many of his brothers and sisters had an unbelieving wife who did all she could to make life miserable for him. He said she made a man out of him because he was required to exercise great self-control. He also had the satisfaction of knowing he was heeding Paul's advice.—1 Cor. 7:12-16. _W 7/1 19a_

## Tuesday, July 27

_If your brother does wrong, correct him; if he repents, forgive him.—Luke 17:3, The New American Bible._

Jesus laid the basis for our expecting to receive, and to give, loving correction. In words that cover cases of serious personal offense, but that in principle can be applied in many situations, Jesus said the foregoing. Also, Paul wrote that a 'servant of the Lord' should be 'patiently and gently correcting' others. (2 Tim. 2:24, 25, _NAB_) Overseers are interested in each Christian individually, and also in protecting the flock as a whole. Hence, when someone falls into gross sin, the matter should come to their attention. The sinner himself ought to 'call the older men of the congregation to him.' If he will not, then any other Christian who learns of the grave sin should, out of concern for the wrongdoer and for the cleanness of the congregation, alert the elders. These may be able to aid the one who has fallen into error and thus 'save his soul from death.'—Jas. 5:14, 20. _W 9/1 6, 7_

## Wednesday, July 28

_I shall positively gather Jacob, all of you; I shall without fail collect the remaining ones of Israel together. . . . And their king will pass through before them, with Jehovah at the head of them.—Mic. 2:12, 13._

Particularly after the close of World War I in the autumn of 1918 the worship of Jehovah as the Most High God was elevated heaven high. How was that? Because, first in the postwar period

of time, was restoration possible for the worshipers of Jehovah from the oppressed state that was like the condition of the Jewish exiles in Babylon during 607-537 B.C.E. And who is the 'king' above referred to? It is the King who was enthroned at God's right hand in the heavens in the fall of 1914, namely Jesus Christ. He made the 'breakthrough' for the oppressed spiritual Israelites, the remnant of spiritual Israel. He led them into real Christian liberty, Jehovah being the Chief One responsible for their marching out to freedom, and who issued the call at Revelation 18:4. _W 7/15 9-11a_

## Thursday, July 29

_I, Jehovah, am . . . the One causing you to tread in the way in which you should walk.—Isa. 48:17._

What is the 'way we should walk' morally? 'This is what God wills \[or requires] . . . that you abstain from fornication.' (1 Thess. 4:3) You young ones who have dedicated your lives to God have formed a close union with him and have become part of a clean organization. How delighted God is by such a course! But for you to engage in premarital sex relations would be the same as if one of the anointed who is 'joined to the Lord' became 'joined \[sexually] to a harlot.' 'Never may that happen!' exclaimed Paul. It would be the highest insult to such a precious relationship. No other sin is quite the same, for 'he that practices fornication is sinning against his own body.' (1 Cor. 6:15-18) Certainly such sin can destroy the spiritual relationship we have with God. No wonder Paul urged 'flee from fornication.' Yes, 'flee'! Do not wait around to reason or debate on it. Get as far away as you can—and fast! _W 11/1 5, 6_

### Friday, July 30

_A large door that leads to activity has been opened to me._—1 Cor. 16:9.

Paul, in his letter to the Corinthian congregation, wrote that he was staying in Ephesus for a time for the above reason. Before us today there continues to be a large door of activity, “sacred service,” that is open. We are living in the days of the final fulfillment of many Bible prophecies dealing with the time of the end when the good news of the Kingdom is to be preached in all the world for a witness. What will the Christian families of Jehovah’s Witnesses do? The urgency is there, for the time is limited. The provisions are there, for Jehovah has provided his Word the Bible and publications furnished by the “faithful and discreet slave” for the benefit of God-fearing ones. (Matt. 24:45-47) The families are there, and Jehovah has continued to add thousands to the ranks of those sharing in the Kingdom proclamation. (Compare Isaiah 60:22.) So the question for all is: What are you doing? W 10/15 13, 14a

### Sunday, August 1

_The Lamb, who is in the midst of the throne, will shepherd them, and will guide them to fountains of waters of life. And God will wipe out every tear from their eyes._—Rev. 7:17.

God’s Word speaks of order in connection with the resurrection and that certain ones will receive better resurrections. However, there is one group whose members are especially favored. And who might these be? They are the “great crowd” described at Revelation 7:9-17. Their hope is everlasting life in

### Saturday, July 31

_All \[God’s] judicial decisions are in front of me . . . And let Jehovah repay me according to my righteousness, according to my cleanness in front of his eyes. With someone loyal you will act in loyalty._—2 Sam. 22:23-26.

Naturally, if a close relative is disfellowshipped, human emotions can pose a major test for us. Sentiment and family ties are particularly strong between parents and their children, and they are also powerful when a marriage mate is disfellowshipped. Still, we must recognize that, in the final analysis, we will not benefit anyone or please God if we allow emotion to lead us into ignoring His wise counsel and guidance. We need to display our complete confidence in the perfect righteousness of God’s ways, including his provision to disfellowship unrepentant wrongdoers. If we remain loyal to God and to the congregation, the wrongdoer may in time take a lesson from that, repent and be reinstated. Yet, whether that occurs or not, we can draw comfort and strength from what David said. W 9/15 28b

the new earth. As a group they pass through the “great tribulation” without having to die. What a precious privilege these enjoy as a result of “washing their robes and making them white in the blood of the Lamb”! In this most important finale to the last days, when a resounding witness must be given to all nations, the “great crowd,” to the number of more than 2,300,000 at present, are seen performing such “sacred service” in the earthly courtyards of God’s temple. To these the above words apply. W 12/15 16

### Monday, August 2

_The path of the righteous ones is like the bright light that is getting lighter and lighter until the day is firmly established._—Prov. 4:18.

The modern-day record of Jehovah’s people clearly demonstrates that their pathway has indeed been and is like the bright light that gets ever brighter. As they came out of the gross darkness enveloping the world empire of false religion, it was not to be expected that they would see all things immediately in their true light. The brilliance of revealed truth could have had a blinding, even a confusing, effect upon them spiritually. This could be compared to a person’s coming out of a totally dark room into bright sunlight. It would take time for the person’s eyes to adjust to the sudden glare of brilliant sunlight. And this progressive light of truth covers a wide range. There are refined understandings as to doctrines, as to prophecies, as to Christian conduct and the commission of Christians, as to the meaning of Jesus’ parables, and so forth. W 12/1 19, 20a

### Tuesday, August 3

_Euodia I exhort and Syntyche I exhort to be of the same mind in the Lord. Yes, I request you too, genuine yokefellow, keep assisting these women who have striven side by side with me in the good news._—Phil. 4:2, 3.

Among the situations in which Christian brothers and sisters can help are such as the foregoing. There apparently was a problem between these two Christian women. Surely the apostle was not urging the Philippians to take sides over the

difference. That would polarize the congregation, producing factions, jealousy and strife. That is a serious danger that must be avoided. Yet, without busybodying as to the women’s grievances or views, the brothers and sisters, like the “genuine yokefellow” in Philippi, could offer corrective encouragement toward Christian unity, being forgiving and working together in love. If you are given such well-motivated correction by a fellow worshiper, you should look upon it as an expression of kindness, even as David expressed at Psalm 141:5. W 9/1 11

### Wednesday, August 4

_Happy is that slave, if his master on arriving finds him doing so! I tell you truthfully, He will appoint him over all his belongings._—Luke 12:43, 44.

This means that, as a reward for showing himself faithful and discreet and keeping constantly awake in expectation of his master’s return, the “slave” is given greater responsibility as a “steward.” This was possible. Why so? Because the master had successfully gained something by his journey and came back with more than what he had when he departed. His “belongings” had been enhanced, and thus there was more over which to appoint his faithful “steward.” His being continued in the office of “steward” would signify serving in a larger capacity. His master himself had returned in a larger capacity than that in which he had found himself when he went away on his certain mission. In this modern-day fulfillment of the parable this has been true with respect to the remnant of the “steward” class and his Master, the glorified Jesus Christ. W 10/1 15

### Thursday, August 5

_I beat my body black and blue, lest haply, after preaching to others, I myself should prove reprobate.—1 Cor. 9:27, The Expositor's Greek Testament._

Paul 'got tough' with himself. So too, rather than let their bodily desires dominate, youths should anticipate situations that will excite sexual feelings and deliberately avoid them. For instance, one knows what will happen to one's bodily desires if one reads books and watches TV programs and movies that glorify sex. Hence, these should be avoided like the plague. Dating, where socially acceptable, when you are not ready for marriage, certain types of dancing, parties with no chaste direction and where couples can pair off, all work up the sexual appetite. So avoid them. (Col. 3:5) Especially when a couple are dating and preparing for marriage do they need to watch their circumstances. Being alone in an auto, apartment, or in some secluded spot outdoors may induce couples to become overly intimate. _W 11/1 15, 16_

### Friday, August 6

_Remember, now, your Grand Creator in the days of your young manhood.—Eccl. 12:1._

Throughout the earth in the branch offices of the Watch Tower Society there are Bethel homes in operation where wholly dedicated young men and women can serve their Creator in the days of their young manhood and womanhood. This form of 'sacred service' also brings many joys to those who qualify for it, since they are able to render a service in behalf of their brothers and sisters who

are in the preaching and teaching ministry. Many families are blessed with the privilege of having children or relatives serving in a Bethel home. While this field of full-time activity does not allow for one to share in the public ministry to the same extent that the pioneer or missionary work does, still those serving at Bethel share in preaching from house to house and spreading the Kingdom message especially on weekends. (Acts 20:20) Moreover, they are providing essential services that help keep the worldwide ministry moving forward. _W 10/15 12a_

### Saturday, August 7

_Clothe yourselves with the tender affections of compassion, kindness.—Col. 3:12._

One of the most basic ways in which a married couple can heed this counsel is by sharing each other's company. By just being together, they can build each other up, make each feel needed and appreciated. In one marriage that broke up after many years the wife had been prone to take long vacations apart from her husband. Just being together is good, but better still is doing together as many things as possible. Do you read the Bible on a personal basis? Why not read it aloud to your spouse? Do you prepare for meetings? To the extent practical, why not prepare lessons together? Do you, the husband, have a part on the program? Why not rehearse it aloud with your wife playing the role of the audience? Sit together at meetings, as well as at other times. Share in the Christian preaching work together. All of these are further ways we can show kindness, strengthen our marriage and let God be in it. _W 7/1 8, 9_

### Sunday, August 8

_Shepherd the flock of God in your care, . . . becoming examples to the flock.—1 Pet. 5:2, 3._

Today, civil strife has flared up throughout the land of El Salvador. Our brothers are strictly neutral as to the violence. Even so, some of them have been killed in the cross fire. But faithful elders have continued to serve congregations and isolated groups, strengthening these. (Compare Isaiah 32:1, 2.) Some of them have narrowly escaped death, but they are determined to keep right on in their teaching and shepherding work, even at continuing risk of their lives. Congregations hold their meetings ahead of curfew, and Jehovah has blessed them richly with a 22-percent increase in publishers during 1981. These 8,242 Kingdom publishers were conducting 13,948 home Bible studies with newly interested persons as the service year drew to its close. How wonderfully our God Jehovah blesses all those who press on fearlessly, though in peril every hour! _W 12/15 7a_

### Monday, August 9

_Indeed, a man's enemies will be persons of his own household.—Matt. 10:36._

Christians do not want such enmity to exist. And there is no reason why relatives should oppose or hate them for having become clean, moral, honest servants of Jehovah God. Yet true Christians realize that they cannot put family before God. In the long run, what is in everyone's best interest is for Christians to continue faithful to God. In time they may be able to influence their relatives to walk on the

way leading to salvation. (Rom. 9:1-3; 1 Cor. 7:12-16) Relatives may also cause problems in another way, as when a relative is disfellowshipped. The conduct of the wrongdoer has changed his relationship with Jehovah and therefore with family members who are Jehovah's Witnesses. God is not to blame for these results, because his standards are righteous and just. (Job 34:10, 12) Nor does the fault rest with the faithful Christian relatives. It is the disfellowshipped one who has made problems. _W 9/15 6, 7b_

### Tuesday, August 10

_Seek meekness. Probably you may be concealed in the day of Jehovah's anger.—Zeph. 2:3._

How essential it is that we 'seek meekness,' as we are surrounded by the proud, demanding, opinionative, 'me-first' attitude of the world! There is always the danger that some of this attitude may rub off on us. We need to be on guard, always willing to accept discipline from Jehovah and his organization, and to conform ourselves to his will. The reward is for those who 'continue in the truth.' (3 John 3, 4) But why 'probably be concealed'? It is because final salvation depends upon the course of the individual, just as Jesus indicated. (Matt. 24:13, 14) Endure, then, all meek ones, in humbly doing God's will for this day, which includes preaching this good news of God's established kingdom in all the inhabited earth. So doing, you may be among those 'hidden of Jehovah' in the day of his anger. The psalmist David writes of such ones: 'The meek ones themselves will possess the earth.'—Ps. 37:11. _W 8/15 21, 22_

## Wednesday, August 11

_I glorify my ministry._—Rom. 11:13.

Since the word _minister_ is derived from the Latin adjective _minus_, meaning “less,” to be a minister means, basically, for one to “be or act like something less.” The corresponding Greek word _di·a·ko·nos_ has a like lowly derivation. It is understood to be drawn from _di·a_ (meaning “through”) and _konis_ (meaning “dust”). To a Greek word would present the idea of someone going through the dust in order to render some service. However, despite the lowly roots of the Greek word, Paul used it when writing the above to the Romans. When Paul finally arrived in Rome and made contact with the congregation, he continued to do what he had said in his letter written years earlier to them: he glorified his ministry. (Acts 28:16-31) As a result of his doing so “most of the brothers in the Lord, feeling confidence by reason of my prison bonds, are showing all the more courage to speak the word of God fearlessly.”—Phil. 1:12-14. W 3/15 6, 8, 9a

## Thursday, August 12

_For the sake of my name I shall check my anger, and for my praise I shall restrain myself toward you that there may be no cutting you off._—Isa. 48:9.

As the light kept on increasing, God’s people appreciated more and more the importance of publishing Jehovah’s name. In fact, in time these faithful servants of Jehovah came to realize that the vindication of Jehovah’s name is the great issue confronting mankind and that this is far more important than even the

salvation of human creatures, as is seen by the above words of Isaiah. This was indeed a brilliant flash of light. This striking truth made the distinction between the true and the counterfeit Christian religion still more obvious. Once this weighty truth was appreciated it was not long until these Christians saw that their designation “Bible Students,” while not incorrect, was not distinctive enough. They were more than just Bible students; they were primarily witnesses for Jehovah God.—Isa. 43:10-12. W 12/1 12, 13a

## Friday, August 13

_My son, do give your heart to me, and may those eyes of yours take pleasure in my own ways._—Prov. 23:26.

This appeal calls for more than just mechanical obedience, but for a young person to open up and confide. However, as a youth, you may feel your Christian dad or mom simply does not understand your feelings. One Christian girl who felt this way worried about talking to her mother. How would she react? Would she understand? She found that her mother did understand and was able to help her. Yes, youngsters have found ready help by confiding in their Christian parents. They come to “take pleasure” in the ways of their godly parents and view their rules and discipline as a precious “necklace” rather than a harsh chain holding them back from a good time. (Prov. 1:8, 9) Are you really honest with your parents? By drawing close to God-fearing parents, you can be helped to avoid needless anguish. Those without Christian parents can approach mature Christians for help. W 11/1 13, 14

## Saturday, August 14

_I do all things for the sake of the good news, that I may become a sharer of it with others._—I Cor. 9:23.

In order to give the witness where our house-to-house activity is banned, our brothers apply Jesus’ words about being “cautious as serpents and yet innocent as doves.” (Matt. 10:16, 17) By doing so they avoid harassment by those who would otherwise trouble the ones preaching from house to house. In this way a thorough witness can be given in spite of the ban. Then again, in years past it may have been normal to call at homes in the mornings. But if conditions change and most are at work then, does that mean that this method of work is not practical? No, it may be advisable to make visits in the afternoon or early evening, when family members are at home. The goal is to reach and help persons, as many as possible. And even Paul adapted his ways to fit the audience, so we should be willing to change our approach if we meet persons who are not interested in the Bible and religion. W 1/1 15-17a

## Sunday, August 15

_This good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness._—Matt. 24:14.

This preaching of the “good news” has been an unusual mercy on God’s part. From the spring of 1935 those making up the great crowd have availed themselves of God’s mercy and have responded to the invitation to dedicate themselves to God and have symbolized their dedication by water baptism. Jesus’ parable of the sheep and

the goats shows that they must do good to his chosen ones, his spiritual brothers, now during this conclusion of the system of things. (Matt. 25:31-46) This includes their helping Christ’s spiritual brothers in preaching “this good news of the kingdom” down till the oncoming great tribulation, the like of which has never occurred before. Because of cooperating with the remnant, they enter into a new status. The great crowd of sheep-like ones who are gathered to Christ’s right side of approval serve in the capacity of envoys for Christ in the sacred service of his kingdom. W 6/15 9, 10a

## Monday, August 16

_Repent and turn to God by doing works that befit repentance._—Acts 26:20.

Thus Paul exhorted persons. When the Jews in the days of Nehemiah repented over having foreign wives, they took definite action that reflected repentance. (Neh. 9:1, 2) Consequently, in dealing with a case of gross sin, the elders will be interested in whether the wrongdoer has “produced fruit that befits repentance.” (Matt. 3:8) If he sinned against a person, has he confessed his sin to that one and asked for forgiveness? For example, in a case of adultery, has he made confession to his innocent mate and asked forgiveness? Or if the wrongdoer is guilty of fraud, has he taken any steps to compensate for the loss? In some circumstances he might be unable to undo all the damage he has caused, but does he give evidence that reasonable steps will be taken to make up for any loss? Yes, to what extent has the guilty person produced the “fruit befitting repentance”? W 9/1 20, 21a

### Tuesday, August 17

_Be obedient to those who are taking the lead among you and be submissive.—Heb. 13:17._

If you are a husband, are you willing to let your wife exercise headship responsibilities so as to maintain peace in the family circle? If you are a wife, do you chafe under the authority exercised by your husband? Do you find it difficult to accept the way in which your husband exercises authority in the family? Do you find yourself rebellious, demanding liberation from such authority? If you are a boy or a girl, how do you view the authority of your parents, be it your father, your mother or a guardian? Do you find yourself willingly submitting to their directions? Or do you fight against their authority? Do you demand independence and freedom from their authority? A Christian's view of authority and his submission to it have a deep and lasting effect on his relationship with the Creator, Jehovah, and with others, both in the world and in the family. The apostle Paul states a principle that also applies to the family circle. _W 10/15 2-5_

### Wednesday, August 18

_Our faith is that . . . those who have fallen asleep in death through Jesus God will bring with him.—1 Thess. 4:14._

The resurrection hope should be a great stimulus to each one of us. Death touches the lives of each one of us at some time or another. When loved ones die, we may reflect on Jehovah's grand purposes, so that we 'may not sorrow just as the rest also do who have no hope.' (1 Thess. 4:13) We may have faith that the dead will rise again, just as

Jesus rose from the dead. Also, we have the assured expectation that, should we ourselves die, Jehovah will remember us in the resurrection, provided we have been faithful in carrying out our dedication to him. In such an event, how desirable it is that we had been expending ourselves in God's service with our whole heart, soul, mind and strength! For then his approval and our share in the resurrection would be assured. Moreover, our keeping integrity would have been an encouragement and blessing to others who observed our conduct. _W 12/15 1, 2a_

### Thursday, August 19

_Cry out joyfully, you barren woman that did not give birth! . . . For your Grand Maker is your husbandly owner, Jehovah of armies being his name.—Isa. 54:1-5._

It is nothing new or recent that God should be spoken of as a Husband. When Jesus Christ himself said: 'It is written in the Prophets, 'And they will all be taught by Jehovah'' (John 6:45), he was quoting from the above prophecy, which speaks of God as a Husband. The apostle Paul at Galatians 4:27 quoted from that same prophecy. Certainly Paul was not there speaking of literal women on earth, for Jehovah God was not the Husband of an individual woman in either a typical sense or in a symbolic sense. What was figuratively God's 'wife' was something greater. What was it? Why, God's organization, which he created for himself. Like a husband, he will make it fruitful so that it gives birth to a 'seed,' or offspring, by means of which God will destroy Satan and the organization he started with Adam and Eve. Yes, God has an organization. _W 5/1 1, 3, 4b_

### Friday, August 20

_The sacrifice of the wicked ones is something detestable to Jehovah . . . The way of the wicked one is something detestable to Jehovah, but the one pursuing righteousness he loves.—Prov. 15:8, 9._

A Christian woman may be married to a man who was a dedicated Christian but was later expelled from the congregation. Yet that would not end their marital ties; only death or a Scriptural divorce would do that. Similarly, if a relative, such as a parent, son or daughter, is disfellowshipped or has disassociated himself, blood and family ties remain. Does that mean, then, that in the family circle everything remains the same when one member is disfellowshipped? Definitely not. A disfellowshipped person has been spiritually cut off from the congregation; the former spiritual ties have been completely severed. This is true even with respect to relatives, including those within his immediate family circle. Thus, family members—while acknowledging family ties—will no longer have any spiritual fellowship with him. _W 9/15 9-11b_

### Saturday, August 21

_I pummel my body and lead it as a slave, that, after I have preached to others, I myself should not become disapproved somehow.—1 Cor. 9:27._

For some 40 years the Bible Students stressed the importance of cultivating a fine Christian personality. True, Christians were also to bear witness, but this was more or less secondary. Later, when God's people began to appreciate the importance of Jehovah's name and that they were to witness to his

name and kingdom, this was emphasized, with the result that less attention was paid to cultivating a Christlike personality. It was argued that above all else Jesus came to bear witness, and that preaching is what really counts. But in due course, a happy balance was reached. Christians need both to cultivate the fruitage of God's spirit and to witness fearlessly and faithfully for Jehovah. We cannot neglect the one on the grounds of doing the other. Paul said: 'Woe is me if I did not declare the good news!' (1 Cor. 9:16) But he also said the above. _W 12/1 10, 11b_

### Sunday, August 22

_Go therefore and make disciples of people of all the nations.—Matt. 28:19._

For the benefit of all his disciples—not only the first-century Christians but all Christians down through the years, and especially those Christians living on earth during 'the conclusion of the system of things'—the resurrected Christ states these words. Here is proof for any who need it that the commission Christ gave his disciples on the day of his ascension as recorded in Acts 1:8 was not completely fulfilled in the first century C.E. It would go on being fulfilled right up to the 'conclusion of the system of things.' (Matt. 28:20) Yes, the commission to be witnesses to the ends of the earth and to make disciples of people of all the nations has been passed on to the anointed remnant of spiritual Israel, who are collectively Jehovah's 'servant' and his 'witnesses.' (Isa. 43:10-12) They have been faithfully fulfilling this commission particularly since 1919. To assist them Jehovah has provided a 'great crowd.'—Rev. 7:9. _W 3/1 2-4a_

## Monday, August 23

_The intimacy with Jehovah belongs to those fearful of him._
—Ps. 25:14.

To avoid the snares of immorality one must cultivate a deep and intimate knowledge of and acquaintance with Jehovah God. Do you, as a young person, have this personal accurate knowledge? To have it you need to study God's Word personally and regularly. Only then can you appreciate God's qualities. Both sacred and secular history show that such accurate knowledge strengthened the early Christians. But more than book learning is necessary. Heartfelt prayers build a closeness to God. Also by becoming a fellow worker with God, engaging in the preaching work, your interests and objectives will become the same as his. This will naturally build a closeness to Jehovah. True, the pressure is on young ones today. To be faithful is a daily battle. Still, the battle will not continue forever. In God's new order, so near at hand, there will be a righteous environment that will make our course so much easier. W 11/1 20-23

## Tuesday, August 24

_Our mouth has been opened to you, Corinthians, our heart has widened out. You are not cramped for room within us, but you are cramped for room in your own tender affections. So, as a recompense in return—I speak as to children—you, too, widen out._—2 Cor. 6:11-13.

Did Paul conduct himself as a lesser one in relation to his brothers? He certainly did, even as we read at 2 Corinthians 11:7: "Did I commit a sin in hum-

bling myself that you might be exalted?" And why was he able to do so? It was because of his great love for them. Thus, in writing to his fellow Christians in Rome, he looked forward not only to strengthening their faith but also to having them strengthen his faith through an interchange of encouragement, even as we read at Romans 1:8-12. Bearing like testimony are his words to the brothers at Corinth, as given above. And he wrote similar expressions of love and affection to the Christians at Philippi and at Thessalonica.
—Phil. 1:8; 4:1; 1 Thess. 2:7, 8. W 6/1 15a

## Wednesday, August 25

_Do not fear those who kill the body and after this are not able to do anything more._—Luke 12:4.

There are those hundreds of Witnesses who were beheaded, shot, starved or beaten to death in Hitler's concentration camps. More recently, many have endured dangers and persecutions in certain socialistic and newly developing lands. Take as an example the African country of Zimbabwe, which for many years was torn asunder by civil strife. Our brothers were outstanding in their neutrality, and in their continued preaching of the Kingdom during that era of violence. It was especially dangerous for the traveling overseers to continue their upbuilding visits to the congregations. One circuit overseer was killed, and his body was left lying by his bicycle as a warning to passersby. However, other circuit overseers continued fearlessly in their service. If ever we should be confronted by similar circumstances, may we be just as fearless in continuing our "sacred service" to God! W 12/15 5, 6a

## Thursday, August 26

_Children, be obedient to your parents in union with the Lord, for this is righteous._—Eph. 6:1.

Such is the pointed counsel of God's Word. Obedience requires recognition of authority. Parents are charged with the responsibility of raising their children in the ways set forth in God's Word so as to be pleasing to him, and that word forbids promiscuity, loose sexual conduct. If children have parents who are interested in their being safeguarded from the immoral world, those parents deserve the loyal support of their children. Rather than chafing under the authority of such parents, the sensible, God-fearing child will follow the Biblical advice to be obedient to them, knowing that this has divine approval, is for his or her own good and promises a secure future. To refuse to show subjection to one's parents is to be disobedient to God. Just as he has set out rules and regulations for the governing of the human family, so he has given parents the right to set out upright regulations. W 10/15 14, 15

## Friday, August 27

_My kingdom is no part of this world. If my kingdom were part of this world, my attendants would have fought . . . But, as it is, my kingdom is not from this source._—John 18:36.

Before Pontius Pilate Jesus outstandingly made the Kingdom the issue. From his question to Jesus (John 18:33), it can be seen that the theme of the Kingdom had already been set. It was the very theme that needed to be pursued that day. The Kingdom was really the is-

sue. Down through the decades the Kingdom always has been the issue, and now, with God's modern-day people, the issue is still the Kingdom. The question has been, Which government is superior in an individual's life—man's or God's? That was the issue during World War II and it still is. The principal issue is not one of blood transfusion or some other prohibition, but always finally, Which government is ultimately superior in a person's life? In Jesus' short reply to Pilate's question he mentioned the Kingdom three times. W 5/15 9, 11, 12

## Saturday, August 28

_In that day . . . I will call my servant, namely, Eliakim the son of Hilkiah. And I will clothe him with your \[Shebna's] robe, . . . and your dominion I shall give into his hand; and he must become a father to the inhabitant of Jerusalem and to the house of Judah._—Isa. 22:20, 21.

This corresponds with Jesus' appointing the faithful, discreet "steward" class over all his Kingdom belongings. Particularly since the spring of 1919 a new chapter opened up in religious history. Then, like Eliakim, the remnant of the "steward" class has been robed with the dignity of being ambassadors of the established kingdom of Jehovah God by Christ. In this capacity they have advertised the established kingdom worldwide. (Matt. 24:14) Pictorially speaking, the Almighty God has girded their loins for the discharge of this weighty ministry by binding about their hips the "sash" of a steward or majordomo. Assisting in this Kingdom-preaching work are the "great crowd" of "other sheep."—John 10:16; Rev. 7:9. W 10/1 12a

<sup>**Page 174**</sup>

[Open Page 174 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=174)

## Sunday, August 29

_Just as the days of Noah were, so the presence of the Son of man will be.—Matt. 24:37._

The flood of Noah's day gave the human race a fresh start from a righteous, God-fearing family, and this in an earth peaceful and safe for the time being. That was the only occasion previous to our time when all humanity was in danger of extinction. It prefigured our day when a world of billions of people is threatened. That is not our pessimistic human reasoning on matters, an extremist idea. It is no more overdrawn than that of a world-famous figure, Jesus Christ. Pointing to our day, he said the above. According to Christ's own prophecy about world conditions during the time of his unseen presence at the earth, our world situation since the year 1914 is like that of Noah's day. Correspondingly, a similar 'act of God' must be at hand. It is the time for the God-given warning to be sounded out to all imperiled mankind. The personal question is, Who will be like those who entered into the ark with Noah? _W 2/1_ 7, 8

## Monday, August 30

_I write you, young men, because you are strong and the word of God remains in you.—1 John 2:14._

Parents must be convinced of the power of God's Word. John said that the spiritually strong 'young men' in the congregation to which he wrote had 'conquered the wicked one' because 'the word of God remained in them.' Hence, in addition to promoting a close family spirit and setting a good example, godly parents should arrange

for the Word of God to be studied regularly by the family so that its message sinks deeply into a youngster's heart. By good preparation on the parents' part, avoiding a mechanical, stiff, overly formal procedure, and tailoring the study to fit the needs of the children, the discussion will be anticipated and will draw the family together spiritually. Granted, with all the demands on parents' time this is not easy, but more important than the length of such discussions is the quality of the time spent together. Children also need to be taught good study habits of their own. _W 11/1_ 21a

## Tuesday, August 31

_Stay awake, stand firm in the faith, carry on as men, grow mighty. Let all your affairs take place with love.—1 Cor. 16:13, 14._

We must hold fast the good news, including its resurrection hope. Faithful men and women of ancient times believed in an earthly resurrection, and they looked forward to it. Christ Jesus was raised as the 'firstfruits' of the resurrection. Anointed Christians have 'preached in all creation that is under heaven,' both in apostolic and in modern times, so that the 'good news' relating to the resurrection has circled the globe. (Col. 1:23) And as the remaining anointed ones finish their earthly course to gain their heavenly reward, a great crowd, with hope of everlasting life on earth, has taken up the proclamation of the Kingdom 'good news.' How privileged are all in these groups as they attain to their goals. Truly, we today have every reason to follow Paul's admonition given above. Thus we will be believers to good purpose. _W 12/15_ 21, 22

## Wednesday, September 1

_Let us not sleep on as the rest do, but let us stay awake and keep our senses.—1 Thess. 5:6._

In 70 C.E., God's righteous execution of judgment came against those who had profaned his name, broken his laws and persecuted his servants. (Luke 21:22) Similarly, God's righteous execution of judgment against this present wicked system of things is soon to come, once again demonstrating that all the things written in Bible prophecy are sure to be fulfilled. And that judgment will come with shocking suddenness to those who are unprepared, even as we read at 1 Thessalonians 5:2, 3. Between now and the time when God's judgments are concluded, it is imperative for Jehovah's servants to maintain the highest sense of vigilance and dedication of purpose, even as Paul counsels above. Any person who wants to survive into God's righteous new order urgently needs to come into a right relationship with Jehovah and His earthly organization now.—Prov. 18:10; Isa. 2:2. _W 11/15_ 1-3a

## Thursday, September 2

_In a large house there are vessels not only of gold and silver but also of wood and earthenware, and some for an honorable purpose but others for a purpose lacking honor.—2 Tim. 2:20._

On occasion there may be a Christian who pursues a course that is out of harmony with God's directions and does not change despite help even from the elders. This is not a case of someone who merely has a personality difference with another person. Nor is it just that a person has yet to grow up to Chris-

tian maturity and so reflects in various ways his need for continued growth. Paul acknowledged that there would be immature Christians who should be helped with patience, love and a desire to see them progress. But, aside from such, there may be someone who chooses to pursue a course that, while it does not yet amount to grave sin, is clearly in conflict with God's counsel. It should not be shocking that there occasionally might be some Christians of this sort. Timothy was warned to keep clear of such dishonorable vessels. _W 9/1_ 12, 13

## Friday, September 3

_You yourselves know that these hands have attended to the needs of me and of those with me.—Acts 20:34._

Till now hundreds of thousands of sheeplike persons have flocked to the side of the anointed remnant and have joined them in the Kingdom ministry. Not all of these have been able to devote their full time to that ministry in the capacity of full-time publishers, traveling representatives of the Society, or as staff members of branch offices of the Society. Earthly obligations require most of them to do secular work for most of their time or a good part of it. Yet their dedication to God, as symbolized by water baptism, calls for them to be ministers of him in serving the interests of his kingdom. They find themselves in a situation like that of Paul. For a year and a half he worked in Corinth as a tentmaker with Aquila, a Jewish believer. (Acts 18:1-11) We also recall what the apostle Paul said to the elders of the congregation at Ephesus, even as noted above. _W 3/15_ 14-16a
