## Saturday, September 4

_He will wipe out every tear from their eyes, and death will be no more, neither will mourning nor outcry nor pain be anymore. The former things have passed away._
—Rev. 21:4.

The new order will be marked by physical blessings coming from God. He will eliminate sickness and death. He will bless the work of our hands so that we can enjoy an abundance of life's necessities. (Ps. 67:6; compare Isaiah 65:21-25.) There will even be peace between mankind and God's animal creation; Jehovah God will see to that, just as in the original paradise when the animals ate vegetation and did not harm humans. Those blessings are right ahead. You can live to experience them. Thus, how unique a time we are living in! Despite the ingratitude and unhappiness that mark mankind in general today, we have abundant reasons to be grateful to Jehovah God, especially for the happy hope we have. Let us regularly thank him for that, in our homes, at our Kingdom Halls and when going from house to house. W 4/1 25, 26a

## Sunday, September 5

_I shall make you people to be a name and a praise among all the peoples of the earth._—Zeph. 3:20.

As faithful witnesses, Jehovah's people have continued to preach with missionary zeal, so that Jehovah's organization has expanded to the very ends of the earth. It has indeed been a time for bringing in God's people, collecting them together. And for what purpose? Jehovah himself answers as above. Happily, the remnant of God's people have

become "a name and a praise" in holding high the precious name of the Sovereign Lord Jehovah, and now many of those "peoples of the earth" are working with them, "shoulder to shoulder," in making known his Kingdom purposes. Our change to speaking the "pure language," and our continuing to herald forth that "good news of the kingdom," will yet help thousands more to call upon the name of Jehovah, that these, too, may be 'hidden of Jehovah' during the day of his anger, and come forth to praise him throughout all eternity. W 8/15 18a

## Monday, September 6

_Counsel in the heart of a man is as deep waters, but the man of discernment is one that will draw it up._—Prov. 20:5.

Christian parents yearn to protect their children from devastating moral pitfalls. Yet, why is it that at times, even after being taken to Kingdom Hall meetings and being taught Bible morality, a child may still become involved in sexual immorality? Though head learning is important, the heart plays a vital role, especially with morals. What can a parent do to reach a child's heart so that it becomes wise? Before you can reach the heart, you have to find out, to some extent, what is in it. The above words are to the point. The real feelings in the heart of a child are like waters at the bottom of a deep well. To draw up your own child's intentions may be difficult. Doing this takes empathy and keen observation. It may require the skillful use of questions, patience—sometimes being willing to talk with the child for hours before his real feelings surface. W 11/1 1-3a

## Tuesday, September 7

_The Most High . . . is kind toward the unthankful and wicked._
—Luke 6:35.

Letting God be in our marriage will immeasurably strengthen it and assure its success and happiness. (Eccl. 4:12) Among the things that God requires of all his earthly creatures, and which has special meaning for marriage mates, is that they be kind to each other. Jehovah God himself sets the example for us, even as his Word assures us. In fact, over a hundred times we read in his Word of his "undeserved kindness," and almost twice as often of his "loving-kindness." To have this kindly God in our marriage we must heed the counsel at Colossians 3:12: "Clothe yourselves with the tender affections of compassion, kindness, lowliness of mind, mildness, and long-suffering." How can we show kindness to our mate and thus let God be in our marriage? To be kind means to minister to the needs of another. It means to be thoughtful, considerate of each other's well-being. W 7/1 6-8

## Wednesday, September 8

_If in this life only we have hoped in Christ, we are of all men most to be pitied._—1 Cor. 15:19.

Yes, then our faith would be useless, our preaching in vain. But our eyes are firmly set on that assured goal of life in Jehovah's new order—whether we attain to it by surviving Har-Magedon, or, as in the case of some, by an early resurrection from the dead. Paul was addressing Christians who had been anointed by God's spirit, and who were required to undergo a baptism into death, just as

was experienced by Jesus during the three and a half years down to his execution at Calvary. So, Paul asks the question: "If the dead are not to be raised up at all, why are they also being baptized for the purpose of being such?" (1 Cor. 15:29) Yes, the resurrection hope sustained those Christians, enabling them to endure any trials, and it can sustain Christians today, whether their hope is eternal life in the heavenly kingdom or—for the greater number—life in the earthly realm of that kingdom. W 12/15 3a

## Thursday, September 9

_You must inculcate them in your son and speak of them when you sit in your house and when you walk on the road and when you lie down and when you get up._
—Deut. 6:7.

Jehovah commanded his chosen people, the ancient Israelites, to teach their children his laws and commandments. They were to repeat the teachings at home, when they were away from home, when they were resting and when they were working. That same instruction is appropriate for families today in their offering "sacred service" to God. When we are taught to 'seek first the Kingdom,' we learn to assume responsibilities. One of those responsibilities is that of sharing in providing others with the opportunity to have the hope we have in God's kingdom. One of the finest qualities a parent can implant in his child is the desire to share the Kingdom good news with others. From an early age children can be taught to have a share in speaking with others about the Kingdom good news. This takes much effort on the part of the parents. W 10/15 5, 6a

### Friday, September 10

_If he does not listen even to the congregation, let him be to you just as a man of the nations and as a tax collector.—Matt. 18:17._

By these words Jesus could not have meant that his disciples were to refuse to do an act of human kindness, as in a case of accident or of desperate need. Jesus showed such kindness to some Gentiles. For example, he did so to a Syrophoenician woman. Though Jesus, his disciples and the woman acknowledged that her situation was unusual because she was a Gentile and Jesus was sent to the Jews, Christ nevertheless healed her daughter. (Matt. 15:21-28) Jesus showed similar human kindness when a Roman army officer implored him to heal a paralyzed and suffering slave. The officer admitted that he did not expect Jesus, a Jewish teacher, to enter his home. Yet Jesus showed mercy. (Luke 7:1-10) So by what he said about someone's being 'as a man of the nations and as a tax collector,' Jesus did not forbid expressions of merciful kindness. W 9/15 14

### Saturday, September 11

_I will drive him in as a peg in a lasting place.—Isa. 22:23._

Thus Jehovah prophesied further regarding the modern Eliakim class. The permanently placed 'peg' from which those household utensils were to hang pictured Eliakim. Accordingly he was put in charge of serving food and drink to the members of the king's household. The appointing of Eliakim to serve in that capacity typified that the Greater Hezekiah, Jesus Christ, at his coming in glory, approved

of the 'faithful steward' class to be over the remnant of his joint heirs yet on earth to issue to this 'body of attendants' the needed measure of food supplies at the proper time, whether in large measure or in small measure. This depended upon the type of solid food or of drink. Of course, such 'food supplies' would be from God's Word. However, from another Bible standpoint, those 'vessels' picture the servants of Jehovah God themselves, having varying capacities for service.—2 Tim. 2:20-22. W 10/1 15, 16a

### Sunday, September 12

_Of this good news I Paul became a minister.—Col. 1:23._

The term 'minister' is a useful one, for it refers to a special kind of 'servant,' one with an exalted, special assignment of service. Anyone, regardless of age and sex, who is able to demonstrate that he or she has a good understanding of God's will and purposes for mankind and who has brought his or her life in line with Bible principles, and who has made a dedication and been baptized in keeping with Jesus' command at Matthew 28:19, 20, is truly one of God's ministers. If others do not appreciate this position or agree with it, this is of no consequence. But it must be emphasized that 'minister' is not a title but a description. (Compare Matthew 20:28.) It is not enough for a person to take the steps qualifying him to be baptized as a servant of Jehovah God. The person must make his ministry, his 'sacred service' to Jehovah God, the chief aim in his life. Otherwise, he could not properly term himself or be considered by others as a minister.—Rom. 12:1. W 12/1 16-18b

### Monday, September 13

_She did a fine deed toward me. She did what she could.—Mark 14:6, 8._

When Mary, Lazarus' sister, anointed Jesus with costly perfumed oil, some disciples complained, for the oil was worth 300 denarii. Considering sabbaths and festivals, that was what a workman would earn in a whole year. How much do you, or a family member, earn in a year? Jesus said the above regarding Mary's effort. Note those words, 'She did what she could.' There is no evidence that Mary was empowered to perform miracles; she could not be an apostle, nor an elder. But 'she did what she could.' Are we like her? Are we intensely interested in the preaching work that Jesus started and that is now continuing through Christians earth wide? We should be. God is. God backed up Christianity when it began. He is still doing that, for he has not changed. So we have every reason for being enthusiastic workers in the service of God 'who is a Savior of all sorts of men, especially of faithful ones.'—1 Tim. 4:10. W 1/1 25, 26, 28

### Tuesday, September 14

_In the last days there will come ridiculers . . . proceeding according to their own desires and saying: 'Where is this promised presence of his? Why, from the day our forefathers fell asleep in death, all things are continuing exactly as from creation's beginning.'—2 Pet. 3:3, 4._

It is a matter of understanding the times in which we live. Do you have eyes that see, really see? Certainly you see that the times are critical and hard

to deal with, that many people are self-centered and haughty, that marriages collapse and families disintegrate, that cheating and lying and crimes of violence are everywhere, and that even many of those claiming to be Christians are proving to be hypocrites. These things signify that we are living in the critical last days that the apostle Paul foretold at 2 Timothy 3:1-5. Do you have ears that hear, really hear? Certainly the wars, famines, earthquakes and pestilences prove that we are in the conclusion of this system of things.—Matt. 24:3-14. W 2/15 9, 10

### Wednesday, September 15

_Bad associations spoil useful habits. Wake up to soberness in a righteous way and do not practice sin.—1 Cor. 15:33, 34._

Sad to say, there are some in the more affluent and seemingly 'safe' countries who have been misled into a spiritually dangerous way of life. They think that they can hobnob with the world and have a good time in the Epicurean style, but at the same time keep one foot, as it were, in God's organization. They may have been influenced by unwise associations with apostate persons, thus leading to a dimming of appreciation of their privilege of regular zealous Kingdom service. How foolish! Paul pointedly says the above to such. Useful theocratic habits like meeting attendance and service to God are so easily replaced by indulgence in the pleasures of a corrupt world. The vision of God's new order fades, and with it the resurrection hope. Similar things happened to Hymenaeus, Alexander and Philetus in Paul's day. And such things are happening today. W 12/15 8a

### Thursday, September 16

_All of a sudden he is going after her, . . . and he has not known that it involves his very soul._
—Prov. 7:22, 23.

The Biblical instructor in Proverbs chapter five warns against the prostitute. Then he touches a tender spot by showing the young man how he can lose his dignity by such conduct. However, he does not accuse or berate the young man. In Proverbs chapter seven, he relates others' experiences and uses straightforward terms. How could a youth ever forget such imagery! Such a warning example stored in the heart will help the youth to cope with temptation. The parent did not just say that sexual immorality was wrong, but told why, explaining the effects and showing how easily the young person could get involved. Many Christian parents have had similar discussions. They have had them on numerous occasions when the subject could be approached in a natural, informal way, as while taking long walks, or when talking about some incident illustrating the value of proper morals. W 11/1 10-12a

### Friday, September 17

_You have your company of young men just like dewdrops._—Ps. 110:3.

In addition to those serving full time at Bethel homes and in the pioneer work, there are many thousands of other "young men" and "virgins" within the congregations of Jehovah's people. Are you 'offering yourselves willingly on the day of Christ's military force'? Or are you letting the symbolic "horses" pile up in your room or lie dormant in your witnessing bag?

Get those "horses" moving! Get out into the field during your after-school hours, your midweek break (where this arrangement exists), weekends and the various vacation periods you have throughout the year. The auxiliary pioneer service gives you a wonderful opportunity to 'offer yourselves willingly' for "sacred service." And the magazine work is a method of Christian activity that is especially appropriate for you young ones. It is within your reach and it can produce fine results. May Jehovah bless you as you do so.—Rev. 9:16-19. W 3/1 19, 22a

### Saturday, September 18

_Vengeance is mine; I will repay._
—Rom. 12:19.

Will our loving God permit wicked nations to burn it to a cinder? That he will never permit such a thing he plainly states at Isaiah 45:18. His peerless name stands as guarantee that He, as loving Creator and Sovereign Lord of the universe, will not permit the desolating of our globe. Divine love demands that God exact vengeance from those who desecrate our earth and who would annihilate humankind. Since World War I broke out, this earth has been drenched with the blood of tens of millions of innocent persons. And now the prospect of a nuclear holocaust is even more fearsome. So, since the nations refuse to disarm, then our loving God declares that shortly he will disarm them, in his own effective and permanent way. (Ps. 46:8, 9) Yes, it is Jehovah's loving purpose to do what the nations will never do—pulverize all their war equipment. Does not God's promise that he will exact vengeance make us glad and make us want to tell others about it? W 1/15 8, 9a

### Sunday, September 19

_This good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness to all the nations._—Matt. 24:14.

To us, these prophetic words are, in effect, a command to tell others of God's kingdom. And it certainly has been obeyed by us in these last days. Unselfishly we have used our time and resources to instruct others about God's marvelous purpose for this earth. As Jehovah's forward-moving organization approaches the final years of its preaching activity toward this world, there is no doubt that the scope of the work will grow. Recall what the Israelites were instructed to do just before God destroyed Jericho. They had to march around the city once each day for six days, but on the seventh day they had to increase their activity seven times! (Josh. 6:2-5) We can expect a similar expansion of our preaching activity now at this climax of the ages. No doubt before the "great tribulation" is finished, we will see the greatest witness to God's name and kingdom of all time. W 11/15 15-18a

### Monday, September 20

_I shall have to correct you to the proper degree, as I shall by no means leave you unpunished._
—Jer. 30:11.

God often corrects us in such a gentle and mild way that we may not even recognize it as correction. Perhaps we read something in his Word that leads us away from an undesirable course or corrects our thinking. At other times correction from God may be more pointed and even somewhat painful. Yet he knows the degree and the meth-

od of correction that are best for us, even as noted above. How fine it is when we willingly accept his correction, for God provides discipline out of love as a father does for a dear son! It is also much wiser for us to accept any limited or restrained correction that Jehovah God gives us than for us to be punished to the point of extermination. Sometimes God offers correction through humans. He sent prophets and judges to the nation of Israel. But he can provide wise correction even on an individual basis. Job had "corrected many."—Job 4:3. W 9/1 4, 5

### Tuesday, September 21

_The lawless one's presence is according to the operation of Satan with every powerful work and lying signs and portents and with every unrighteous deception._—2 Thess. 2:9, 10.

Clearly, the antichrist is Satan's masterpiece in the form of an organization. It is an imitation of organized Christianity and so an opposer of it. As a replacement of true organized Christianity, it finds its expression in present-day Christendom, this name meaning the realm of Christianity. That is certainly a deceptive name, for Christendom is really the most powerful part of Babylon the Great. In the light of what Paul and John said, Christendom is a departure from the true, original congregation of Jesus Christ, really an apostasy from it. (1 John 2:18, 22) Unlike the first-century Christian congregation, Christendom today embraces more than a thousand religious sectarian organizations, each and every one of which claims to be Christian, the exponent of Bible Christianity. W 5/1 9, 10

<sup>**Page 178**</sup>

[Open Page 178 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=178)

### Wednesday, September 22

_The blessing of Jehovah—that is what makes rich, and he adds no pain with it.—Prov. 10:22._

For your sincere and united efforts to increase in your “sacred service,” Jehovah’s blessing will be yours, and “he adds no pain with it.” So, why not sit down together as a family and consider what you are doing and what you can do? Honesty will no doubt force many of us to admit that with a little more effort we could expand the part our family has in sharing in Jehovah’s “sacred service.” Remember that Jehovah does not ask us to do more than is reasonable. Our love for him, for our family and for mankind in general, should prompt us to examine ourselves and determine what we might do. Think about the various features of our “sacred service,” our glorious ministry as Christ’s disciples. Take the matter to Jehovah in prayer, asking his direction and help in connection with the efforts you make. The blessings are many now with godly contentment, and the future holds promise of life that is life indeed. _W 10/15_ 15, 16a

### Thursday, September 23

_Through love slave for one another.—Gal. 5:13._

Unquestionably, unselfish love will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones toward our brothers and our families. Yes, ‘love does not brag, it does not get puffed up, it does not even seek its own interests.’ It is not unduly concerned about getting its just due. (1 Cor. 13:4, 5) And certainly slaving for one another requires us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. Love can also truly help us for it will

move us to ‘keep seeking, not our own advantage, but that of others.’ (1 Cor. 10:24) And do we not have many examples of this in modern times? Many are those in positions of responsibility who set fine examples in conducting themselves as lesser ones. Humbly they are at the service of their brothers needing help. Particularly does this become apparent at our larger gatherings. Then, regardless of natural endowments or position in the organization, they all ‘roll up their sleeves and pitch in,’ as the saying goes, to get the work done. _W 6/1_ 17, 16a

### Friday, September 24

_Jehovah is one who exacts punishment for all these things.—1 Thess. 4:6._

Some even claim that one will get sick if one does not have sex after reaching puberty. This is false. No sickness has ever been linked by doctors to chastity! Sadly, some persons who have settled for lust rather than love have regretted this the rest of their life! Never forget Paul’s words above! This punishment may be a painful conscience, discipline from the congregational elders, or reaping the consequences of what you sow. Of course, if we are repentant, Jehovah freely forgives and completely covers our sins. But some are stubborn and refuse to heed God’s laws. “Who can show stubbornness to \[God] and come off uninjured?” asked Job. (Job 9:4) _No one!_ It pains Jehovah to see such stubbornness. It hurts concerned elders who have seen many of our young persons suffer in such ways. These overseers realize that no one can violate God’s laws and principles and walk away “uninjured.” It just is not worth it! _W 11/1_ 10, 11

### Saturday, September 25

_God blessed them and God said to them: “Be fruitful and become many and fill the earth and subdue it, and have in subjection the fish of the sea and the flying creatures of the heavens and every living creature that is moving upon the earth.”—Gen. 1:28._

Thus, after creating the first human pair, God revealed his purpose regarding the earth and man. Such is still God’s “will,” which, through his Messianic kingdom, will be done ‘on earth even as it is in heaven.’ (Matt. 6:10) The whole tenor of the Bible shows beyond a doubt that God has not abandoned this original purpose. (Isa. 46:9, 10) The millennium, or 1,000-year reign of Christ, fits into God’s “eternal purpose,” part of which is “to gather all things together again in the Christ, the things in the heavens and the things on the earth.” (Eph. 3:11; 1:8-10) In other words, the millennium is not an end in itself; it is a means to an end, the carrying out of God’s original purpose for the earth. Ours is the privilege to make this known to others. _W 4/15_ 13, 14a

### Sunday, September 26

_Become steadfast, unmovable, always having plenty to do in the work of the Lord.—1 Cor. 15:58._

The quality of steadfastness is essential to those who desire to ‘make it’ into Jehovah’s new order. To be steadfast means to be constant, firm, unwavering. That is the kind of faith that we must have, an assured expectation of the unseen things that lie ahead, but which, through our study of the Scriptures and our Christian experience, have become as real to us as if they

were actually here. (Heb. 11:1) We know that the Messianic kingdom is at hand. We have seen the sign of Christ’s presence in Kingdom glory in the tumultuous events on earth in these last days. We have observed the angelic direction as we have preached with missionary zeal in all the inhabited earth, with literally millions of persons paying attention to the good news. Pressures from Satan’s world will no doubt increase, but that is no reason for our wavering in our “sacred service” to Jehovah God. _W 12/15_ 11, 12a

### Monday, September 27

_All the things that were written aforetime were written for our instruction, that through our endurance and through the comfort from the Scriptures we might have hope.—Rom. 15:4._

In fulfillment of things written aforetime in the Holy Scriptures, Jesus endured reproach and persecution even to a disgraceful death on a stake like a political criminal. In this he became a perfect example for us that would strengthen us to endure faithfully to the end. Because of his steadfastly enduring to the finish of his earthly course Jesus held on to his God-given hope. During those grueling hours on the torture stake, Jesus doubtless experienced much comfort by calling to mind the things “written aforetime” that referred to him, and he was thus strengthened mightily. No less so, his devoted followers who suffer the reproaches heaped upon Jehovah God and Jesus Christ keep a firm grip on their Scripturally inspired hope for the future. They too are tremendously comforted by the Scriptures “written aforetime.” _W 6/15_ 5, 6

<sup>**Page 179**</sup>

[Open Page 179 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=179)

### Tuesday, September 28

_Let each one of you individually so love his wife as he does himself; on the other hand, the wife should have deep respect for her husband.—Eph. 5:33._

What does principled, unselfish love require of a wife? It requires that she recognize her husband as her head. (Eph. 5:22-24) This may not always be an easy thing to do, but love will help her; it will make it easier to put her husband's interests ahead of her own, and always to show him deep respect. What does unselfish love require of a husband? To love his wife as he does his own body. What a great deal this asks of a husband! Just as he takes good care of his own body as to food, clothing, shelter, rest, recreation and spiritual interests, just so he should care for his wife. As he would not want to embarrass himself before others, neither should he embarrass his wife before others. Loving her as his own body, he would dwell with her according to knowledge, being kind and considerate as to the more intimate aspects of marriage. _W 7/1 18, 19_

### Wednesday, September 29

_They will have to beat their swords into plowshares and their spears into pruning shears. They will not lift up sword, nation against nation, neither will they learn war anymore.—Mic. 4:3._

Clearly among the sheeplike ones of the 'great crowd' began the fulfillment of these words. Fearlessly, without letting anyone of this world make them tremble, even after World War II was lighted, the Witnesses openly declared and published their neutrality toward all nations engaging in violent combat.

They have held to this neutral stand ever since. In many cases this has meant their being confined in concentration camps, or in prisons, or even their being killed as being unpatriotic. They refuse to become a part of this world, just as Jesus Christ refused to do so. Properly, then, the Witnesses should be expected to keep peace within their own congregations, dwelling together like brothers. (Ps. 133) Figuratively speaking, they sit, each one 'under his vine and under his fig tree' in security. _W 7/15 4, 5b_

### Thursday, September 30

_Train up a boy according to the way for him; even when he grows old he will not turn aside from it.—Prov. 22:6._

Children need much help to appreciate that, even though many view the glorious message from God as death-dealing, the young proclaimers of the good news should not lose their joy and zeal. Nor should they slack the hand and stop having a part in rendering 'sacred service' to Jehovah. It is readily apparent, then, that parents have a deep and abiding responsibility to reflect in their own lives the zeal and devotion they have by regularly sharing in the work of Kingdom declaration. They must make it a part of their own routine in life to be with their children in Kingdom service, not sending their young ones off to be cared for by others all the time. No one else can shoulder this responsibility for them. Setting a good example is the finest way to inculcate right goals and motives in children. To give proper training, parents should be with their children, sharing 'sacred service' as a family. _W 10/15 7, 8a_

### Friday, October 1

_The path of the righteous ones is like the bright light that is getting lighter and lighter until the day is firmly established.—Prov. 4:18._

The light on the pathway of Jehovah's servants from earliest times to the present has kept on increasing. This has been even more so since the notable year 1914 when, as developments on earth showed, 'the kingdom of the world did become the kingdom of our Lord \[Jehovah] and of his Christ.' (Rev. 11:15) Light from God's Word has flashed forth, like the sunshine on 'a morning without clouds' to illuminate ever more clearly the pathway that Jehovah's servants must tread. (2 Sam. 23:3, 4) Seemingly, that path has not always gone straight forward. At times explanations given by Jehovah's visible organization have shown some adjustments. But there is no question that Jehovah is continuing to bless the global activity of his witnesses, as directed by the 'faithful and discreet slave.' This can be seen by the fruits. —Matt. 7:17; 24:45-47. _W 12/1 1-3b_

### Saturday, October 2

_Always \[have] plenty to do in the work of the Lord, knowing that your labor is not in vain in connection with the Lord.—1 Cor. 15:58._

Yes, 'have plenty to do,' not 'doing one's own thing,' but 'in the work of the Lord' Jesus Christ. Therein lies a recipe for keeping spiritually healthy. Spiritual laziness can lead to doubts. It can be fatal, as Proverbs 19:15 indicates: 'Laziness causes a deep sleep to fall, and a slack soul goes hungry.' In the case of persistent spiritual la-

laziness, spiritual starvation and death are the result. Let us, then, be busy—preaching regularly from house to house (even in unreceptive territories), witnessing informally at every opportunity, making return visits on sheeplike persons and conducting regular home Bible studies with them in one of the Society's publications. Some of us may be able to serve as 'pioneers,' full-time Kingdom proclaimers in this work of the Lord Jesus, who is, indeed, the 'pioneer' of our faith.—Heb. 12:2, _Moffatt_. _W 12/15 18a_

### Sunday, October 3

_Who really is the faithful steward, the discreet one?—Luke 12:42._

Today an international 'great crowd' has responded to the warning sounded by this 'steward' and has sounded it out to still others. (Rev. 7:9-17) They are encouraged by what happened to Eliakim and the other inhabitants of Jerusalem, for these escaped being war casualties by the wholesale destruction of the Assyrian invaders. Likewise the 'steward' class in this 'time of the end' will be spared from being destroyed with the worldly system of things at Har-Magedon. (Dan. 12:4) Happy, indeed, are all those who have ascertained and adhere to the right answer to Jesus' question above. In loyalty to the stewardship of the 'faithful steward,' let them continue to accept at this proper time the needed 'measure of food supplies' at the hands of the 'faithful steward,' the Greater Eliakim. That symbolic 'peg' is, according to God's foreknowledge, driven into a 'lasting place' to stay, with God's help. So hang on to it confidently, in all loyalty. _W 10/1 22, 23a_

#### Monday, October 4

_God called us, not with allowance for uncleanness, but in connection with sanctification._—1 Thess. 4:7.

Some unmarried couples have engaged in passionate, sexually stimulating touching of intimate body parts. This is “uncleanness” and could easily—and often does—lead to immoral sexual intercourse. This practice can inflame a person with sexual appetite to the point of virtual frenzy. When an unmarried person begins engaging in some of the sexual practices reserved for the marriage bed, he or she can be fooled into marrying someone who does not have the qualities needed to be a good husband or a good wife. Sex tends to cover over serious differences that reappear after marriage and cause problems. It is not surprising that a study revealed that out of 265 weddings where the bride was pregnant, after five years only 15 couples were still together! Such practice does not make for a happier marriage! And it is one of the chief causes for the breaking of engagements. _W 11/1 9, 10_

#### Tuesday, October 5

_Since all these things are thus to be dissolved, what sort of persons ought you to be in holy acts of conduct and deeds of godly devotion!_—2 Pet. 3:11.

While now the witness yet includes the invitation to come to God’s organization for salvation, the time no doubt will come when the message takes on a harder tone, like a “great war cry.” Revelation 16:21 shows that “a great hail with every stone about the weight of a talent \[nearly 100 pounds] descended out of heaven upon the men.”

Hailstones are frozen, hardened water. So this pictures how, at the end, God’s judgment message sent down upon disobedient mankind will be like a barrage of hard-hitting hail. The fact that the plague of hailstones is spoken of as being “unusually great” suggests that at the very end there will be an unusually great proclamation of Jehovah’s day of vengeance by Jehovah’s servants. So, then, we do well to say as Peter counsels us above. To this end, let us not fail to “stay awake and keep our senses.”—1 Thess. 5:6. _W 11/15 18, 19a_

#### Wednesday, October 6

_If anyone comes to you and does not bring this teaching, never receive him into your homes or say a greeting to him. For he that says a greeting to him is a sharer in his wicked works._—2 John 10, 11.

Should a Christian not speak at all to a disfellowshipped person, not even saying “Hello”? The apostle John who gives us this wise warning was close to Jesus and knew well what Christ had said about greeting others. He also knew that the common greeting of that time was “Peace.” As distinct from some personal “enemy” or worldly man in authority who opposed Christians, a disfellowshipped or disassociated person who is trying to promote or justify his apostate thinking or is continuing in his ungodly conduct is certainly not one to whom to say “Peace.” And we all know from our experience over the years that a simple “Hello” to someone can be the first step that develops into a conversation and maybe even a friendship. Would we want to take that first step with a disfellowshipped person? _W 9/15 21, 23a_

#### Thursday, October 7

_If it is with all your heart you are returning to Jehovah, put away the foreign gods from your midst . . . and direct your heart unswervingly to Jehovah and serve him alone, and he will deliver you._—1 Sam. 7:3.

Astrology, fortune-telling, spiritism—these find a place among professed Christians today. Under the pretext that ‘all religions lead to the same goal,’ there is wide practice of interfaith in Christendom today. Superstitions of false religion are mixed in with the Bible. Thus, in place of obeying Jesus’ command to observe the Memorial of his death, Christendom highlights Easter, named after the goddess of sensual love, incorporating fertility symbols such as rabbits and Easter eggs. Any who have been involved in such practices would do well to follow the good advice of Samuel. Yes, respect for Jehovah, his Word and his requirements is essential for us if we desire to be among those invited to share in his day of vindication.—Zeph. 1:7. _W 8/15 7_

#### Friday, October 8

_“With the wife of your youth may no one deal treacherously. For he has hated a divorcing,” Jehovah . . . has said._—Mal. 2:15, 16.

It does appear that at times a divorce on the ground of adultery could have been avoided had the “innocent” mate shown more empathy, wisdom, affection, understanding. Of course, regardless of how a wife may have failed in any respect, there is absolutely no justification for adultery on the part of her husband. Truly, it is with good reason that the

God of love hates a divorcing. Treacherous divorcing is sinning against God, against one’s mate, against the Christian congregation with which one is associated and against the individuals within it. But how fine it is that Jehovah’s Witnesses in general are not persons inclined toward divorcing! We are known as a people who earnestly strive to apply God’s counsel regarding marriage. This makes us happier persons. And our strong, happy marriages are often noticed by others, drawing them to the truth. _W 7/1 20, 21a_

#### Saturday, October 9

_You are my witnesses. Does there exist a God besides me? No, there is no Rock._—Isa. 44:8.

Activities like those of the prophet Micah have been carried on particularly since the end of World War I in 1918. Then the Micah class resolutely stepped forward onto the post-war scene and outstandingly acted as the witness for the God of Micah against the idolatrous systems of Christendom and pagandom. On valid grounds this Micah class adopted a resolution in 1931 by which they confessed themselves to be “Jehovah’s witnesses” and pledged themselves to carry out this obligation of being His witnesses. Following that example, congregations of dedicated Christians worldwide, who recognized themselves to be “the Israel of God,” unitedly assented to that 1931 resolution and took upon themselves the Bible-based name “Jehovah’s witnesses.” Not long thereafter an ever-increasing number of truth lovers with earthly hopes joined these Witnesses in making known Jehovah’s name and kingdom. _W 7/15 14_

## Sunday, October 10

_Be glad, you nations, with his people.—Deut. 32:43._

When Paul made and applied his quotation of those words of Moses, about 56 C.E., Jesus had died, been resurrected and ascended to heaven long before, in 33 C.E. (Rom. 15:10) So from Pentecost of that year onward, “his people,” Jehovah’s people, were the dedicated, baptized, spirit-begotten disciples of Jesus Christ. It was on Pentecost that, by means of his glorified royal Son, Jesus Christ, the heavenly Father poured out his holy spirit first on the waiting disciples, about 120 of them at Jerusalem. Thus they were transferred from under the authority of worldly darkness into the spiritual kingdom of God’s beloved Son. (Col. 1:13) That spiritual kingdom was set up by Jehovah God, and in it Jesus Christ as the spiritual King served as Jehovah’s minister of state. In carrying on this ministry he uses his spirit-begotten disciples on earth as “ambassadors substituting for Christ.” (2 Cor. 5:20) Today an increasing crowd of “other sheep” assist these ambassadors in the capacity of envoys. W 6/15 2, 3a

## Monday, October 11

_Clothe yourselves with the tender affections of compassion, kindness, lowliness of mind, mildness, and long-suffering. Continue putting up with one another and forgiving one another freely if anyone has a cause for complaint against another.—Col. 3:12, 13._

For Jehovah to be pleased with the family unit, there must be love shown toward one’s parents. Husbands are told to love their wives as themselves, and wives are to have deep respect

for their husbands. All, parents and children alike, should carry out toward one another the kingly law of love. The apostle Paul gave some pointed counsel on this matter when he wrote the above. Those are not just words to be read or parroted. They are the sayings of holy spirit that are to be considered seriously. It is recommended that every Christian reading these lines go over each one of those Christian requirements. Ponder over them. Think about them in relation to the family of which you are a part. W 10/15 16, 17

## Tuesday, October 12

_“Not by a military force, nor by power, but by my spirit,” Jehovah of armies has said.—Zech. 4:6._

A fine appreciation of the power of God’s holy spirit will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones. Regardless of our natural endowments or attainments, what is important in God’s organization is his holy spirit. That spirit enabled the early Christians to be so effective in their ministry that their enemies complained that these had “overturned the inhabited earth.” (Acts 17:6) It was because of God’s spirit that they could speak with such outspokenness in confronting their religious opposers, unlearned though they were from a natural standpoint. (Acts 4:13) Recognizing that all our brothers have God’s holy spirit will help us to conduct ourselves as lesser ones in relation to them even though we may excel in some abilities from a worldly standpoint. This should make it easier for us to heed the counsel: “In showing honor to one another take the lead.”—Rom. 12:10. W 6/1 8a

## Wednesday, October 13

_If the dead are not to be raised up, “let us eat and drink, for tomorrow we are to die.”—1 Cor. 15:32._

In proclaiming the good news, Paul experienced many dangers and persecutions, and through these he had God’s protection and blessing. If the resurrection were merely a hoax, all of this would have been in vain. In that case, like the Epicurean philosophers, Christians might have indulged themselves by having a good time. However, the resurrection hope and the Messianic kingdom are real. They are intertwined with God’s eternal purpose. Just as Christians in apostolic times were sustained by that hope, so they are sustained today. Our modern history is embellished by the example of thousands of Christians who have faced death unflinchingly, knowing that their integrity assured them of a resurrection into either the new heavens or the new earth. Always their confidence has rested in God’s incoming kingdom. That has been their goal and it should be the goal of each one of us. W 12/15 4, 5a

## Thursday, October 14

_A good man brings forth good out of the good treasure of his heart.—Luke 6:45._

Often, just frankly and unashamedly answering a young child’s questions about sex is sufficient. However, a teenager needs instruction on how to control such desires. To reach the heart, the instruction must come across as friendly help, not as an accusation. In line with Jesus’ words above, to reach your child’s heart requires that you put into that young heart pre-

precious things—things that he or she will respond to emotionally and will cherish. Why? So that good things will come out of that heart. The instruction given about this subject in the Proverbs furnishes a good example for parents. It treats sexual behavior frankly and yet with dignity. Thus, at chapter five the instructor or parent realistically discusses the pleasure of sex relations and especially the need to avoid sexual immorality. Yet this is not an ‘all sex is sin’ discussion. What a beautiful picture he paints of sexual relations within marriage! W 11/1 8-10a

## Friday, October 15

_The prayer of faith will make the indisposed one well, and Jehovah will raise him up. Also, if he has committed sins, it will be forgiven him.—Jas. 5:15._

It displays wisdom and humility on the part of the Christian guilty of grievous sin to approach the elders on his own. “He that is covering over \[‘who hides,’ Lamsa] his transgressions will not succeed, but he that is confessing and leaving them will be shown mercy.” (Prov. 28:13) Sometimes, because of embarrassment, a feeling of guilt or a lack of heartfelt sorrow, a sinner does not approach the elders, as James advises. Any Christian aware of the sin should encourage the wrongdoer to turn away from his error and seek the spiritual help that he needs. If the wrongdoer still will not go to the elders, the other Christian should alert them so that they can provide the needed help. All Christians should want to ‘turn a sinner back from the error of his way’ and thus “save his soul from death.”—Jas. 5:19, 20. W 9/1 7a

### Saturday, October 16

_He that has the bride is the bridegroom. However, the friend of the bridegroom, when he stands and hears him, has a great deal of joy on account of the voice of the bridegroom._—John 3:29.

Yes, just as the heavenly Bridegroom is part of God's spiritual organization so the prospective 'bride' class, begotten of God's spirit while yet on earth, is the visible part of God's spiritual organization. The truth of this situation is strengthened by the fact that this 'bride,' who has a husbandly head, is at the same time the spiritual body of Christ. That is why Paul wrote at Romans 12:5: 'So we, although many, are one body in union with Christ, but members belonging individually to one another.' Since now the Head of that spiritual body, the glorified Jesus Christ, is part of God's spiritual organization, so the members of his 'body' are part of God's organization, only, at present, the visible part of it. It is not amiss to say that they are God's visible organization on earth. See also 1 Corinthians 12:24, _Jerusalem Bible_. W 5/1 10b

### Sunday, October 17

_There are new heavens and a new earth that we are awaiting according to his promise, and in these righteousness is to dwell._—2 Pet. 3:13.

Far from teaching the pagan concept of inherent immortality of the human soul, Jesus showed that any hope for future life depends on the resurrection, even as can be seen from John 5:26-29. Interestingly, some modern-day theologians of Christendom are coming around to the idea that inherent immortality

is unsupported by either the Hebrew or the Christian Greek Scriptures. We do not deny that the Scriptures teach that some Christians receive 'the heavenly calling.' (Heb. 3:1) What we do deny is that such 'heavenly calling' does away with God's original purpose to have the earth cultivated into a paradise and filled with a righteous race of men and women. We are all the more convinced of this because of the promise of 'a new earth' in which 'righteousness is to dwell.' Do you make use of every opportunity to make this good news known to others? W 4/15 2-4a

### Monday, October 18

_Light itself has flashed up for the righteous one, and rejoicing even for the ones upright in heart._—Ps. 97:11.

One thing that distinguishes the righteous, God's servants, from those in Satan's bondage doubtless is the fact that the righteous, Jehovah's servants, enjoy light. For them light has indeed 'flashed up.' As for the wicked, they walk in darkness. Yes, 'the path of the righteous is like the first gleam of dawn, shining ever brighter till the full light of day. But the way of the wicked is like deep darkness; they do not know what makes them stumble.' (Prov. 4:18, 19, _New International Version_) Note that the shining of light on the path of the righteous is progressive. It keeps 'shining ever brighter.' As the light grows brighter and we draw closer to events, our understanding of the outworking of Jehovah God's purposes becomes ever clearer. Prophecies open up to us as God's spirit sheds light upon them, and as they are fulfilled in world events or in the experiences of God's people. W 12/1 1-3

### Tuesday, October 19

_Clothe yourselves with the tender affections of compassion, kindness, lowliness of mind, mildness, and long-suffering._—Col. 3:12.

How do these words affect your relationship with your husband, your wife, your father, your mother or others in the immediate family? Are you pleasing Jehovah in each of these ways mentioned by the apostle? A young Christian cannot conform his life to those words of counsel and at the same time be crying for independence from parental rule or engage in shouting matches with his or her parents. Nor can parents deal in such a way with their children. The happy family that has the blessing of Jehovah must deeply, seriously and regularly consider the admonition found in God's Word, so as to imitate the 'Hearer of prayer,' who by holy spirit inspired such words to be written. Whether there are both a father and a mother in the household, or just one parent, the admonition and the responsibility are the same.—Ps. 65:1, 2; Eph. 4:31, 32. W 10/15 17, 18

### Wednesday, October 20

_Do not be misled: God is not one to be mocked. For whatever a man is sowing, this he will also reap._—Gal. 6:7.

Many persons envy the happy ones, wanting to be that way too. They may try to remedy their unhappiness through artificial means—drugs, alcoholic drinks, illicit sex, indolence—and still wonder why happiness eludes them. Any thrills they get from such pursuits amount to a counterfeit happiness, which, under test, is as worthless as counterfeit money. The evidence of

long history and the experience of millions of persons alive right now prove that we cannot turn our backs on moral standards or principles and yet be lastingly happy. Those who have 'come to be past all moral sense, giving themselves over to loose conduct to work uncleanness of every sort with greediness' are not the happy ones. (Eph. 4:17-19; Rom. 13:13) Such a course, either immediately or later, brings additional sorrows, throwing up barriers on the road to happiness. We cannot escape the truth of the words above. W 4/1 6, 7

### Thursday, October 21

_He gave some as apostles, some as prophets, some as evangelizers, some as shepherds and teachers, with a view to the readjustment of the holy ones, for ministerial work._—Eph. 4:11, 12.

In the showdown 'war of the great day of God the Almighty' at Armageddon, the officiating 'ministers' of the political states there arrayed against God the Almighty will be destroyed with their earthly governments. Those who are non-political 'ministers' of the victorious Almighty God, Jehovah, will be preserved by him through that war of all wars with which the present system of earthly governments will end. What a grand reward that will be for their having kept on faithfully in their Christian ministry to him! Their being called 'ministers' may be objected to by some. However, from the standpoint of the Bible in its original languages, a person who dedicates himself wholly to God and devotes himself to doing the will of Jehovah God certainly is a 'minister' of Jehovah God, in imitation of Jesus Christ. W 3/15 7, 9

## Friday, October 22

_Become steadfast, unmovable._
—1 Cor. 15:58.

The apostle Paul exhorts us to become "unmovable." This is akin to being steadfast. It means that, in observing God's commandments, we must never be shaken in our integrity. That was the attitude of the exemplary integrity-keeper Job, who said of Jehovah his God and Judge: "After he has tested me out, I shall come forth as gold itself." Can we express such confidence? We can, if we are able to say of Jehovah, as did Job: "His way I have kept, and I do not deviate. From the commandment of his lips I do not move away. I have treasured up the sayings of his mouth more than what is prescribed for me." (Job 23:10-12) If we, like Job, will go even beyond what is scheduled for our regular meetings, digging down deep into the Watch Tower Society's publications for further enlightenment on the modern-day application of Jehovah God's Word, we will be helped, indeed, in remaining steadfast, unmovable in the truth. W 12/15 13a

## Saturday, October 23

_God assigned us, not to wrath, but to the acquiring of salvation through our Lord Jesus Christ._
—1 Thess. 5:9.

God's servants have full confidence that he will keep his organization well informed, so that they can take the proper steps for survival. We can be sure of this because Revelation, chapter seven, shows that "a great crowd, which no man was able to number, out of all nations and tribes and peoples and tongues, . . . come out of the great tribulation" and survive into God's

new order where "God will wipe out every tear from their eyes." (Rev. 7:9, 14, 17) And why does the "great crowd" survive? Because "they are rendering God sacred service day and night," and so he "spreads out his protective tent over them." (Rev. 7:15) Being properly informed, they know how to serve God acceptably. They do not wait passively to see what will happen. They know that the one who "does the will of God remains forever." (1 John 2:17) That will includes preaching the good news of the kingdom. W 11/15 14, 15a

## Sunday, October 24

_Your people will offer themselves willingly on the day of your military force. In the splendors of holiness, from the womb of the dawn, you have your company of young men just like dewdrops._
—Ps. 110:3.

The responsibility to be "witnesses . . . to the most distant part of the earth" rests upon all Christians, young and old. The prophetic 110th Psalm says the foregoing about Christ. Another Messianic psalm speaks of "virgins" who would be "companions" of Christ's bride. (Ps. 45:13, 14) Both of these passages apply to the anointed remnant and to the "great crowd" that literally includes you "young men" and "virgins." So you, too, must "offer yourselves willingly" and be loyal "companions" to the remaining ones of the anointed bride class still on earth, also symbolized by the "four angels" who are directing the "armies of cavalry" against Satan's religious world empire. (Rev. 9:15-19; 21:2, 9) Those who regularly render such "sacred service" to Jehovah can testify to the joy and contentment resulting therefrom. W 3/1 15, 16a

## Monday, October 25

_We have this treasure in earthen vessels, that the power beyond what is normal may be God's and not that out of ourselves._—2 Cor. 4:7.

If a healthy fear of Jehovah is instilled from an early age, then the child will develop a good conscience, and then in the face of temptation will feel as Joseph did. (Gen. 39:7-9) By working together in the Christian ministry, parents will help their children to cultivate the same tender interest in people that Jehovah has. As the child grows in appreciation, its heart will see how it can make many 'rich' by teaching them the good news and seeing the beneficial changes in their lives. This ministry is also a fine aid in developing a close relationship with God. Because of the problems facing Christian parents, they do indeed need "power beyond what is normal." Yes, look to Jehovah for help; rely on him. Pray with your children and for them. See Jehovah's hand at work in your family. Do all you can to reach your child's heart.
—3 John 4. W 11/1 23-27a

## Tuesday, October 26

_In accord with the Holy One who called you, do you also become holy yourselves in all your conduct, because it is written: "You must be holy, because I am holy."_
—1 Pet. 1:15, 16.

After the Christian congregation was formed, it replaced the Jewish nation in having God's name upon it. Accordingly, Christians could rightly be expected to uphold Jehovah's righteousness. Jehovah loves his people and wants to protect the purity of the Christian congre-

gation. So he outlined a provision to reject or expel a person who persists in a course that dishonors God and endangers the congregation. Paul advised: "As for a man that promotes a sect, reject him after a first and a second admonition; knowing that such a man has been turned out of the way and is sinning, he being self-condemned." (Titus 3:10, 11) Yes, elders first try lovingly to help a wrongdoer. If he will not respond, love for God and for the congregation requires that the elders reject him. W 9/15 4, 5a

## Wednesday, October 27

_Honor your father and your mother in order that your days may prove long upon the ground that Jehovah your God is giving you._—Ex. 20:12.

"Honor your father and your mother": The apostle Paul in discussing this, the fifth of the Ten Commandments, said it was "the first command with a promise," namely, "that it may go well with you and you may endure a long time on the earth." (Eph. 6:2, 3) A child claiming to be a Christian is under obligation to be obedient to the command to honor his father and mother. How does a child honor, or esteem, and manifest respect for his parents? Certainly that child must show love for his parents and appreciation for the effort they are making to care for and train such a child. Children have to learn to respect the judgment and decisions of their parents. (Prov. 22:15) Even when the children may think that their individual rights are being interfered with, they are obliged to be obedient to their "parents in union with the Lord."—Eph. 6:1. W 10/15 11

<sup>**Page 184**</sup>

[Open Page 184 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1982%20Yearbook%20%28yb82-E%29.pdf#page=184)

## Thursday, October 28

_By faith Noah . . . constructed an ark for the saving of his household.—Heb. 11:7._

Bible history helps us to understand the relationship between God's love and his vengeance. Lovingly, God had permitted the offspring of Adam to multiply. But wayward humans did not respond to that love. So when 'the earth came to be ruined in the sight of the true God,' what would God do? (Gen. 6:11, 12) Would he exact vengeance? Yes! But even in this, his quality of love came to the fore, because at that time, there was one family on earth devoted to doing God's will. It was the family of Noah, the man who is called 'a preacher of righteousness.' (2 Pet. 2:5) Lovingly, Jehovah had Noah constructed an ark 'for the saving of his household.' Then came the global flood. The entire earth was cleansed of ruinous violence and immorality, so that it was again a fit place for the families of mankind to increase. How thankful we can be that in the near future Jehovah God will take similar action! W 1/15 11, 12

## Friday, October 29

_All those in the memorial tombs will hear his voice and come out, those who did good things to a resurrection of life.—John 5:28, 29._

No doubt the great crowd will be joyful as they move out into the cleansed earth and welcome resurrected faithful ones. Then, in orderly arrangement, the billions of mankind in the 'memorial tombs' will come forth on earth in a general resurrection. As the society of the new earth begins to function, and as Christ

and his corulers apply the benefits of Christ's ransom sacrifice in healing mankind and lifting persons to perfection, what rejoicing there will be among the happy, united families of mankind! One thousand years will pass like one day, at least from Jehovah's standpoint. The earth will be filled with perfect humanity, just as Jehovah purposed when he first created man, some 7,000 years earlier. His 7,000-year day of rest will be at its end, and it will not have been in vain, for his grand purpose toward our earth will have been fulfilled. W 12/15 17, 18

## Saturday, October 30

_Symeon \[Peter] has related thoroughly how God for the first time turned his attention to the \[Gentiles] to take out of them a people for his name.—Acts 15:14._

The 'steward' class is a people for Jehovah's name. (Luke 12:42) This fact was emphasized some time after the year 36 C.E., at a special meeting of the apostles and the elders of the Jerusalem congregation. There James, the half brother of Jesus Christ, said the above. Such non-Jews became part of the first-century 'steward' class. As a result, this class was the anointed people for God's name, which name is Jehovah. God's name, Jehovah, was called upon them. They could not dodge being his witnesses. This also must be true of the anointed remnant of the 'steward' class today, when the 'sign' visible since 1914 indicates that the returned master of the 'steward' class is present and that 'the conclusion of the system of things' is about to reach its climax. Assisting them is the 'great crowd' of other sheep. W 10/1 22, 23

## Sunday, October 31

_Preach, saying, 'The kingdom of the heavens has drawn near.' . . . You received free, give free.—Matt. 10:7, 8._

Jesus 'set out on a tour . . . preaching the good news of the kingdom.' (Matt. 9:35) When crowds wanted him to stay with them longer and tried to detain him, he said: 'To other cities I must declare the good news of the kingdom of God.' (Luke 4:43) When he saw the crowds that had been spiritually 'skinned and thrown about,' he was 'moved with pity for them,

because they were as sheep without a shepherd. And he started to teach them many things.' (Matt. 9:36; Mark 6:34) We now live in the critical last days of this satanic system. 'The time left is reduced.' It is urgent for us to declare the 'good news.' (1 Cor. 7:29; 9:16) Whatever may be Jehovah's time to bring the end to this wicked system, never forget this: NOW while you are living; NOW before unpredictable death may take you; NOW is your time to share in preaching the 'good news' and to make a good name with God! W 2/15 19, 20a

## Monday, November 1

_You became imitators of us and of the Lord.—1 Thess. 1:6._

The greatest protection against immorality is for a youth to develop a personal intimate relationship with Jehovah. Even though this is what the child itself must do, a parent can help. First of all, your own example of devotion will give a living pattern to be imitated. Those who became Christians in Thessalonica saw 'what sort of men' Paul and his companions were and 'became imitators,' developing similar 'strong conviction.' (1 Thess. 1:4-6) What 'sort of person' do your children see in you? Do they see your 'strong conviction,' noting that you are building your entire life around your devotion to God and are making sacrifices for his worship? Do they see your strong dislike of immorality by your not being entertained by what is morally corrupt? Do they see an example of love in the way you treat your mate or deal with others? Do they hear you talk about Jehovah in a way that shows he is real to you? Also, guard your children's association. W 11/1 19, 20a

## Tuesday, November 2

_I shall give to peoples the change to a pure language, in order . . . to serve \[Jehovah] shoulder to shoulder.—Zeph. 3:9._

When we make mistakes, as all imperfect humans do, let us be ready to acknowledge them, even as the 'faithful and discreet slave,' made up of imperfect men, has had to make corrections. However, let us never be critical of the grand body of truth that Jehovah has built up among his united people over the past 100 years, and which, by correction and adjustment, has come to shine ever more brightly on 'the path of the righteous.' (Prov. 4:18) From time to time, there have arisen from among our ranks those who have adopted an independent, faultfinding attitude. They do not want to serve 'shoulder to shoulder' with the worldwide brotherhood. Rather, they present a 'stubborn shoulder.' (Zech. 7:11) They say it is sufficient to read the Bible exclusively. But strangely, their doing so has caused them to revert to the apostate teachings of the clergy. W 8/15 13, 14a

### Wednesday, November 3

_Because sentence against a bad work has not been executed speedily, that is why the heart of the sons of men has become fully set in them to do bad.—Eccl. 8:11._

Each judicial committee should be very concerned about keeping the congregation clean and should exercise particular care if the wrongdoer has secretly carried on gross sin over a long period. The same would be true if they were dealing with a person who had established a pattern of sinning and then seemingly had repented. A number of times he may have done wrong but because he appeared repentant he was reproved each time and was allowed to remain in the congregation. Now he has sinned again. In such cases the elders, having in mind also the welfare of the whole flock, must consider whether his life gives true evidence that he is producing fruit that befits repentance. Has he not by his way of life shown that it is very questionable that he belongs in God's congregation? Might the above words of Solomon apply to him? _W 9/1 23a_

### Thursday, November 4

_Your word is a lamp to my foot, and a light to my roadway.—Ps. 119:105._

God's Word has ever served in this way. But Jehovah has also provided his visible organization, his 'faithful and discreet slave,' to help Christians in all nations to understand and to apply the Bible properly in their lives. Unless we are in touch with this channel of communication that God is using, we will not progress along the road to life, no matter how much Bible

reading we do. (Compare Acts 8:30-40.) Regarding God's channel of communication, Jesus said that the 'faithful and discreet slave' would provide spiritual nourishment at the right time for all of his followers and that he would set this 'slave' over all his belongings. (Matt. 24:45-47) Thus Paul, at Ephesians 4:11-16, indicated that the Christian congregation needed not only such inspired instruments as apostles and prophets, but also evangelizers, shepherds and teachers to help Christians to gain spiritual maturity. _W 12/1 4, 5b_

### Friday, November 5

_Pay attention to yourselves that your hearts never become weighed down . . . and suddenly that day be instantly upon you.—Luke 21:34._

Any people of the nations who want to live in God's paradisaic new order must now stream to Jehovah's mountain, his true worship, which is firmly established and is exalted high above any other type of worship. (Isa. 2:2) Why is there such an urgency about coming to Jehovah's true worship now? Could not a sincere person wait until he actually sees Jehovah's executional judgments beginning, and then quickly come over to His side? While that might sound appealing to some, would there then be enough time to build the right relationship with Jehovah? The evidence from the Scriptures answers, No, even as Jesus warned. Note that those who are not awake spiritually are caught unawares, for that 'day' comes 'suddenly,' 'instantly,' in just the same way that 'sudden destruction is to be instantly upon' those who are saying 'Peace and security!' _W 11/15 3, 4a_

### Saturday, November 6

_A watchman is what I have made you to the house of Israel, and at my mouth you must hear the word and give them warning from me.—Ezek. 33:7._

Christendom has failed to heed the warning sounded by Jehovah's people. What if she had joined them in sounding the warning? What a difference this would have made in human affairs! Jehovah knew beforehand that she would need to have his final warning served upon her. He knew what the history of his chosen people of pre-Christian times foreshadowed. In the year 613 B.C.E., he raised up a Jew named Ezekiel to be a prophet to his people. So even though he was then an exile in Babylon, Ezekiel was Jehovah's 'watchman' to Israel. His prophesyings were carried southwest to Jerusalem in the land of Judah. What Jehovah said to Ezekiel back there is of interest to us today, for he pictured Jehovah's anointed witnesses of today. They, together with their companions, have acted upon what God said to Ezekiel. Are you sharing in this work as much as you can? _W 2/1 3, 4a_

### Sunday, November 7

_We are working hard and exerting ourselves, because we have rested our hope on a living God, who is a Savior of all sorts of men, especially of faithful ones.—1 Tim. 4:10._

Think of that. Salvation from God—everlasting life in happiness—is open to 'all sorts of men.' But who finally will be saved? It is only the 'faithful ones,' those who develop and exercise faith. So the apostle Paul could not take it easy, living a rather normal life and

sometimes, if it was convenient, speak to a few others about this possible salvation. No! The prospect that others could learn the Christian message and come onto the way of salvation was so overwhelmingly important to Paul that he was 'working hard and exerting himself.' Reflect on your course of activity over the past month or six months. Is it fully plain to you—and to others, including God—that you are working hard, exerting yourself? When this work is concluded, will you be satisfied that you had a full share in it? _W 1/1 11, 20_

### Monday, November 8

_Do the work of an evangelizer, fully accomplish your ministry.—2 Tim. 4:5._

Dedicated, baptized witnesses of Jehovah are ministers of the greatest government in existence. They are ministers of the Sovereign of the Universe, the Creator of heaven and earth. In fulfillment of Matthew 24:14, they are the appointed heralds of the Messianic kingdom. Their ministry will not end with the catastrophic end of the old system of things. Paul could hardly have had in mind the common, ordinary services performed by worldly men in general, when he wrote Archippus: 'Keep watching the ministry which you accepted in the Lord, that you fulfill it.' (Col. 4:17) During his final imprisonment Paul wrote to Timothy, as above. These inspired instructions to faithful ministers of the primitive congregation are excellent admonitions for all dedicated, baptized witnesses of Jehovah today to apply to themselves in this 'time of the end' of the doomed old system of things, to God's eternal glory.—Dan. 12:4. _W 3/15 19, 20_

### Tuesday, November 9

_Quit mixing in company . . . not even eating with such a man._
—1 Cor. 5:11.

Disfellowshipping implies more than ceasing to have spiritual fellowship. The Bible here rules out social fellowship, too, such as joining an expelled person in a picnic or sitting down to a meal with him. Sometimes a Christian might feel under considerable pressure to ignore this Bible advice. His own emotions may create the pressure, or it may be brought to bear on him by acquaintances. But to yield to such pressure would be to compromise. Other problems arise in connection with business or employment. Because of contractual or financial obligations, discussions of business matters with a disfellowshipped person might be necessary, but spiritual discussions and social fellowship would be things of the past. In that way you could demonstrate your obedience to God and have a protective barrier for yourself. Also, this might impress on him how much his sin has cost him in various ways. W 9/15 17-20a

### Wednesday, November 10

_You must lift up prayer in behalf of the remnant that are to be found._—Isa. 37:4.

King Hezekiah, feeling utterly helpless, appealed to the prophet Isaiah. (Isa. 36:1-37:7) Here we see the same typical features that enter into the critical situation of today. Correspondingly Sennacherib the king of Assyria typifies the foe of God's kingdom, namely, Satan the Devil, who demands the surrender of Jehovah's organized people under pain of enslavement or destruction. The anointed king

Hezekiah, whose name means "Jah has strengthened," typifies the now-reigning King, Jesus Christ, who feels very keenly the distress of his loyal disciples on earth as these suffer reproaches and face the threat of destruction at the hand of the Devil's organization. Eliakim, the "steward" over the household of King Hezekiah, typifies the remnant of the "steward" class yet on earth during this "conclusion of the system of things" since 1914. Associated with this "steward" class are the "great crowd" of "other sheep."—John 10:16; Rev. 7:9. W 10/1 3, 4a

### Thursday, November 11

_Observe, O my son, the commandment of your father, and do not forsake the law of your mother. Tie them upon your heart constantly; bind them upon your throat._—Prov. 6:20, 21.

Authority must be recognized. Closely akin to the honoring of one's parents is the recognition of authority, the parents' right to set certain limits and to determine what their children may or may not do. Submission to authority is often very difficult for children and may cause many disputes in the household. The news media and other sources often present information that undermines parental authority, encouraging youths to demand freedom from parental authority. A case in point is the attitude taken today toward promiscuity. This situation has developed to the point that often young men or women who maintain virginity are thought ill of by their contemporaries. Such persons consider those holding to the Bible's high standards to be lacking in some way, to be unattractive. —See 1 Peter 4:4. W 10/15 12

### Friday, November 12

_Men were tortured because they would not accept release by some ransom, in order that they might attain a better resurrection._—Heb. 11:35.

The 144,001 are not the only ones to be resurrected in proper order. Hebrews 11:40 tells us that their resurrection is to "something better," a heavenly spiritual estate. This is better than what? Why, better than the estate attained in the resurrection that takes place next in order! This resurrection must include those who will be made "princes in all the earth," and who reasonably will be among the first to be resurrected on earth following Har-Magedon. Thus they will be able to take up their assigned duties in the new earth—the theocratic society of God's people in the cleansed earth. Theirs is a "better resurrection," for it takes place under God's kingdom, with prospect of everlasting life for the resurrected ones. In this time group we expect to find Christians who have an earthly hope and who die prior to the coming in of the new earth. W 12/15 15

### Saturday, November 13

_Get out of her, my people, if you do not want to share with her in her sins, and if you do not want to receive part of her plagues._—Rev. 18:4.

At a time when false religion thinks she is sitting pretty, the "ten horns" of the symbolic "wild beast" will devastate her. (Rev. 17:16, 17) And as God's hour for judgment of her approaches, how is God expressing his love? In this way: to all sincere people of all false religions who want to become

God's people, God extends the above urgent invitation. Without a doubt, the execution of God's judgment draws ever nearer! Antireligious nations already wield great power in the United Nations. Oil-hungry militaristic countries threaten the Moslem world as well as those nations long regarded as the bulwark of Christendom's religions. That is why it is now high time for all of Jehovah's people to help all lovers of truth and righteousness to get well away from the "famous prostitute," yes, to flee to Jehovah God's kingdom. Are you having a full share in this lifesaving work? W 1/15 18-20a

### Sunday, November 14

_Do not think I came to destroy the Law or the Prophets. I came, not to destroy, but to fulfill._—Matt. 5:17.

Since Jesus came to make the teachings of the prophets come true, his coming was a guarantee that their prophecies concerning the restoration of paradise on earth would be fulfilled. (Here are just a few: Psalms 37:11, 29; 72:1-8, 16-19; Isaiah 9:6, 7; 11:1-10.) Also in the Sermon on the Mount, Jesus showed quite clearly that the earth is due to play a part in the outworking of the divine will or purpose. He taught his followers to pray: "Let your kingdom come. Let your will take place, as in heaven, also upon earth." (Matt. 6:9, 10) He linked the accomplishment of God's will on earth with the coming of God's kingdom. Hence, the Lord's Prayer, repeated literally millions of times through the years, is, in fact, among other things, a prayer for the fulfillment of the Messianic promises tied in with the millennial hope. What a privilege is ours to make the Kingdom hope known to others! W 4/15 8, 9
