## Monday, November 15

_Strip off the old personality with its practices, and clothe yourselves with the new personality, which through accurate knowledge is being made new.—Col. 3:9, 10._

The force that actuates our mind and leads us to God's favor is the accurate knowledge of his Word the Bible. To grimly set about stopping the forbidden things does not always work. What if you succeeded in stopping them for a time? You are still not safe, even as Jesus showed by an illustration. An unclean spirit left a man, its 'house,' and later returned. Finding the house unoccupied, it moved in with seven other spirits. It was not enough for the house to be emptied of the wicked spirit; it needed to be filled with good so that the wicked spirits could not reenter. (Matt. 12:43-45) It is not enough to stop the doing of what is wrong and leave a vacuum—we must start the doing of what is right. Crowd out the wrongdoing by occupying yourself with the doing of good. _W 2/15 3, 4a_

## Tuesday, November 16

_The fruitage of the spirit is...self-control...Moreover, those who belong to Christ Jesus impaled the flesh together with its passions and desires.—Gal. 5:22-24._

Yes, youths, cultivate the fruit of God's spirit, self-control. By controlling your body you will be able to look back with no regrets. Think of the joy of the young Shulammite girl and her shepherd lover when eventually they were united in wedlock. With the help of others, and

by their own efforts, they conquered their bodily desires and remained chaste. Although they had spoken words of endearment, they had not been immoral before entering wedlock, thereby diminishing the pleasure they would enjoy after adjusting to each other in marriage. How different from the passionate Ammon who could not wait and whose body led him as a slave into immorality! Truly, self-control is the course of wisdom. Bible morality is the best way!—Song of Sol. 2:16; 4:16; 5:1; 2 Sam. 13:1, 2, 10-16. _W 11/1 19_

## Wednesday, November 17

_If we confess our sins, he is faithful and righteous so as to forgive us our sins.—1 John 1:9._

Even though God recognizes that we are all sinners, he wants to help us and is willing to forgive. Our concept of God, though, would not be accurate if we did not accept his whole view of sin. Whereas Psalm 103 assures us that Jehovah 'is forgiving all \[our] error,' it also indicates our obligations, saying: 'The loving-kindness of Jehovah is from time indefinite even to time indefinite toward those fearing him, . . . toward those remembering his orders so as to carry them out.' (Ps. 103:3, 17, 18) If a person commits grave sin, does not repent and seek God's fatherly mercy, but continues to pursue sin, what then? Exodus 34:6, 7 gives us a description of Jehovah. After stressing his mercy, his slowness to anger and willingness to pardon error, it adds: 'But by no means will he give exemption from punishment.' Hence, we may not presume on his mercy or take it for granted. _W 9/1 2, 3a_

## Thursday, November 18

_In connection with everything give thanks. For this is the will of God in union with Christ Jesus respecting you.—1 Thess. 5:18._

Gratitude is beneficial both for the recipient and for the person who expresses it, who has developed the spirit of thankfulness. Today most persons are primarily concerned about self. That is contrary to the spirit of gratitude, in the showing of which a person thinks of others and readily expresses appreciation for what others do. The paradox is, though, that if you work at cultivating a sincere interest in others and freely express your appreciation for their services, deeds and efforts, you will be happier. Furthermore, developing a spirit of gratitude can have a bearing on your relationship with Jehovah God. It can affect your overall happiness in life now and the extent to which you will share in 'the happy hope' that is held out to Christians. (Titus 2:13) As noted above, the Bible urges us to cultivate a spirit of gratitude or thankfulness. _W 4/1 2-4a_

## Friday, November 19

_There were a thousand thousands that kept ministering to him, and ten thousand times ten thousand that kept standing right before him.—Dan. 7:10._

Genesis 3:24 and Job 38:6, 7 tell of spirit creatures in God's heavenly household. Psalm 80:1 says that Jehovah is 'sitting upon the cherubs' and in vision the prophet Isaiah saw Jehovah on his throne and attended by seraphs. Then there are the angels in general and 'Michael the archangel.' (Jude 9; Dan. 10:13) In the light of these Scriptural

references, including the above, what do we find spelled out for us by the fact that God's heavenly personnel includes an archangel, princes, cherubs, seraphs, angels, all these ranks and orders or levels of being and 'armies' acting in perfect harmony with full subjection to the Ancient of Days, Jehovah God? It spells out, it calls for, an organizing of all those in God's heavenly personnel. It constitutes all such heavenly personnel God's organization. It means that God has an organization. _W 5/1 7-9, 11, 12a_

## Saturday, November 20

_Jesus went into Galilee, preaching the good news of God and saying: 'The appointed time has been fulfilled, and the kingdom of God has drawn near.'—Mark 1:14, 15._

Prior to his earthly sojourn, Jesus had acted as the Word (or, _Logos_), the mouthpiece of our heavenly Father. So Jesus knew of the prophecies recorded at Genesis 3:15 and Isaiah 9:6, 7. He was aware of the words directed to Abraham. (Gen. 12:3; 22:17, 18) Additionally, he was conscious of the fact that he would be the one to fulfill the wonderful promises related to the promised seed and the Kingdom government. During Jesus' earthly sojourn and particularly during his three-and-a-half-year ministry, he drew attention to that kingly role. Such expressions as 'the kingdom of God is in your midst' and the one given above were heard from his lips. By means of his many illustrations and parables, Jesus drew attention to that kingdom. It is our privilege to direct truth lovers to that kingdom.—Luke 17:21. _W 5/15 6, 7_

## Sunday, November 21

_As substitutes for Christ we beg: 'Become reconciled to God.' —2 Cor. 5:20._

The Kingdom ambassadors take a strictly neutral stand toward worldly conflicts. They know that the times of the Gentiles, or the appointed times of the nations, ended in the early fall of the year 1914, during the third month of World War I. At that time the lease of the Gentile nations for exercising world domination ran out. Such worldly nations, including those of Christendom, stubbornly refuse to recognize that fact. So they oppose and persecute the Christian witnesses of Jehovah who call attention to that fact. All such persecution is just as was foretold in Bible prophecies. In spite of it all, the ambassadors of God's established kingdom keep on rejoicing. They invite the people of all the nations to rejoice with them and to join with them in announcing the kingdom that he has laid on the shoulder of Jesus Christ. As a result, a great crowd are now sharing in this blessed work. _W 6/15 7a_

## Monday, November 22

_Euodia I exhort and Syntyche I exhort to be of the same mind in the Lord. Yes, I request you too, genuine yokefellow, keep assisting these women who have striven side by side with me in the good news.—Phil. 4:2, 3._

In the Christian congregation today there may be a little rivalry, or a little feminine jealousy may surface, especially between those who might have similar endowments or advantages. This seems to have been the situation in the congregation

tion at Philippi. Quite likely both these sisters were particularly able and zealous preachers of the 'good news,' so that a spirit of rivalry entered their hearts, causing friction. In like manner, there may be problems at times among brothers having similar appointments. In the Christian organization, by and large, ministerial servants have no difficulty in conducting themselves as lesser ones in relation to the elders; elders in relation to the circuit overseer, and so forth. But the test comes in relation to peers. _W 6/1 11, 12_

## Tuesday, November 23

_The heart is more treacherous than anything else and is desperate. Who can know it? I, Jehovah, am searching the heart, examining the kidneys, even to give to each one according to his ways. —Jer. 17:9, 10._

Jehovah God knows the motive and is aware of any deceit or malicious scheming on the part of persons who violate his law on marriage. Yes, 'all things are naked and openly exposed to the eyes of him with whom we have an accounting.' (Heb. 4:13) However, in this regard it must be observed that God's law does not require an innocent mate to obtain a divorce. Circumstances may make it a fine thing for the innocent mate to forgive the guilty one, especially if that one is humbly and sincerely repentant. True, unfaithfulness on the part of one's mate might be quite a humiliating experience—that one's mate has looked elsewhere for pleasure and satisfaction. But many a loving wife and mother has put up with a selfish or even an unfaithful husband for the sake of her children. _W 7/1 15, 16a_

## Wednesday, November 24

_I shall certainly let remain in the midst of you a people humble and lowly, and they will actually take refuge in the name of Jehovah.—Zeph. 3:12._

Through his prophet Zephaniah, Jehovah tells His people how He will dispose of those who shamelessly try to sow discord in His earthly organization. (Zeph. 3:11) Then in happy contrast, Jehovah says the above. Yes, these are the meek ones, who work 'shoulder to shoulder' as they engage in 'the holy work of the good news.' (Rom. 15:15, 16) They are not too proud to do the lowly work of calling from house to house after the pattern that Jesus' disciples learned from the Master. (Matt. 10:5-13) Taking refuge in Jehovah's name, they proclaim that glorious name and his purpose to vindicate it by the triumphant kingdom of his Christ. As Jehovah's people serve and live according to his righteousness, they are confident that 'there will be no one making them tremble.' (Zeph. 3:13) They enjoy true peace of mind! _W 8/15 15a_

## Thursday, November 25

_The hand of Jehovah was with them, and a great number that became believers turned to the Lord.—Acts 11:21._

Once Peter had used another of the 'keys of the kingdom' to unlock Kingdom opportunities to the uncircumcised, in 36 C.E., the way was open to carry the Christian witness to all peoples, yes! 'away to the ends of the earth.' (Acts 1:8, _The New English Bible_) Apparently, systematic witnessing among the uncircumcised Gentiles first got under way in Syrian Antioch, at that

time the third largest city in the known world, after Rome and Alexandria. The zealous preaching activities of Christians from Cyprus and Cyrene among the non-Jews were blessed by Jehovah. (Acts 11:20-26) For 10 years or so Syrian Antioch became a center from which intense missionary activities were carried out, under the direction of the holy spirit. Paul, together with various fellow missionaries, undertook three extensive witnessing tours. The modern missionary work under the direction of the Watch Tower Society has likewise been greatly blessed. _W 3/1 14-16_

## Friday, November 26

_Unrighteous persons will not inherit God's kingdom.—1 Cor. 6:9._

When a Christian gives himself over to sin and has to be disfellowshipped, he forfeits much: his approved standing with God; membership in the happy congregation of Christians; sweet fellowship with the brothers. The pain he has caused may even survive him. So all of us need to appreciate that it is Jehovah's judgment that counts. The Bible shows that there are things that God hates. (Prov. 6:16-19) But it is also true as to his judgment of persons. His Word plainly says that 'unrighteous persons,' those carrying on the 'works of the flesh,' will not inherit his kingdom. (Gal. 5:19-21) Such persons have no place in heaven, nor will they fit in the earthly realm of the Kingdom. Accordingly, anyone who wants to remain in the clean congregation of God today must meet His standards. God simply will not permit 'leaven' to remain as a corrupting influence among his holy people.—1 Cor. 5:6-13. _W 9/15 25, 27b_

## Saturday, November 27

_This good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness.—Matt. 24:14._

World War I was a time of darkness such as humanity had never before experienced. It was as if the powers of darkness had prevailed. Even the worship of Jehovah God was at a low ebb. It appeared as if the end had come—for the whole system of things on earth. Worshipers of the God of the Bible resigned themselves to that view of matters, as they became “objects of hatred by all the nations.” (Matt. 24:9) However, they had only entered “the conclusion of the system of things.” (Matt. 24:3) There was yet much life-saving work for them to do before they reached the terminal of that “conclusion of the system of things.” The words that the Great Governor out of Bethlehem had foretold, at Matthew 24:14, were being fulfilled. The prophecy at Micah 4:1-4 was in agreement with that, although written many hundreds of years beforehand. Are you having a share in fulfilling that prophecy? _W 7/15_ 1-3a

## Sunday, November 28

_I will put the key of the house of David upon his shoulder, and he must open without anyone’s shutting, and he must shut without anyone’s opening.—Isa. 22:22._

Especially since 1935 have the “great crowd” of “other sheep” benefited from the fatherly care of the modern Eliakim class. (Isa. 22:20, 21) And heavy responsibility has been laid upon this class of today similar to that described above. The composite “steward” class has shown itself worthy of shouldering the royal

“key of the house of David.” It has kept alert to safeguard the earthly interests of God’s kingdom as pictured by the “house of David.” It welcomed in those whom the Master chose to make up the final members of the remnant of Kingdom heirs. In 1935 it began to welcome in the “other sheep” whom the Fine Shepherd was pleased to bring together to form “one flock” with the anointed remnant. It shut the door on all the expelled apostates and those who tried to sneak in to corrupt Jehovah’s Witnesses. _W 10/1_ 13, 14a

## Monday, November 29

_It is a means of great gain, this godly devotion along with \[contentment].—1 Tim. 6:6._

The sharing of the good news with others has beneficial results, besides giving a person the satisfaction of knowing that he is doing what Jehovah has commanded. It gives one proper goals to work toward in life. The pursuit of selfish goals by the great majority of young people does not bring genuine and lasting happiness to them. The pursuit of a goal that is pleasing to Jehovah brings godliness with contentment, the most worthwhile goal of all. While the world in general searches for contentment, godly families in the Christian congregation find it well within reach, if only they will seize it. There are many avenues of satisfaction available. For example, there is some form of the pioneer service. Pioneers find great contentment in having such a full share in the Kingdom proclamation. This form of “sacred service” brings them many blessings from Jehovah. At times, family members can arrange for one of their number to pioneer. _W 10/15_ 9, 10a

## Tuesday, November 30

_Is there anyone sick among you? Let him call the older men \[or, elders] of the congregation to him, and let them pray over him . . . And the prayer of faith will make the indisposed one well, and Jehovah will raise him up.—Jas. 5:14, 15._

In the case of grave sin, God wisely advises that a Christian ‘confessing his sins’ ought to take an additional step, even as the disciple James wrote in a context that evidently refers to spiritual sickness involving sins. This step is reasonable, for a Christian

who has given in to grave sin displays a measure of spiritual weakness and a need for help. He could benefit from the prayers of faith of the elders. They are in position, also, to offer him Biblical counsel and help him so that he can regain spiritual strength. Further, some transgressions amount to sins against the congregation, for they bring reproach and sorrow upon God’s people. This makes it even more appropriate that such a sinner seek the elders’ help.—2 Cor. 2:10. _W 9/1_ 5, 6a

## Wednesday, December 1

_Keep your way far off from alongside her, and do not get near to the entrance of her house.—Prov. 5:8._

When the body craves being around those with loose morals, lead it away from such association, even as above recommended. Of course, while in school, young persons are plunged into association with many immoral persons. But do you socialize with them? The Scriptures indicate that some immoral persons would slip into the Christian congregation. Therefore, be on guard. If you believe any may be of this sort, refer them to the elders for spiritual assistance. Thus you will be showing them genuine love as well as perhaps protecting others. True, some worldly immoral persons may mock you for your chaste stand. But, just think! Should you let persons who are slaves of corruption—slaves to their own selfish passions—make you feel embarrassed? Who has the greater strength—the immoral persons, or the chaste virgin who can say of her moral strength, “I am a wall”?—Song of Sol. 8:10. _W 11/1_ 17, 18

## Thursday, December 2

_Sorrow is better than laughter; for by the sadness of the countenance the heart is made better. The heart of the wise is in the house of mourning; but the heart of fools is in the house of mirth.—Eccl. 7:3, 4, Authorized Version._

Rather than consume time in frivolous merriment, it is better to examine your life, face mistakes made in the past and be saddened by wrongs committed. It will improve your heart and move you to change your ways and start acting wisely, rather than laughing and giggling your way through life like an irresponsible fool. Furthermore, “it is better to hear the rebuke of the wise, than for a man to hear the song of fools.” (Eccl. 7:5, AV) Contact with death, as at a funeral, is a sobering experience and should cause a person to reflect on his own course in life. It may also put him in a frame of mind to listen to wise counselors. Criticism, even when kindly given, is difficult to endure, but it is better than listening to the song or the “praise of fools.” (_The New English Bible_) _W 2/15_ 6, 7

### Friday, December 3

_Lord, whom shall we go away to? You have sayings of everlasting life.—John 6:68._

That the development of understanding does not always go forward in a wholly straight line has often served as a test of loyalty for those associated with the “faithful and discreet slave.” (Matt. 24:45) However, progress is being made continually toward fuller appreciation of the “good news” and all that it means. It has been the experience of those who stay close to God’s organization that questions and things hard to understand are always cleared up with the passing of time. And as the light shines forth ever more clearly, how heartwarming and satisfying the way proves to be! It is even as Peter expressed it above when some of the disciples stumbled over Jesus’ teaching. The King Jesus Christ still has those “sayings,” and today he is dispensing them through the one “faithful and discreet slave” organization that he is using on earth. It is like the “good tree” that Jesus described as bringing forth “fine fruit.”—Matt. 7:17. W 12/1 19b

### Saturday, December 4

_In case you have warned someone wicked and he does not actually turn back from his wickedness . . . he himself for his error will die; but as for you, you will have delivered your own soul.—Ezek. 3:19._

Back there it was just one government, the small kingdom of Judah, that was in peril. Today it is what that ancient kingdom pictured, namely, Christendom, along with all her worldly associates. In fact, the entire system of things worldwide is on

judgment, just as in Noah’s day when it was the world that was doomed to destruction. Very plainly, the position of a watchman is a highly responsible one. In wartime, if a soldier goes to sleep at his sentry post he is put to death, because the lives of others were put at stake with also the risk of suffering defeat. So Jehovah God is concerned, not only about the lives of those needing to be warned, but also about the life of his watchman, even as can be seen from his words to Ezekiel. All of this contains a wholesome warning for us today. W 2/1 7, 10a

### Sunday, December 5

_Pay constant attention to yourself and to your teaching. Stay by these things, for by doing this you will save both yourself and those who listen to you.—1 Tim. 4:16._

Paul did not suggest that we be interested only in our own salvation. We ought to be concerned with helping others to accept Jesus as the Christ and to practice the godly devotion that “holds promise of the life now and that which is to come.” (1 Tim. 4:8) Of course, we know that we cannot get saved by merely performing certain works, as if thereby earning righteousness and salvation. (Rom. 3:28) It is thus fitting to give prayerful thought to our faith and works. In heeding Paul’s words to Timothy we can share in fulfilling a prophetic truth that Jesus uttered. We can help to prove his words true. Which words? That his disciples would do works greater than those he did. How so? Christ’s followers are doing so in that they are preaching for a longer time and reaching more people than Jesus did.—John 14:9, 10, 12. W 1/1 10, 12-15

### Monday, December 6

_Pay attention to yourselves that your hearts never become weighed down with overeating and heavy drinking and anxieties of life, and suddenly that day be instantly upon you as a snare. For it will come in upon all those dwelling upon the face of all the earth.—Luke 21:34, 35._

Jesus likened such persons to an animal that unwittingly walks into a trap. He also likened spiritually unaware persons to those in Noah’s day who “took no note until the flood came and swept them all away.” (Matt. 24:37-39) Was there any time for those people to learn of Jehovah and come under his protection after the Flood began? No, because when the Flood came it “swept them all away.” They were not already in the ark of protection with Noah and his family. Also, when God’s judgment came against Sodom and Gomorrah, were there any who escaped after fiery destruction began raining down from heaven? No, for even Lot’s wife, who hesitated, perished. With good reason Jesus said: “Remember the wife of Lot.”—Luke 17:32. W 11/15 5a

### Tuesday, December 7

_I have exhibited to you in all things that by thus laboring you must assist those who are weak.—Acts 20:35._

By working temporarily in secular employment, at a paying job, Paul was not downgrading his Kingdom ministry. He arranged to make his preaching and teaching without cost to his hearers and pupils. In that way he was really keeping his educational work clear of the charge of being a money-making rack-

et. (1 Cor. 9:13-18) In this case he was really doing what he said: “I glorify my ministry.” (Rom. 11:13) His being a worker-minister for self-support proved that he had a pure, unselfish motive in his Kingdom ministry. Most of his dedicated Christian associates were worker-ministers, some of them even being slaves to non-Christian masters. Necessary secular work does not downgrade the Kingdom ministry, for we must bear in mind that the Levites under the Mosaic Law covenant served at the temple just one week each half year, besides at the annual festivals. Thus they too were worker-ministers. W 3/15 17a

### Wednesday, December 8

_Thanks to God, for he gives us the victory through our Lord Jesus Christ!—1 Cor. 15:57._

Whether pioneers or not, all of us can perform our “sacred service” joyfully, in a true pioneer spirit. This may lead many of us to do auxiliary pioneer work from time to time. This work of the Lord Jesus, following in his footsteps, is also “the work of Jehovah.” (1 Cor. 16:10; John 5:17) How privileged we are to be co-workers with the Sovereign Lord Jehovah, the Lord Jesus Christ and the heavenly angels at “the conclusion of the system of things”! (1 Cor. 3:9; Matt. 25:31-33; 28:19, 20) For we know that our labor is “not in vain in connection with the Lord.” (1 Cor. 15:58) As we give of our time, energy and means in this Kingdom work, may our eyes be fixed on the goal. Whether we reach the New Order by resurrection or by survival through the great tribulation, may we be able to say, in the words of the faithful apostle Paul, the above. W 12/15 19, 20a

## Thursday, December 9

_On account of these detestable things Jehovah your God is driving them away from before you._
—Deut. 18:12.

Years after destroying Sodom and Gomorrah Jehovah again did 'what was right' toward his people Israel. (Gen. 18:25) How? In arranging to drive away the Canaanites from the Promised Land. Those Canaanites had become disgusting in their practices of sexual uncleanness and sacrificing of children. For example, in the worship of their false gods they sacrificed their children by throwing them alive into the fire. Also, they had female and male temple prostitutes. Therefore, Jehovah gave his people instructions to avoid all such practices. (Lev. 18:1-25) Again, it was out of love for his own people that Jehovah commanded Israel to clear out those wrongdoers. Their filthy way of life endangered God's people. It was 'something detestable to Jehovah.' Rightly, then, God's vengeance blazed against them. It is our privilege to warn people that soon at Armageddon Jehovah God will do likewise. W 1/15 14, 15

## Friday, December 10

_It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God._—Heb. 10:31.

Heartfelt prayer builds a close relationship with God. Help your child to learn of the need for prayer and how to 'pour out his heart' to Jehovah. (Ps. 62:8) Let your child hear your heartfelt prayers. Discuss what can be included in his prayers. By telling him how Jehovah has answered your prayers and by encouraging the child to look for answers to his own, the child will real-

ize that prayer has power and is not a mere ritual. 'The intimacy with Jehovah belongs to those fearful of him.' (Ps. 25:14) For your child really to develop an intimate relationship with God, he must have a wholesome fear of the awesome consequences of displeasing 'the living God.' True, the child must love Jehovah and deeply appreciate His loving-kindness and goodness, but he must also have a solemn respect for Jehovah's ability to punish or to allow a person to 'reap what he has sown.'—Gal. 6:7. W 11/1 22, 23a

## Saturday, December 11

_You do not know what your life will be tomorrow. For you are a mist appearing for a little while and then disappearing._
—Jas. 4:14.

If we make a good name with God then the day of our death will be better than the day of our birth. (Eccl. 7:1) Or, the day of death may never come at all! But perhaps you think the end should have come by now. Do you think the Lord has delayed his coming? Do you begin to slack your hand from the vital work of proclaiming God's kingdom, or even drift into misconduct? Regardless of when the end comes, this is the time your hand should do what it finds to do in making a good name. Any one of us may be gone tomorrow. We are like the flower that fades, the mist that vanishes. And we must do more than the young man who came to Jesus asking how he could gain everlasting life. Jesus said to him: 'Come be my follower.' (Matt. 19:16-22) Jesus not only practiced God's precepts in his personal life; he also proclaimed 'the good news of the kingdom' to others. W 2/15 17-19a

## Sunday, December 12

_By means of your seed all nations of the earth will certainly bless themselves due to the fact that you have listened to my voice._
—Gen. 22:18.

How do we know that Jesus Christ was, indeed, that principal 'seed' of Abraham? From Paul's words at Galatians 3:16: 'Now the promises were spoken to Abraham and to his seed. It says, not: 'And to seeds,' as in the case of many such, but as in the case of one: 'And to your seed,' who is Christ.' And by means of this same 'seed' mentioned at Genesis 3:15, not just some but all nations of the earth would bless themselves. Yes, and so would every individual in those nations, regardless of race, provided that person acknowledges the importance of that 'seed.' Indeed, as matters turn out, 'everyone exercising faith in him \[will] not be destroyed but have everlasting life.' (John 3:16) The opportunity is there for many to be subjects of that government, if they exercise faith. It is our privilege to make this good news known. W 5/15 4, 5

## Monday, December 13

_In connection with everything give thanks._—1 Thess. 5:18.

We daily have opportunities to show that we are grateful. It could be for something as simple as a courtesy. It should be more than a form of etiquette. If from our heart we truly feel grateful, others will detect that and be happier—and so will we. A fine step would be to try to broaden out in our spirit of gratitude toward persons we normally take for granted. Those most deserving of our gratitude may be persons close to us whose efforts

we tend to overlook. How often do you thank your wife for the good meals she prepares, for her effort to keep the house clean or for her patient care of the children? Youths, could you reflect more often on all that your parents do for you, and then offer them heartfelt expressions of appreciation? As you become more accustomed to expressing gratitude, your life will be richer. Others will like you more and the bonds of affection will grow. Yes, you will be happier. W 4/1 6, 7a

## Tuesday, December 14

_Moses . . . is being entrusted with all my house. . . . Why, then, did you not fear to speak against my servant, against Moses?_—Num. 12:7, 8.

The unwillingness to conduct herself as a lesser one can be seen as the reason that Eve succumbed to Satan's wiles. Satan assured her that by eating the forbidden fruit she would become like God, being able to decide for herself good and bad. (Gen. 3:5) Eve's not wanting to conduct herself as a lesser one proved fatal to her. And what about her firstborn son? Is it not true that Cain murdered his brother Abel because he simply could not conduct himself as a lesser one in relation to him? That is how galling it was for Cain to have Abel preferred. This proud mental attitude caused Cain to be banished as the first human murderer. Years later even Miriam and Aaron rebelled against conducting themselves as lesser ones in relation to their younger brother, Moses. Jehovah was so displeased that he struck Miriam with leprosy. Surely that is a lesson for us. W 6/1 5, 6

### Wednesday, December 15

\[Jehovah God] has hated a divorcing.—Mal. 2:16.

God hates a divorcing on unscriptural grounds because it is sinning against him. He has a vested interest in the institution of marriage and so any who go contrary to his will in regard to it are actually sinning against God. This can be seen from what Joseph told Potiphar's wife when she tried to seduce him to become party to adultery: 'How could I commit this great badness and actually sin against God?' (Gen. 39:9) Jehovah God also hates divorcing because he is interested in justice. Divorcing (not based on Scriptural grounds) almost invariably means dealing treacherously with one's mate. It works an injury on the innocent mate. Just as God repeatedly stated that he espoused the cause of the oppressed fatherless and widows, and would punish those who took advantage of these, so Jehovah God will judge adversely those who take advantage of legal loopholes to get rid of their mates so that they can marry others. W 7/1 8, 9a

### Thursday, December 16

I will cut off from this place . . . those who are drawing back from following Jehovah and who have not sought Jehovah and have not inquired of him.—Zeph. 1:4, 6.

Those Judeans had abandoned the pure worship of Jehovah to follow the Baalism of surrounding nations. Because of drawing away from the living God, they had developed 'a wicked heart lacking faith.' (Heb. 3:12) Is it any different in Christendom today? The nations of Christendom

dom still carry a tremendous bloodguilt, because of the millions of lives sacrificed on the altar of war during this century. The clergy on both sides supported those wars, and their bloodguilt remains. And just as the Judeans indulged in all manner of sexual immorality, so we find the peoples of Christendom today involved in premarital sex, wife-swapping, homosexuality and the like, while many of the clergy look the other way or even condone such practices. How contrary to God's Word! May we never be guilty of such practices! W 8/15 5, 6

### Friday, December 17

An overseer must be . . . holding firmly to the faithful word.—Titus 1:7, 9.

An individual who has committed serious sins needs the attention of 'the older men of the congregation.' (Jas. 5:14, 15) These men are in a good position to offer godly correction to a person who has been overreached and has fallen into grievous sin. They can provide the spiritual help that he needs. Usually a committee of three elders is designated to handle a case of grave wrongdoing. They do not act as mere judges or 'spiritual policemen.' They are shepherds of the flock, and prove to be such when dealing with an individual wrongdoer. A shepherd of literal sheep does not display a spirit of vengeance, harshness or faultfinding. Nor should the elders. They are there to help, not to condemn. Their goal is to turn the sinner back from his way, if that is possible. Yet, in dealing with grievous sin, elders must 'hold firmly to the faithful word,' exhorting and reproving as the need may be. W 9/1 22-24

### Saturday, December 18

We are therefore ambassadors substituting for Christ, as though God were making entreaty through us.—2 Cor. 5:20.

It is with such ambassadors that all the nations not made up of spiritual Israelites are to be glad. Over what? The Christian ambassadorial minister Paul answers when he writes to the congregation in Rome and says: 'Christ actually became a minister of those who are circumcised \[the natural Jews] in behalf of God's truthfulness, so as to verify the promises He made to their forefathers, and that the nations might glorify God for his mercy.' (Rom. 15:8, 9a) What was Jehovah God's mercy to the Gentile nations? Beginning with Cornelius in the year 36 C.E., Jehovah God allowed the uncircumcised Gentiles (or, people of the nations) to come in under the promises that he had made to the forefathers of the circumcised Jews. All such became ambassadors preaching the message of reconciliation. Today a great crowd of other sheep share in making this good news known far and wide. W 6/15 4, 5a

### Sunday, December 19

You are holding it fast, unless, in fact, you became believers to no purpose.—1 Cor. 15:2.

The apostle Paul climaxes his first letter to the Corinthians with a masterful discussion of the resurrection. Why did he argue so fervently in behalf of the resurrection? It was for a timely purpose. Those Corinthian Christians were surrounded by a greedy, immoral world, and some had even fallen into Satan's snares. Paul did not want his beloved brothers to be 'believers to no purpose,' for that

would mean their destruction. Rather, he wanted them to stand firm in the good news that he declared to them. Likewise today, we live in a world that does not know God. Therefore, we who 'have rested our hope on the living God' must work hard and exert ourselves in behalf of the good news. (1 Tim. 4:10) This good news now focuses on the established kingdom of Jehovah and of his Christ. It is by means of a resurrection that a great majority of mankind will attain to the earthly realm of that kingdom. W 12/15 1, 2

### Monday, December 20

These men . . . like unreasoning animals \[are] . . . wrongdoing themselves as a reward for wrongdoing.—2 Pet. 2:12, 13.

The apostle Peter says that those within the congregation who speak abusively of 'glorious ones' and get involved in immorality are thus 'rewarded.' These 'rewards' for sexual wrongdoing go much deeper than just venereal disease or an illegitimate pregnancy. They destroy love, self-respect and peace of mind, even as some young people who went too far by committing fornication sorrowfully admitted. Our heavenly Father tells us to avoid not just fornication but also 'uncleanness.' (1 Thess. 4:7) While this term covers a wide range, it refers to conduct that is morally repugnant. For instance, masturbation (sexual self-abuse) is an 'unclean' habit that many young persons have engaged in. It certainly whips up the 'sexual appetite' and can cause extreme guilt. In some cases where the person did not seriously strive to overcome it, this pattern of thinking created problems after marriage. W 11/1 7, 8

## Tuesday, December 21

_Your adversary, the Devil, walks about like a roaring lion, seeking to devour someone.—1 Pet. 5:8._

Sad to say there is an intense, and sometimes successful, effort being made by Satan to break up families, to divide and conquer. We must at all times bear in mind the above words. Will we allow the Devil to break up our family unity that is so pleasing to Jehovah? Will we succumb to the spirit of independence, one that says: 'I do not have to listen to my parents; I will do as I like'? Pursuing such a course can cost us our true happiness and bring God's disfavor. If we are to enjoy his approval—and, after all, he does hold life and death in the power of his hand—we have to support authority as he has constituted it within the family arrangement. It is to our advantage to subject ourselves to it. Only then can true happiness be found. Because of promoting family unity and harmony, we may seek to have applied to us the blessing David spoke about at Psalm 24:3-5. _W 10/15_ 18, 19

## Wednesday, December 22

_As for us who belong to the day, let us keep our senses and have on the breastplate of faith and love and as a helmet the hope of salvation.—1 Thess. 5:8._

There is no Scriptural reason to conclude that there would be enough time to turn to God after false religion is destroyed. Instead, those who want to escape alive into God's new order should 'Search for Jehovah . . . while he may be found. Call to him while he proves to be near.' (Isa. 55:6) _Now_ is when sincere persons should develop lives of

faith, courage, love and service. _Now_ is when they need to prepare themselves to withstand the pressures that will come against God's people during the rapidly approaching 'great tribulation.' God's Word likens his faithful servants to well-trained soldiers who are attentive, who have on their protective armor, and who are ready to obey any command given them by their leader. They are not like unprepared troops sleeping in the barracks in time of danger and urgency. _W 11/15_ 12, 13a

## Thursday, December 23

_He that is turning his ear away from hearing the law—even his prayer is something detestable.—Prov. 28:9._

If a husband is disfellowshipped, his wife and children will not be comfortable with him conducting the family study or leading in Bible reading or prayer. If he wants to say a prayer, such as at mealtime, he has a right to do so in his own home. But they can silently offer their own prayers to God. What if a disfellowshipped person in the home wants to be present when the family reads the Bible together or has a Bible study? The others might let him be present to listen if he will not try to teach them or share his religious views. If a minor child is disfellowshipped, the parents will still care for his physical needs and provide moral training and discipline. They could require him to sit in on the family study and they might direct attention to parts of the Bible that contain counsel he needs. They can also have him accompany them to and sit with them at Christian meetings.—Eph. 6:4. _W 9/15_ 12, 13b

## Friday, December 24

_When one ruling over mankind is righteous, ruling in the fear of God, then it is as the light of morning, when the sun shines forth, a morning without clouds.—2 Sam. 23:3, 4._

There is no question about it! The facts show that, indeed, 'light itself has flashed up for the righteous one.' (Ps. 97:11) Proverbs 4:18 is being fulfilled, in that 'the path of the righteous ones' is like a light shining ever more brightly. If at times there is a measure of adjustment, invariably an improved position results. The changes have not been in vain. With Christ now reigning, the enlightenment enjoyed by Jehovah's people is indeed as described above. Favored indeed are all those who serve loyally with the 'faithful and discreet slave' organization, Jehovah's visible agent of communication! (Matt. 24:45-47) Theirs is the wise choice, for their pathway leads on to the precious goal of everlasting life in the new order that Jehovah is creating.—Isa. 65:17, 18; 66:22. _W 12/1_ 20b

## Saturday, December 25

_When it comes true—look! it must come true—they will also have to know that a prophet himself had proved to be in the midst of them.—Ezek. 33:33._

Is the house-to-house evangelizing effective? The evidence shouts, YES! Even religionists acknowledge the effectiveness of the house-to-house evangelizing that we do. Our house-to-house evangelizing is so distinctive of us that in many lands a householder will open the door and say, 'Oh, you must be one of Jehovah's Witnesses.' Even if

nothing more was discussed from the Bible, good has been done. Attention has been drawn thus to the holy name of God, which deserves to be sanctified. And the householder has had impressed on him that Jehovah God's servants were there with His message. In fact, the person-to-person witnessing at homes is so useful and effective in contacting persons that great effort is put forth by our brothers to share in it even under difficult situations, in keeping with Matthew 10:16, 17. _W 1/1_ 12-15a

## Sunday, December 26

_That is why I will openly acknowledge you among the nations and to your name I will make melody.—Rom. 15:9._

In the early morning of the day of Pentecost of 33 C.E. the glorified Jesus channeled the holy spirit of Jehovah God upon about 120 disciples at Jerusalem. As a result of their preaching that day, about 3,000 Jews and Jewish proselytes accepted the Kingdom tidings and got baptized in water. Afterward these recipients of the holy spirit joined in the ambassadorial work as substitutes for Christ. After persecution broke out in Jerusalem some began 'openly acknowledging' Jehovah among the Samaritans and still later among uncircumcised Gentiles of Roman nationality and others. As they heard Jehovah being 'openly acknowledged' and lauded among them, people of all nationalities could call upon his name through Christ for salvation. So that others can be glad with God's people we must preach God's kingdom to them, even as Paul shows at Romans 10:13-15; 15:10. _W 6/15_ 13, 14a

### Monday, December 27

_God will judge fornicators and adulterers.—Heb. 13:4._

Time and again those who have committed adultery have divorced their mates and, having remarried, have been disfellowshipped, only to be reinstated after a year or so. Evidently the elders handling such cases based their decision on what God's Word has to say about showing mercy. True, Jehovah God is merciful and so must elders be. However, to any man and woman who have cunningly planned such actions in order to marry each other it can only be said that even though the elders reinstated them that is by no means the end of the matter. Elders can base their decision only on the apparent repentance, but they are unable to read the heart fully. Not being able to judge the motive, they may reinstate the now married couple. But let that couple never forget Paul's words at Hebrews 13:4. The final judgment in all such cases rests in the hands of Jehovah God, who knows all the circumstances. He does read hearts, even as Jeremiah 17:9, 10 states. _W 7/1 15a_

### Tuesday, December 28

_In the last days critical times hard to deal with will be here.—2 Tim. 3:1._

Is the world today really so bad that it must meet up with God's vengeance? Paul's words indicate so. These critical times are the final fruitage, the end result of the failure of people to respond to our preaching of the truth. Everywhere we see a frightening increase in crime and violence, the breakdown of the family, disrespect for authority, and loss of morality. At the

root of it all are selfishness and greed, the lack of genuine love. Paul goes on to say: 'Men will be lovers of themselves, . . . without love of goodness, . . . lovers of pleasures rather than lovers of God.' (2 Tim. 3:2-5) This prophecy is indeed being fulfilled in precise detail. As each Christmastime comes around, there is much talk in Christendom about love, about peace and goodwill among men, but at the same time nations intensify their war preparations. Truly, God's kingdom is man's only hope, and it is our privilege to make it known. _W 1/15 5-7a_

### Wednesday, December 29

_Listen! Your own watchmen have raised their voice. In unison they keep crying out joyfully; for it will be eye into eye that they will see when Jehovah gathers back Zion.—Isa. 52:8._

In these closing days of Christendom and all the rest of this doomed system of things, there is the greatest need for united action. Long ago Ezekiel and Jeremiah, though many hundreds of miles apart, united their voices in warning people of their wayward nation of the 'day of vengeance' that was about to break upon them. Now, in our century, since the end of World War I, the above words of Isaiah have applied to Jehovah's dedicated people. All of those who were regathered under the 'heavenly Zion' saw the same vision in the light of then fulfilling prophecy; together they saw Jehovah's hand in action in their favor. Today, more than 60 years later, they must keep up their united witness, only now including the urgent warning about Jehovah's 'day of vengeance.' (Isa. 61:2) Are you alert to do your part? _W 2/1 18a_

### Thursday, December 30

_For everything there is an appointed time.—Eccl. 3:1._

For parents who dealt with sex early and worked hard at keeping in close communication with their children, the results were good. For those who did not deal early with the problem, the results usually were bad. The benefits from such discussion are many. First, it can shield the child's mind from the false, dirty information he will later hear. Second, it can build respect for the parents and confidence in them and lay a common ground of communication that will carry over into puberty. And third, it can make it easier for your child to discuss the most intimate matters with you. Still, many parents wonder just how to go about discussing this somewhat embarrassing subject. The value of starting to instruct your child at a young age cannot be overemphasized. Some even urge that communication about sex be firmly established before a child is six. Otherwise it may never be achieved. Yes, that is the time to start talking about sex to a child. _W 11/1 6-8a_

### Friday, December 31

_O Jehovah of armies, if you . . . actually give to your slave girl a male offspring, I will give him to Jehovah all the days of his life.—1 Sam. 1:11._

Many parents have been willing—like Hannah of old—to forgo the pleasure of having their children nearby, but encouraged them to expand their ministry and thus help others in foreign countries. Thousands of single as well as married ministers have been blessed with the privilege of taking up missionary service in foreign lands. They have been enriched by blessings in that form of 'sacred service' and have been instrumental in spreading the good news to the far-off parts of the earth. To this day there are graduates of the first classes of Gilead School serving in foreign lands, and in many instances they form the firm foundation of the work that was started decades ago. In carrying on your 'sacred service' as a family, are you in position to encourage any of your children to consider this field of activity? _W 10/15 11a_
